The formal theory of tangentads
MARCELLO LANFRANCHI

Abstract
Tangent categories offer a categorical context for differential geometry, by categorifying geometric notions like the tangent bundle functor, vector fields, Euclidean spaces, vector bundles, connections, etc. In the last decade, the theory has been extended in new directions, providing concepts such as tangent monads, tangent fibrations, tangent restriction categories, reverse tangent categories and many more. It is natural to wonder how these new flavours interact with the geometric constructions offered by the theory. How does a tangent monad or a tangent fibration lift to the tangent category of vector fields of a tangent category? What is the correct notion of vector bundles for a tangent restriction category? In this paper, we answer these questions by adopting the formal approach of tangentads. Introduced in a previous paper, tangentads proved to be a powerful tool for capturing the different flavours of the theory and for extending constructions like the Grothendieck construction or the equivalence between split restriction categories and ℳ \mathscr{M} -categories, to the tangent-categorical context. We construct formal notions of vector fields, differential objects, differential bundles, and connections for tangentads by isolating the correct universal properties enjoyed by these constructions in ordinary tangent categories. We show that vector fields form a Lie algebra and a 2 2 -monad; we extend the result that differential bundles over the terminal object are equivalent to differential objects; we recapture the notions of covariant derivative, curvature and torsion for connections. We also show how to construct vector fields and connections using PIE limits, and we compute the formal constructions for some examples of tangentads.



Acknowledgements
.
We are grateful to Steve Lack, who suggested an equivalent description of the universal properties of the formal constructions, which greatly improved the exposition and the clarity of this paper. We also want to thank Richard Garner and JS Lemay for useful discussions, corrections, and advice. This material is based upon work supported by the AFOSR under award number FA9550-24-1-0008.

1
Introduction

Differential geometry offers a long list of important geometric constructions such as vector fields, Euclidean spaces, vector bundles, principal bundles, connections, etc. A consistent component of tangent category theory aims to extend these constructions in the more general setting of tangent categories. Many efforts in this direction have been taken (see, for instance
[
5
]
,
[
8
]
,
[
7
]
, or
[
9
]
), and more recently, some of these constructions have also been compared with related notions in algebraic geometry, adopting a tangent-categorical perspective (
[
12
]
,
[
15
]
).

Interestingly, for each of these constructions, one assembles a new tangent category from a given one, so for instance, the vector fields of a tangent category form a new tangent category, and so do differential objects, connections, and so on.

Tangent category theory not only extends many differential geometry constructions, but also comes in different categorical flavours. The first instance of this appeared in
[
8
]
in which the notion of tangent fibrations, namely, fibrations between two tangent categories whose tangent bundle functors are compatible with the Cartesian lifts, was introduced. Similarly, the paper
[
14
]
introduced the notion of tangent monads, which are monads over a tangent category with a distributive law between the monad and the tangent bundle functor, compatible with the tangent structure.

One is left to wonder how constructions like vector fields or differential bundles interact with the different flavours of tangent category theory. For instance, it is natural to ask whether or not a tangent monad on a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
lifts to the tangent category of vector fields of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, or if it does so on the tangent categories of differential bundles or of connections. Furthermore, one might like to ask the same question for other types of flavours, such as tangent fibrations or restriction tangent categories.

Our approach is rooted in the formal theory of tangentads, a notion firstly introduced in
[
19
]
and further studied in
[
18
]
as a formalization of tangent category theory where it was shown to capture a multitude of existing concepts, such as tangent monads, tangent fibrations, reverse tangent categories, strong display tangent categories, split restriction tangent categories, and infinitesimal objects.

To put it into a slogan,
A tangentad is to a tangent category as a monad internal to a
2
-category is to a monad in ordinary category theory
.

This formal approach is a powerful tool to extend equivalences and constructions between
2
-categories into the tangent context. In particular, it was employed to prove the Grothendieck construction for tangent fibrations
[
19
, Theorem 5.5]
, the construction of algebras for tangent monads
[
18
, Theorem 4.4]
, and an equivalence between split restriction tangent categories and
\mathscr{M}
-tangent categories
[
18
, Theorem 4.45]
.

One of the main benefits of the formal approach is to study a problem in a model-independent way. This saves one from defining similar constructions multiple times and from proving similar results in different contexts. For instance, it saves us from proving that a tangent monad sends the Lie bracket of two vector fields to another vector field. This is a direct consequence of the following two statements: (1) a tangent monad is an example of a tangentad in the
2
-category of monads; (2) the formal theory of vector fields for tangentads, developed in this paper, establishes a notion of Lie bracket for vector fields.

The goal of this paper is to develop a formal theory which generalizes the constructions of vector fields, differential objects, differential bundles, and connections in the general context of tangentads. We capture the correct universal property satisfied by each of these constructions in the context of tangent categories and use this characterization to define these notions in the general context.

In particular, we show that the tangent categories of vector fields, differential objects, differential bundles, and connections of a given tangent category come equipped with extra structure which makes a suitable class of
2
-functors from the
2
-category of tangent categories to itself, corepresentable. These
2
-functors are constructed by applying each of the aforementioned constructions to the Hom-tangent categories. For example, the
2
-functor which classifies vector fields on a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is the
2
-functor which sends a tangent category
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
to the tangent category
\mathsf{VF}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}|\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
of vector fields of the Hom-tangent category of tangent morphisms from
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
to
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. Therefore, a tangentad in a
2
-category admits one of the mentioned constructions when the corresponding
2
-functor from the
2
-category of tangentads to the
2
-category of tangent categories is corepresentable in a suitable sense.

Despite the abstractness of this characterization, we are capable of extending many of the constructions and results of the usual setting of tangent category theory. Here is a list of some of these results:

•

In Propositions
5.10
,
6.12
,
7.16
,
8.40
, and
8.41
, we prove that the constructions of vector fields, differential objects, differential bundles, linear and affine connections are
2
-functorial;

•

In Theorem
5.21
, we show that the tangentad
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of vector fields of a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
comes equipped with the structure of a commutative monoid;

•

In Theorem
5.20
, we show that the tangentad
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of vector fields of a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
with negatives comes equipped with the structure of a Lie algebra;

•

In Theorem
5.21
, we show that the
2
-functor
\mathsf{VF}\colon\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})\to\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
is a
2
-monad;

•

In Theorem
7.18
, we show how to obtain the construction of differential objects from the construction of differential bundles, formalizing the result that differential objects are equivalent to differential bundles over the terminal object;

•

In Section
8.5
, we extend the notions of covariant derivative, curvature, and torsion for linear and affine connections.

Furthermore, after discussing in Section
4
how to lift PIE limits to the
2
-category of tangentads internal to a given
2
-category, we employ PIE limits to construct vector fields and connections for tangentads. In particular:

•

In Theorem
5.23
, we use inserters and equifiers to construct vector fields;

•

In Theorems
8.51
,
8.52
,
8.53
,
8.55
,
8.56
, and
8.57
, we use inserters and equifiers (and two
2
-pullbacks) to construct vertical/horizontal linear/affine connections.

Finally, we study the formal constructions for tangent monads, tangent fibrations, tangent indexed categories, tangent split restriction categories and discuss how to extend them to general tangent (non-necessarily split) restriction categories via suitable pullbacks.

2
Recollections on tangentads

Tangentads were initially introduced in
[
19
]
with the name of tangent objects, to establish a Grothendieck construction in the context of tangent categories. In
[
18
]
, this notion was revisited and proved to be suitable to capture a long list of existing concepts in tangent category theory, such as tangent fibrations, tangent monads, tangent split restriction categories, reverse tangent categories, strong display tangent categories, and infinitesimal objects. In this section, we briefly review the definition of a tangentad in a
2
-category.

2.1
A brief introduction to tangent categories

Tangent categories were introduced by Rosický in
[
23
]
, and later revisited and generalized by Cockett and Cruttwell in
[
5
]
, as a categorical context for differential geometry. This section is dedicated to recalling this definition. For starters, recall the definition of an additive bundle and related morphisms.

Definition 2.1
.

In a category
\mathbb{X}
, an
additive bundle
consists of a pair of objects
B
and
E
together with three morphisms
q\colon E\to B
,
z_{q}\colon B\to E
, and
s_{q}\colon E_{2}\to E
, respectively called the
projection
, the
zero morphism
, and the
sum morphism
, where
E_{n}
denotes the
n
-fold pullback of
q
along itself. Moreover,
q
,
z_{q}
, and
s_{q}
need to satisfy the following conditions:

(a)

For every positive integer
n
, the
n
-fold pullback

denotes the canonical symmetry

.

In the following, an additive bundle
(q\colon E\to B,z_{q},s_{q})
will be denoted by
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to B
or by
\mathbf{q}^{E}_{B}
. When the symbol that denotes the additive bundle is decorated with a superscript or a subscript, the same decoration is applied to the projection, the zero morphism, and the sum morphism. For instance, the additive bundle
\mathbf{q}^{\prime}\colon E^{\prime}\to B^{\prime}
consists of the projection
q^{\prime}
, the zero morphism
z_{q}^{\prime}
, and the sum morphism
s_{q}^{\prime}
.

.

An
additive bundle morphism
from an additive bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to B
to an additive bundle
\mathbf{q}^{\prime}\colon E^{\prime}\to B^{\prime}
consists of a pair of morphisms
f\colon B\to B^{\prime}
and
g\colon E\to E^{\prime}
satisfying the following conditions:

An additive bundle morphism is denoted by
(f,g)\colon\mathbf{q}\to\mathbf{q}^{\prime}
, where, in the first position
f
indicates the base morphism and in the second one
g
indicates the top morphism.

The “correct” notion of limits in a tangent category is the notion of
\mathrm{T}
-limits.

Definition 2.3
.

Let
\mathbb{X}
be a category and
\mathrm{T}\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}
be an endofunctor of
\mathbb{X}
. A
\mathrm{T}
-limit diagram
consists of a limit diagram in
\mathbb{X}
whose universal property is preserved by all iterates
\mathrm{T}^{n}
of
\mathrm{T}
. In the following, we adopt the convention to call
\mathrm{T}
-pullbacks
,
\mathrm{T}
-equalizers
, etc., the
\mathrm{T}
-limit diagrams which, as limit diagrams, are respectively pullbacks, equalizers, etc. When the endofunctor
\mathrm{T}
represents the tangent bundle functor of a tangent structure,
\mathrm{T}
-limits are also called
tangent limits
and the convention extends to
\mathrm{T}
-pullbacks,
\mathrm{T}
-equalizers, etc.

Definition 2.4
.

In a category
\mathbb{X}
equipped with an endofunctor
\mathrm{T}
, a
\mathrm{T}
-additive bundle
consists of an additive bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to B
such that the
n
-fold pullback
E_{n}
of the projection
q
along itself is an
n
-fold
\mathrm{T}
-pullback diagram.

Definition 2.5
.

A
tangent structure
on a category
\mathbb{X}
consists of the following data:

\mathrm{T}

\mathbb{X}

Vertical lift
]

A natural transformation

\displaystyle(z_{M},l_{M})\colon\mathbf{p}_{M}\to\mathrm{T}\mathbf{p}_{M}

[
Canonical flip
]
:

A natural transformation
c_{M}\colon\mathrm{T}^{2}M\to\mathrm{T}^{2}M
such that:

\displaystyle(\mathsf{id}_{M},c_{M})\colon\mathrm{T}\mathbf{p}_{M}\to\mathbf{p}_{\mathrm{T}M}

is an additive bundle morphism;

Moreover, the following coherence conditions must hold:

(a)

The vertical lift is coassocative, and compatible with the canonical flip:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{2}M}}

A
tangent category
consists of a category
\mathbb{X}
equipped with a tangent structure
\mathbb{T}\>\colon\!=(\mathrm{T},p,z,s,l,c)
. A tangent structure
has negatives
when it is equipped with:

​
[
negation
]
:

A natural transformation
n_{M}\colon\mathrm{T}M\to\mathrm{T}M
, natural in
M
, satisfying the following condition:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{2}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{id}_{M}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}M}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle n_{M},\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{T}M}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{M}}

In the following, we denote the tangent bundle functor of a tangent structure with the same symbol adopted to denote a tangent structure, e.g.,
\mathbb{T}
, in the font
\mathrm{T}
. The projection, the zero morphism, the sum morphism, the vertical lift, and the canonical flip are respectively denoted by the letters
p
,
z
,
s
,
l
, and
c
, and when the tangent structure has negatives, the negation is denoted by
n
. When the symbol denoting the tangent structure is decorated with a subscript or with a superscript, the tangent bundle functor and each of the structural natural transformations are decorated in the same way, e.g., for a tangent structure
\mathbb{T}^{\prime}_{\circ}
, the tangent bundle functor is denoted by
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}_{\circ}
, the projection by
p^{\prime}_{\circ}
and so on.

Examples of tangent categories can be found in
[
5
]
,
[
8
]
, and
[
16
]
. Here is a brief list of selected examples from these resources:

•

Any category with tangent bundle functor and structural natural transformations as identities;

•

The category of (finitely-dimensional) smooth manifolds;

•

The subcategory of microlinear objects in a model of synthetic differential geometry;

•

Any Cartesian differential Category;

•

The category of (affine) schemes over a commutative and unital ring;

•

The category of algebras of a symmetric operad and its opposite category.

2.2
The definition of a tangentad

A tangentad consists of an object in a
2
-category together with a structure which formalizes a tangent structure on a category. In particular, tangentads in the
2
-category
\mathsf{CAT}
of categories are precisely tangent categories. One way to define such a formalization is to exploit Leung’s approach to tangent categories. In
[
20
]
, Leung showed that tangent structures
\mathbb{T}
on a category
\mathbb{X}
are in correspondence with strong monoidal functors
\mathfrak{L}[\mathbb{T}]
from a monoidal category
\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}
to the monoidal category
\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X})
of endofunctors of
\mathbb{X}
, which satisfy an extra technical condition. We leave the reader to consult
[
20
]
for a complete explanation of this result.

For the sake of completeness, here we give a rough sketch of this correspondence. Firstly, let us briefly describe the monoidal category
\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}
. Such a monoidal category is generated by the following family of rigs, a.k.a. semirings

\displaystyle W_{n}\>\colon\!=\mathbb{N}[x_{1},\dots,x_{n}]/(x_{i}x_{j};i,j=1,\dots,n)

where the monoidal product is the tensor product of rigs, namely, the pushout in the category of rigs. Moreover, the morphisms of
\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}
are generated by the following rig morphisms

\displaystyle p\colon W\to\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle z\colon\mathbb{N}\to W

\displaystyle s\colon W_{2}\to W

\displaystyle l\colon W\to W\otimes W

\displaystyle c\colon W\otimes W\to W\otimes W

where
W=W_{1}
,
p
is the augmentation map, namely,
p(a+b\varepsilon)\>\colon\!=a
, for each
a+b\varepsilon\in W
, where
\varepsilon^{2}=0
;
z
is the inclusion, namely,
z(a)\>\colon\!=a
;
s
is the sum, namely,
s(\varepsilon_{1})=\varepsilon=s(\varepsilon_{2})
;
l
sends
\varepsilon
to
\varepsilon\otimes\varepsilon
; finally,
c
is the canonical symmetry of the tensor product.

With this description of the monoidal category
\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}
, a tangent structure
\mathbb{T}
on a category
\mathbb{X}
is associated to a strong monoidal functor

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}[\mathbb{T}]\colon\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}\to\mathsf{END}_{\mathbf{K}}(\mathbb{X})

which sends
W
to the tangent bundle functor
\mathrm{T}
, each
W_{n}
to the
n
-fold pullback of the projection along itself, namely,
\mathfrak{L}[\mathbb{T}](W_{n})\>\colon\!=\mathrm{T}_{n}
;
p
to the projection
p\colon\mathrm{T}\to\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}
;
z
to the zero morphism
z\colon\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}\to\mathrm{T}
;
s
to the sum morphism
s\colon\mathrm{T}_{2}\to\mathrm{T}
;
l
to the vertical lift
l\colon\mathrm{T}\to\mathrm{T}^{2}
; and
c
to the canonical flip
c\colon\mathrm{T}^{2}\to\mathrm{T}^{2}
.

Adopting Leung’s perspective, the definition of a tangentad becomes straightforward.

Definition 2.6
.

A
tangentad
in a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
consists of an object
\mathbb{X}
of
{\mathbf{K}}
together with a strong monoidal functor

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}[\mathbb{T}]\colon\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}\to\mathsf{END}_{\mathbf{K}}(\mathbb{X})

from the monoidal category
\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}
to the monoidal category of
1
-endomorphisms of
\mathbb{X}
in
{\mathbf{K}}
, which preserves the fundamental tangent limits pointwisely, namely, for every morphism
F\colon\mathbb{X}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{X}
, the functor

\displaystyle\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{L}[\mathbb{T}]}\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X})={\mathbf{K}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{X})\xrightarrow{{\mathbf{K}}(F,\mathbb{X})}{\mathbf{K}}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{X})

preserves the following pullback diagrams:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{W^{\otimes n}\otimes W_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{W^{\otimes n}\otimes W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{W^{\otimes n}\otimes W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{W^{\otimes n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{\otimes n}\otimes\pi_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{\otimes n}\otimes\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{\otimes n}\otimes p}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{\otimes n}\otimes p}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{W_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{W\otimes W_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{W\otimes W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle l\circ\pi_{1},(z\otimes W)\circ\pi_{2}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\otimes s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\otimes p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

Remark 2.7
.

In
[
20
, Definition 14.2]
, Leung already suggested a generalization of a tangent category, by introducing the notion of a
tangent structure internal
to a monoidal category
\mathcal{E}
as a strong monoidal functor
\mathfrak{L}[\mathbb{T}]\colon\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}\to\mathcal{E}
which preserves the fundamental tangent limits in a pointwise way. In this sense, a tangentad consists of an object
\mathbb{X}
in a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
together with a tangent structure internal to the monoidal category
\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X})
.

Another approach to generalize tangent structures was employed in
[
1
]
in which
\infty
-tangent category is defined as a strong monoidal functor from the
\infty
-category of Weil algebras
\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}^{\infty}
to the monoidal category
\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X})
of endomorphisms of an
\infty
-category
\mathbb{X}
.

As pointed out by Michael Ching in an informal discussion with the author,
\infty
-tangent categories generalize to strong monoidal functors from
\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}^{\infty}
to the monoidal category
\mathsf{END}_{\mathbf{K}}(\mathbb{X})
, where
{\mathbf{K}}
is a
(\infty,2)
-category, which preserve suitable limits. Such functors split along the homotopy category of
\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}^{\infty}
, which coincides with
\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}
. Thus, every such functor gives rise to a tangentad.

Before proceeding further, let us unpack Definition
2.6
. A tangentad in a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
consists of:

​
[
Base object
]
:

An object
\mathbb{X}
;

​
[
Tangent bundle
1
-morphism
]
:

A
1
-endomorphism
\mathrm{T}\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}
;

​
[
Projection
]
:

A
2
-morphism
p\colon\mathrm{T}\Rightarrow\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}
, which admits all pointwise
n
-fold pullbacks

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

which are preserved by all iterates
\mathrm{T}^{n}
of
\mathrm{T}
;

​
[
Zero morphism
]
:

A
2
-morphism
z\colon\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}\Rightarrow\mathrm{T}
;

​
[
Sum morphism
]
:

A
2
-morphism
s\colon\mathrm{T}_{2}\Rightarrow\mathrm{T}
;

such that,
\mathbf{p}\>\colon\!=(p,z,s)
is a
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
-additive bundle of
\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X})
, where
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
sends an endomorphism
F\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}
to
\mathrm{T}\circ F
;

​
[
Vertical lift
]
:

A
2
-morphism
l\colon\mathrm{T}\Rightarrow\mathrm{T}^{2}
such that:

\displaystyle(z,l)\colon\mathbf{p}\to\mathrm{T}\mathbf{p}

is an additive bundle morphism;

​
[
Canonical flip
]
:

A
2
-morphism
c\colon\mathrm{T}^{2}\Rightarrow\mathrm{T}^{2}
such that:

\displaystyle(\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}},c)\colon\mathrm{T}\mathbf{p}\to\mathbf{p}_{\mathrm{T}}

is an additive bundle morphism;

Moreover, the following coherence conditions must hold:

(a)

The vertical lift is coassocative, and compatible with the canonical flip:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}l}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{\mathrm{T}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{\mathrm{T}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{\mathrm{T}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}l}

(b)

The canonical flip is a symmetric braiding:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{\mathrm{T}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{\mathrm{T}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{\mathrm{T}}}

(c)

The tangent bundle is locally linear, namely, the following is a pointwise pullback diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}\circ\mathrm{T}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{\mathrm{T}}\times l}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z}

Furthermore, a tangentad admits negatives when it is equipped with:

​
[
Negation
]
:

A
2
-morphism
n\colon\mathrm{T}\Rightarrow\mathrm{T}
such that:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle n,\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{T}}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z}

2.3
The
2
-categories of tangentads

The tangentads of a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
form at least four different
2
-categories. In this section, we recall the notion of
1
and
2
-morphisms between tangentads. Let us start with
1
-morphisms.

Definition 2.8
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
be two tangentads of
{\mathbf{K}}
. A
lax tangent
1
-morphism
from
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
to
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
consists of a
1
-morphism
F\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
together with a
2
-morphism
\alpha\colon F\circ\mathrm{T}\to\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\circ F
, called a
lax distributive law
satisfying the following conditions:

(a)

Additivity:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\circ\mathrm{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\circ F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fp}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}F}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\circ\mathrm{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\circ F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fz}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z^{\prime}F}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\circ\mathrm{T}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}_{2}\circ F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\circ\mathrm{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\circ F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\alpha\circ F\pi_{1},\alpha\circ F\pi_{2}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fs}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\prime}F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

(b)

Compatibility with the vertical lift:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\circ\mathrm{T}^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\circ F\circ\mathrm{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime 2}\circ F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\circ\mathrm{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\circ F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha\mathrm{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fl}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l^{\prime}F}

(c)

Compatibility with the canonical flip:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\circ\mathrm{T}^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\circ F\circ\mathrm{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime 2}\circ F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\circ\mathrm{T}^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\circ F\circ\mathrm{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime 2}\circ F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha\mathrm{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fc}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha\mathrm{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{\prime}F}

A
colax tangent
1
-morphism
from
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
to
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
consists of a
1
-morphism
G\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
together with a
2
-morphism
\beta\colon\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\circ G\to G\circ\mathrm{T}
, called a
colax distributive law
, which satisfies the dual conditions of a lax distributive law, namely, additivity and the compatibility with the vertical lift and the canonical flip, in which the direction of the distributive law is now reversed.

A
strong tangent
1
-morphism
is a lax tangent
1
-morphism whose distributive law is invertible. A
strict tangent
1
-morphism
is a strong tangent
1
-morphism whose distributive law is the identity.

Finally, a tangent morphism
(F,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})

preserves the fundamental tangent limits
when the functor
{\mathbf{K}}(\mathbb{X},F)\colon\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X})\to{\mathbf{K}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{X}^{\prime})
preserves the fundamental tangent limits of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
in a pointwise way.
A lax tangent
1
-morphism is denoted by
(F,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
while for colax tangent
1
-morphisms we adopt the notation
(F,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\nrightarrow(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
. Finally, a strict tangent
1
-morphism is denoted by its underlying
1
-morphism.

We can now recall the notion of
2
-morphisms of tangentads.

Definition 2.9
.

Given two lax tangent morphisms
(F,\alpha),(G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
of tangentads in
{\mathbf{K}}
, a
tangent
2
-morphism
from
(F,\alpha)
to
(G,\beta)
consists of a
2
-morphism
\varphi\colon F\Rightarrow G
satisfying the following compatibility between the distributive laws:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\circ\mathrm{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\circ F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G\circ\mathrm{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\circ G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi\mathrm{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}

A tangent
2
-morphism between two colax tangent morphisms
(F,\alpha),(G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\nrightarrow(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
consists of a
2
-morphism
\varphi\colon F\Rightarrow G
which satisfies the same compatibility as a tangent
2
-morphism between lax tangent morphisms, where the distributive laws are reversed.

Finally, given two lax tangent morphisms
(F_{\circ},\alpha_{\circ})\colon(\mathbb{X}_{\circ},\mathbb{T}_{\circ})\to(\mathbb{X}_{\circ}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}_{\circ}^{\prime})
and two colax tangent morphisms
(G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X}_{\circ},\mathbb{T}_{\circ})\nrightarrow(\mathbb{X}_{\bullet},\mathbb{T}_{\bullet})
and
(G^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})\colon(\mathbb{X}_{\circ}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}_{\circ}^{\prime})\nrightarrow(\mathbb{X}_{\bullet}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}_{\bullet}^{\prime})
, a
double tangent morphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}_{\circ},\mathbb{T}_{\circ})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}_{\bullet},\mathbb{T}_{\bullet})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime}_{\circ},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}_{\circ})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}_{\bullet}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}_{\bullet}^{\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G,\beta)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\shortmid}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F_{\circ},\alpha_{\circ})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F_{\bullet},\alpha_{\bullet})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G,^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\shortmid}

consists of a
2
-morphism
\varphi\colon F_{\bullet}\circ G\Rightarrow G^{\prime}\circ F_{\circ}
satisfying the following compatibility with the distributive laws:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{\bullet}\circ\mathrm{T}_{\bullet}\circ G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{\bullet}\circ G\circ\mathrm{T}_{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G^{\prime}\circ F_{\circ}\circ\mathrm{T}_{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}_{\bullet}\circ F_{\bullet}\circ G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{\bullet}^{\prime}\circ G^{\prime}\circ F_{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G^{\prime}\circ\mathrm{T}^{\prime}_{\circ}\circ F_{\circ}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\bullet}\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{\bullet}G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi\mathrm{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\prime}\alpha_{\circ}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime}_{\bullet}\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta^{\prime}F_{\circ}}

Tangentads with their tangent
1
and
2
-morphisms can be organized into four distinct
2
-categories:

•

Tangentads of
{\mathbf{K}}
, lax tangent morphisms, and corresponding
2
-morphisms form the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
;

•

Tangentads of
{\mathbf{K}}
, colax tangent morphisms, and corresponding
2
-morphisms form the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNG}_{\mathsf{co}}({\mathbf{K}})
;

•

Tangentads of
{\mathbf{K}}
, strong tangent morphisms, and corresponding
2
-morphisms form the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNG}_{\cong}({\mathbf{K}})
;

•

Tangent categories, strict tangent morphisms, and corresponding
2
-morphisms form the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNG}_{=}({\mathbf{K}})
.

Furthermore:

•

Tangentads of
{\mathbf{K}}
, lax tangent
1
-morphisms for vertical morphisms, colax tangent
1
-morphisms for horizontal morphisms, and double tangent morphisms for double morphisms form a double category denoted by
\mathbb{T}\mathsf{NG}({\mathbf{K}})
.

2.4
Cartesian tangentads

In
[
18
, Section 3.2]
, Cartesian tangentads were introduced. In this section, we recall this definition. In the following, we assume that
{\mathbf{K}}
is a Cartesian
2
-category, namely, a
2
-category with finite
2
-products and we denote by
\mathds{1}
the
2
-terminal object of
{\mathbf{K}}
and by
\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
the Cartesian
2
-product of two objects
\mathbb{X}
and
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
of
{\mathbf{K}}
.

Definition 2.10
.

In a Cartesian
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
, a
Cartesian object
consists of an object
\mathbb{X}
of
{\mathbf{K}}
together with two
1
-morphisms

\displaystyle\ast\colon\mathds{1}\to\mathbb{X}

\displaystyle\times\colon\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}

which are right adjoints to the terminal
1
-morphism
!\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathds{1}
and to the diagonal map
\Delta\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}
, respectively.

Cartesian objects in the
2
-category of categories are precisely Cartesian categories, namely, categories with finite products. Cartesian objects in a
2
-category form a new
2
-category denoted by
\mathsf{CART}({\mathbf{K}})
whose
1
-morphisms are
1
-morphisms of
{\mathbf{K}}
which preserve the Cartesian structure up to
2
-isomorphisms, and whose
2
-morphisms are
2
-morphisms of
{\mathbf{K}}
which commute with the coherence
2
-isomorphisms of the
1
-morphisms. Furthermore, if
{\mathbf{K}}
is a Cartesian
2
-category so does the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
of tangentads of
{\mathbf{K}}
. We suggest the reader consult
[
18
]
for details.

Definition 2.11
.

A
Cartesian tangentad
is equivalently a tangentad in the
2
-category
\mathsf{CART}({\mathbf{K}})
of Cartesian objects of
{\mathbf{K}}
or a Cartesian object in the Cartesian
2
-category
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
of tangentads of
{\mathbf{K}}
.

We denote by
\mathsf{CTNG}({\mathbf{K}})
the
2
-category
\mathsf{CART}(\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}}))\cong\mathsf{TNG}(\mathsf{CART}({\mathbf{K}}))
of Cartesian tangentads. Concretely, a Cartesian tangentad consists of a Cartesian object
\mathbb{X}
of
{\mathbf{K}}
equipped with a tangent structure
\mathbb{T}
in
{\mathbf{K}}
whose tangent bundle
1
-morphism
\mathrm{T}\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}
such that the
2
-morphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathds{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ast}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ast}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{\ast}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\times}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}\times\mathrm{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{\times}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\times}

induced by the adjunctions
\ast\dashv!
and
\times\dashv\Delta
are invertible and compatible with the structural
2
-morphisms of the tangent structure.

2.5
Examples of tangentads

In
[
18
]
, we explored a long list of tangentads. We now briefly recall some of these examples.

Example 2.12
.

Tangent categories are the archetypal example of tangentads. In particular, they are tangentads in the
2
-category
\mathsf{CAT}
of categories.

Example 2.13
.

Tangent monads, introduced in
[
14
, Definition 19]
, are monads in the
2
-category of tangent categories. Concretely, a tangent monad on a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
consists of a monad
S\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}
equipped with a natural transformation
\alpha_{M}\colon S\mathrm{T}M\to\mathrm{T}SM
, for
M\in\mathbb{X}
, such that
(S,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is a lax tangent morphism. Furthermore, the unit
\eta_{M}\colon M\to SM
and the multiplication
\mu_{M}\colon S^{2}M\to SM
of the monad
S
are tangent natural transformations, namely, they are compatible with
\alpha
.

More generally, a tangent monad internal to a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
is monad in the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
of tangentads of
{\mathbf{K}}
.

[
18
, Proposition 4.2]
shows that, for a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
, the
2
-category
\mathsf{MND}(\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}}))
of monads in the
2
-category of tangentads of
{\mathbf{K}}
is isomorphic to the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNG}(\mathsf{MND}({\mathbf{K}}))
of tangentads in the
2
-category of monads of
{\mathbf{K}}
. In particular, tangent monads are tangentads in the
2
-category
\mathsf{MND}\>\colon\!=\mathsf{MND}(\mathsf{CAT})
of monads.

Example 2.14
.

Tangent fibrations, introduced in
[
8
, Definition 5.2]
, are (cloven) fibrations
\Pi\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
between two tangent categories which preserve the tangent structures strictly, namely,
\Pi
is a strict tangent morphism, and whose tangent bundle functors define an endomorphism
(\mathrm{T},\mathrm{T}^{\prime})\colon\Pi\to\Pi
of fibrations, namely, the tangent bundle functor
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}
sends each Cartesian lift
\varphi_{f}\colon f^{\ast}E\to E
of each morphism
f\colon A\to B
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and each
E\in\Pi^{-1}(B)
in the fibre over
B
, to a Cartesian lift
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(\varphi_{f})\colon\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(f^{\ast}E)\to\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E
of
\mathrm{T}f
onto
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}E
.

[
19
, Proposition 5.1]
proves that tangent fibrations are tangentads in the
2
-category
\mathsf{FIB}
. Concretely, the objects of
\mathsf{FIB}
are triples
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{X}^{\prime};\Pi)
formed by two categories
\mathbb{X}
and
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
and a fibration
\Pi\colon\mathbb{X}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{X}
.
1
-morphisms of
\mathsf{FIB}

(F,F^{\prime})\colon(\mathbb{X}_{\circ},\mathbb{X}_{\circ}^{\prime};\Pi_{\circ})\to(\mathbb{X}_{\bullet},\mathbb{X}^{\prime}_{\bullet};\Pi_{\bullet})
are pairs of functors
F\colon\mathbb{X}_{\circ}\to\mathbb{X}_{\bullet}
and
F^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{X}_{\circ}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{X}_{\bullet}^{\prime}
which strictly commute with the fibrations, namely,
\Pi_{\bullet}\circ F^{\prime}=F\circ\Pi_{\circ}
, and preserve the Cartesian lifts, namely,
F^{\prime}
sends each Cartesian lift
\varphi_{f}\colon f^{\ast}E\to E
of a morphism
f\colon A\to B
of
\mathbb{X}_{\circ}
to a Cartesian lift of
Ff
. Finally,
2
-morphisms of
\mathsf{FIB}

\displaystyle(\varphi,\varphi^{\prime})\colon(F,F^{\prime})\Rightarrow(G,G^{\prime})\colon(\mathbb{X}_{\circ},\mathbb{X}_{\circ}^{\prime};\Pi_{\circ})\to(\mathbb{X}_{\bullet},\mathbb{X}_{\bullet}^{\prime};\Pi_{\bullet})

are pairs of natural transformations
\varphi\colon F\Rightarrow G
and
\varphi^{\prime}\colon F^{\prime}\Rightarrow G^{\prime}
which commute with the fibrations, namely,
\Pi_{\bullet}\varphi^{\prime}=\varphi_{\Pi_{\circ}}
.

Example 2.15
.

Tangent indexed categories were introduced in
[
19
, Definition 5.3]
as tangentads in the
2
-category
\mathsf{INDX}
of indexed categories. Concretely, the objects of
\mathsf{INDX}
are pairs
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{I})
formed by a category
\mathbb{X}
and an indexed category, a.k.a., a pseudofunctor
\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{op}}\to\mathsf{CAT}
. The
1
-morphisms
(F,F^{\prime},\xi)\colon(\mathbb{X}_{\circ},\mathbb{I}_{\circ})\to(\mathbb{X}_{\bullet},\mathbb{I}_{\bullet})
of
\mathsf{INDX}
consist of a functor
F\colon\mathbb{X}_{\circ}\to\mathbb{X}_{\bullet}
, a collection
F^{\prime}
of functors
F_{A}^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{I}_{\circ}(A)\to\mathbb{I}_{\bullet}(FA)
, indexed by the objects
A
of
\mathbb{X}_{\circ}
, and a collection of natural isomorphisms
\xi^{f}\colon F_{A}^{\prime}\circ\mathbb{I}_{\circ}(f)\Rightarrow\mathbb{I}_{\bullet}(Ff)\circ F_{B}^{\prime}
, called distributors, indexed by the morphisms
f\colon A\to B
of
\mathbb{X}_{\circ}
. Finally, the
2
-morphisms of
\mathsf{INDX}

\displaystyle(\varphi,\varphi^{\prime})\colon(F,F^{\prime},\xi)\to(G,G^{\prime},\kappa)\colon(\mathbb{X}_{\circ},\mathbb{I}_{\circ})\to(\mathbb{X}_{\bullet},\mathbb{I}_{\bullet})

consist of a natural transformation
\varphi\colon F\Rightarrow G
together with a collection
\varphi^{\prime}
of natural transformations
\varphi_{A}^{\prime}\colon F_{A}^{\prime}\Rightarrow G_{A}^{\prime}\circ\mathbb{I}_{\bullet}(\varphi_{A})
which are compatible with the distributors.

A tangentad in
\mathsf{INDX}
, a.k.a., a tangent indexed category, consists of the following data:

​
[
Base tangent category
]
:

A tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
;

​
[
Indexed category
]
:

An indexed category
\mathbb{I}\colon\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{op}}\to\mathsf{CAT}
which sends each object
M\in\mathbb{X}
to a category
\mathbb{X}^{M}
and each morphism
f\colon M\to N
to a functor
f^{\ast}\mathbb{X}^{N}\to\mathbb{X}^{N}
;

​
[
Indexed tangent functor
]
:

A list of functors
\mathrm{T}^{\prime M}\colon\mathbb{X}^{M}\to\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{T}M}
, indexed by the objects of
M
, together with a list of natural isomorphisms
\xi^{f}_{E}\colon(\mathrm{T}f)^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime N}E\to\mathrm{T}^{\prime M}f^{\ast}E
, indexed by the morphisms
f\colon M\to N
of
\mathbb{X}
;

​
[
Indexed projection
]
:

A list of natural transformations
p^{\prime M}_{E}\colon\mathrm{T}^{\prime M}E\to p^{\ast}E
;

​
[
Indexed zero morphism
]
:

A list of natural transformations
z^{\prime M}_{E}\colon E\to z^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime M}E
;

​
[
Indexed sum morphism
]
:

A list of natural transformations
s^{\prime M}_{E}\colon\mathrm{T}^{\prime M}_{2}E\to s^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime M}E
;

​
[
Indexed vertical lift
]
:

A list of natural transformations
l^{\prime M}_{E}\colon\mathrm{T}^{\prime M}E\to l^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime\mathrm{T}M}\mathrm{T}^{\prime M}E
;

​
[
Indexed canonical flip
]
:

A list of natural transformations
c^{\prime M}_{E}\colon\mathrm{T}^{\prime\mathrm{T}M}\mathrm{T}^{\prime M}E\to c^{\ast}\mathrm{T}^{\prime\mathrm{T}M}\mathrm{T}^{\prime M}E
.

The indexed projection, zero morphism, sum morphism, vertical lift, and canonical lift must also be compatible with the distributors of
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}
and need to satisfy the axioms of a tangent structure.

Finally, we mention that
[
19
, Theorem 5.5]
shows a
2
-equivalence between the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNGFIB}
of tangent fibrations and the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNGINDX}
of tangent indexed categories.

Example 2.16
.

Tangent restriction categories, introduced in
[
5
, Definition 6.14]
, are restriction categories equipped with an endofunctor
\mathrm{T}
which preserves the restriction idempotents, together with structural total natural transformations
p_{M}\colon\mathrm{T}M\to M
,
z_{M}\colon M\to\mathrm{T}M
,
s_{M}\colon\mathrm{T}_{2}M\to\mathrm{T}M
,
l_{M}\colon\mathrm{T}M\to\mathrm{T}^{2}M
, and
c_{M}\colon\mathrm{T}^{2}M\to\mathrm{T}^{2}M
similar to the structural natural transformations of a tangent structure and that satisfy similar axioms, but in which the
n
-fold pullback of
p
along itself and the pullback diagram of the universality of the vertical lift are replaced with restriction pullbacks. We suggest the reader consult
[
10
]
for a definition of restriction category and
[
11
]
for a definition of restriction limits. As noticed in
[
18
, Section 4.6]
, tangent restriction categories are not an example of tangentads. Nevertheless, as proved in
[
18
, Proposition 4.35]
, tangent split restriction categories, namely, tangent restriction categories whose restriction idempotents split, are precisely the tangentads in the
2
-category
\mathsf{SRSTR}
of split restriction categories, restriction functors, and total natural transformations.

As proved by
[
18
, Lemma 4.36]
, every tangent restriction category embeds into a tangent split restriction category. Thus, in particular, every tangent restriction category embeds into a tangentad.

3
The Hom-tangent categories of tangentads

[
8
, Example 2.2(vii)]
shows that the category
\mathsf{CAT}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{X})
of functors
F\colon\mathbb{X}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{X}
comes equipped with a tangent structure defined pointwise, provided that
\mathbb{X}
comes with a tangent structure
\mathbb{T}
. In particular, the tangent bundle functor of a functor
F
is the functor
\mathrm{T}\circ F
. In this section, we generalize this construction by proving that the Hom-categories in the
2
-category of tangentads of a given
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
come with a tangent structure defined pointwise. This plays a crucial role in our story.

3.1
The pointwise tangent structure on the Hom categories

For starters, notice that each tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is a tangentad in the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
of tangentads of
{\mathbf{K}}
. In particular, the tangent bundle
1
-morphism is the strong tangent morphism
(\mathrm{T},c)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and the structural
2
-morphisms are the same of
\mathbb{T}
.

Proposition 3.1
.

For two tangentads
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
(with negatives), the category of lax tangent morphisms
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime};\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
from
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
to
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
comes with a tangent structure (with negatives) defined pointwise. Concretely, the tangent bundle functor sends a lax tangent morphism
(F,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
to the lax tangent morphism:

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(F,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\xrightarrow{(F,\alpha)}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\xrightarrow{(\mathrm{T},c)}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

Moreover,
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
sends a morphism
\varphi\colon(F,\alpha)\to(G,\beta)
of lax tangent morphisms to the morphism:

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\varphi\>\colon\!=\mathrm{T}\varphi\colon\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(F,\alpha)\to\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(G,\beta)

Furthermore, the Hom-category
2
-functor
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})(-,?)
lifts along the forgetful functor
\mathsf{U}\colon\mathsf{TNGCAT}\to\mathsf{CAT}
to a
2
-functor

\displaystyle\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})^{\mathsf{op}}\times\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}

strict and contravariant in the first argument and lax and covariant in the second.

Proof.

To define a tangent structure is to define a Leung functor (cf.
[
18
, Definition 2.11]
), namely, a strong monoidal functor

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu]\colon\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}\to\mathsf{END}\left(\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime};\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]\right)

which preserves the pointwise fundamental tangent limits. Let us define
\mathfrak{L}[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu]
as follows.
\mathfrak{L}[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu]
sends the Weil algebra
W_{n}
to the endofunctor
(\mathrm{T}_{n},c_{n})\circ(-)
of
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime};\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
which postcomposes with
(\mathrm{T}_{n},c_{n})
, namely the
n
-fold pullback of the projection along itself. Moreover, the structural morphisms
p
,
z
,
s
,
l
, and
c
(and
n
when negatives are considered) are sent to the tangent natural transformations
p\circ(-)
,
z\circ(-)
,
s\circ(-)
,
l\circ(-)
, and
c\circ(-)
(and
n\circ(-)
, when negatives are considered), respectively. One needs to use the axioms of a tangent structure to check that these are, in fact, tangent natural transformations.

This defines an assignment on the generators of
\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}
, thus it automatically extends to a strong monoidal functor
\mathfrak{L}[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathbb{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu]
. Let us prove that such a strong functor preserves the pointwise fundamental tangent limits. The preservation of the pointwise
n
-fold pullback of the projection is a direct consequence of
W_{n}
being sent to
(\mathrm{T}_{n},c_{n})
, which corresponds to the
n
-fold pointwise pullback of the projection along itself. Therefore, the only remaining axiom to be checked is the universal property of the vertical lift. Consider a lax tangent morphism
(F,\alpha)
and the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathrm{T}_{2},c_{2})\circ(F,\alpha)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathrm{T},c)\circ(\mathrm{T}_{2},c_{2})\circ(F,\alpha)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathrm{T},c)^{2}\circ(F,\alpha)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(F,\alpha)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathrm{T},c)\circ(F,\alpha)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l\times z_{(\mathrm{T},c)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p\circ\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{T},c)s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{T},c)p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z}

By
[
18
, Proposition 2.24]
, the forgetful functor
\mathsf{U}\colon\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})\to{\mathbf{K}}
reflects tangent limits. By applying
\mathsf{U}
to the above diagram we obtain the diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{2}\circ F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}\circ\mathrm{T}_{2}\circ F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{2}\circ F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}\circ F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l\times z_{\mathrm{T}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p\circ\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z}

which is a pointwise
\mathrm{T}
-limit by the universal property of the vertical lift of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. Therefore, by reflectivity, the original diagram is a pointwise
(\mathrm{T},c)
-limit, proving that the vertical lift is universal in
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime};\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
.

To prove that the Hom-category
2
-functor lifts along the forgetful functor
\mathsf{TNGCAT}\to\mathsf{CAT}
, consider a lax tangent morphism
(F,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
. It is not hard to see that the functor

\displaystyle[F,\alpha\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]\to[\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

which precomposes lax tangent morphisms with
(F,\alpha)
extends to strict tangent morphism. Similarly, given a lax tangent morphism
(G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
, the lax ditributive law
\beta\colon G\circ\mathrm{T}\Rightarrow\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}\circ G
lifts a lax distributive law
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>G,\beta]\circ\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\Rightarrow\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\circ[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>G,\beta]
. Finally, tangent
2
-morphisms are sent to tangent
2
-morphisms in the obvious way.
∎

In the following, when the ambient
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
is clear from the context, we denote the Hom-tangent categories of Proposition
3.1
by:

\displaystyle[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]\>\colon\!=\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime};\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

The converse of Proposition
3.1
is also true, namely, every corepresentable
2
-functor
\Gamma\colon{\mathbf{K}}\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}
defines a tangentad in
{\mathbf{K}}
.

Corollary 3.2
.

A tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
in a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
defines a corepresentable
2
-functor
[-\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]\colon{\mathbf{K}}^{\mathsf{op}}\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}
. Moreover, every corepresentable
2
-functor
{\mathbf{K}}[-\>|\>\mathbb{X}]\colon{\mathbf{K}}^{\mathsf{op}}\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}
defines a tangentad in
{\mathbf{K}}
.

Proof.

Given a corepresentable
2
-functor
{\mathbf{K}}[-\>|\>\mathbb{X}]\colon{\mathbf{K}}^{\mathsf{op}}\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}
, using the enriched version of the Yoneda lemma, one can show that
\mathbb{X}
comes equipped with a tangent structure.
∎

3.2
The Cartesian structure of the Hom-tangent categories

In this section, we show that the Hom-tangent categories inherit a Cartesian structure provided the target tangentad is Cartesian. Consider an ambient
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
which admits finite
2
-products, whose terminal object is denoted by
\mathds{1}
and Cartesian
2
-product by
\times
. A Cartesian object of
{\mathbf{K}}
is an object
\mathbb{X}
of
{\mathbf{K}}
together with two
1
-morphisms

\displaystyle\ast\colon\mathds{1}\to\mathbb{X}

\displaystyle\times\colon\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}

which are right adjoints to the
1
-morphisms
!\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathds{1}
and
\Delta\>\colon\!=\langle\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}},\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}\rangle\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}
, respectively. The next result establishes that the Hom-categories
{\mathbf{K}}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{X}]
are Cartesian provided that
\mathbb{X}
is a Cartesian object of
{\mathbf{K}}
.

Lemma 3.3
.

If
\mathbb{X}
is a Cartesian object of
{\mathbf{K}}
, the Hom-categories
{\mathbf{K}}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{X}]
are Cartesian, whose terminal object is the
1
-morphism

\displaystyle\top\colon\mathbb{X}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{!^{\prime}}\mathds{1}\xrightarrow{\ast}\mathbb{X}

and whose Cartesian product between two
1
-morphisms
A,B\colon\mathbb{X}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{X}
is the
1
-morphism

\displaystyle A\Box B\colon\mathbb{X}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\langle A,B\rangle}\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\xrightarrow{\times}\mathbb{X}

and whose projections are the
2
-morphisms

\displaystyle\pi_{1}\colon A\times B=\times\circ\langle A,B\rangle\xrightarrow{\pi_{1}\langle A,B\rangle}\Pi_{1}\circ\langle A,B\rangle=A

\displaystyle\pi_{2}\colon A\times B=\times\circ\langle A,B\rangle\xrightarrow{\pi_{2}\langle A,B\rangle}\Pi_{2}\circ\langle A,B\rangle=B

where
\Pi_{1},\Pi_{2}\colon\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}
are the two projections and
\pi_{k}\colon\times\Rightarrow\Pi_{k}
are the corresponding
2
-morphisms.

Proof.

Consider the units and the counits of the adjunctions
(\eta^{\ast},\varepsilon^{\ast})\colon!\dashv\ast
and
(\eta^{\times},\varepsilon^{\times})\colon\Delta\dashv\times
in
{\mathbf{K}}
:

\displaystyle\eta^{\ast}

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}\to\ast\>\circ\>!

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\ast}

\displaystyle\colon!\circ\ast\to\mathsf{id}_{\mathds{1}}

\displaystyle\eta^{\times}

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}\to\times\circ\Delta

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\times}

\displaystyle\colon\Delta\circ\times\to\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}}

Consider a
1
-morphism
A\colon\mathbb{X}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{X}
and define the following
2
-morphism

\displaystyle A\xrightarrow{\eta^{\ast}_{A}}\ast\>\circ\>!\circ A=\ast\>\circ\>!^{\prime}

where we used that
!\circ A=!^{\prime}
, since
\mathds{1}
is
2
-terminal in
{\mathbf{K}}
. If
\varphi\colon A\to\top
is a
2
-morphism, by employing the counit
\varepsilon^{\ast}
we define a
2
-morphism

\displaystyle!\circ A\xrightarrow{\varphi}!\circ\ast\>\circ\>!\circ A\xrightarrow{\varepsilon^{\ast}!A}!\circ A

However, since
\mathds{1}
is
2
-terminal in
{\mathbf{K}}
, such a morphism must coincide with the identity on
!\circ A
. Therefore, by employing the unit again and using the triangle identities, we conclude that
\varphi
must coincide with
\eta^{\ast}
, namely, that
\top
is the terminal object of
{\mathbf{K}}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{X}]
. To prove that
A\Box B
is the Cartesian product of two
1
-morphisms
A,B\colon\mathbb{X}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{X}
, first, notice that the counit
\varepsilon^{\times}
gives rise to the two projections

\displaystyle\pi_{1}\colon\times=\Pi_{1}\circ\Delta\circ\times\xrightarrow{\Pi_{1}(\varepsilon^{\times})}\Pi_{1}

\displaystyle\pi_{2}\colon\times=\Pi_{2}\circ\Delta\circ\times\xrightarrow{\Pi_{2}(\varepsilon^{\times})}\Pi_{2}

where we used that
\Pi_{k}\circ\Delta=\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}
since
\Delta=\langle\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}},\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}\rangle
, by definition. From this and using the triangle identities of
\eta^{\times}
and
\varepsilon^{\times}
, one deduces that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A\Box B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}

is a product diagram in
{\mathbf{K}}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{X}]
.
∎

[
18
, Definition 3.13]
establishes that a Cartesian tangentad is a Cartesian object in the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
of tangentads of
{\mathbf{K}}
. From this observation, it follows by Lemma
3.3
that the Hom-categories
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
are Cartesian categories, provided that
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is a Cartesian tangentad. One also needs the tangent structure of Proposition
3.1
to be compatible with the Cartesian structure.

Proposition 3.4
.

The Hom-tangent categories
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
are Cartesian tangent categories provided that
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is a Cartesian tangentad.

Proof.

Since the tangent structure and the Cartesian structure of
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
are both induced by the tangent structure and the Cartesian structure of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, which are compatible since it is a Cartesian object in
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
, it follows that
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
is a Cartesian tangent category.
∎

4
PIE limits for tangent objects

PIE limits, namely, the
2
-limits generated by
2
-Products, Inserters, and Equifiers, are a special class of weighted limits in the context of
2
-category theory. They were introduced in
[
22
]
to construct the Eilenberg-Moore object
\mathbb{X}^{S}
of a monad
(\mathbb{X},S)
internal to a
2
-category. In Sections
5
and
8
we achieve a similar result for the formal theory of tangentads: we use (some) (P)IE limits to construct vector fields, affine connections, and, under one extra assumption, linear connections of tangentads
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
internal to
{\mathbf{K}}
.

In general, the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
of tangentads of
{\mathbf{K}}
does not admit PIE limits, even when the base
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
does so, the main obstruction being the non-invertibility of the distributive law
\alpha
of the
1
-morphisms
(F,\alpha)
of
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
. However, we show that, when we consider strong tangent morphisms, one can lift PIE limits from the base
2
-category to the
2
-category of tangentads.

4.1
Products

For starters, let us consider
2
-products. It is not hard to believe that if a
2
-category admits
2
-products, so does the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
of tangentads.

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
denote two tangentads of
{\mathbf{K}}
. If the
2
-product
\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
between the underlying objects
\mathbb{X}
and
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
exists in
{\mathbf{K}}
, then
\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
comes equipped with a tangent structure
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{T}^{\prime}
and the corresponding tangentad
(\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
is the
2
-product of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
in
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
. In particular, if
{\mathbf{K}}
admits all
2
-products so does
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
and are preserved by the forgetful
2
-functor
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})\to{\mathbf{K}}
. Moreover, the projections
\pi_{1}\colon(\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
\pi_{2}\colon(\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
are strict tangent morphisms.

Proof.

Let us start by showing that
\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
comes equipped with a tangent structure. First, notice that, from the universal property of the
2
-product
\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
, we can construct a functor

\displaystyle\mathsf{END}_{\mathbf{K}}(\mathbb{X})\times\mathsf{END}_{\mathbf{K}}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{END}_{\mathbf{K}}(\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}^{\prime})

which sends a pair
(A,B)
of endomorphisms
A\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}
and
B\colon\mathbb{X}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
to the endomorphism
A\times B\colon\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
and a pair
(\varphi,\psi)
of
2
-morphisms
\varphi\colon A\Rightarrow A^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}
,
\psi\colon B\Rightarrow B^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{X}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
to the
2
-morphism
\varphi\times\psi\colon A\times B\Rightarrow A^{\prime}\times B^{\prime}
. It is also easy to check that such a functor preserves the monoidal structure given by the composition of endomorphisms. Finally, if
c\mapsto(D_{1}(c),D_{2}(c))
is a diagram in
\mathsf{END}_{\mathbf{K}}(\mathbb{X})\times\mathsf{END}_{\mathbf{K}}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime})
and
(L_{1},L_{2})\to(D_{1}(c),D_{2}(c))
is a limit cone for such a diagram, the corresponding morphism
L_{1}\times L_{2}\to D_{1}(c)\times D_{2}(c)
is a limit cone for the diagram
c\mapsto D_{1}(c)\times D_{2}(c)
of
\mathsf{END}_{\mathbf{K}}(\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}^{\prime})
. Since
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
are tangentads, we can consider their Leung functors
\mathfrak{L}[\mathbb{T}]\colon\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}\to\mathsf{END}_{\mathbf{K}}(\mathbb{X})
and
\mathfrak{L}[\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]\colon\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}\to\mathsf{END}_{\mathbf{K}}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime})
. Therefore, we construct a new Leung functor

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}[\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}\xrightarrow{\left\langle\mathfrak{L}[\mathbb{T}],\mathfrak{L}[\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]\right\rangle}\mathsf{END}_{\mathbf{K}}(\mathbb{X})\times\mathsf{END}_{\mathbf{K}}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{END}_{\mathbf{K}}(\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}^{\prime})

which defines a tangent structure
\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{T}^{\prime}
for
\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
. Furthermore, by construction,
\mathfrak{L}[\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]
is precisely the product of
\mathfrak{L}[\mathbb{T}]
and
\mathfrak{L}[\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]
in the category of Leung functors, whose objects are Leung functors and morphisms are monoidal natural transformations between the underlying strong monoidal functors. Thus,
(\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
is the
2
-product of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
in
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
.
∎

4.2
Inserters

In this section, we discuss the existence of inserters in the context of tangentads. First, let us recall the definition of an inserter in a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
. Consider a pair of
1
-morphisms
F,G\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
and an object
\mathbb{Y}
of
{\mathbf{K}}
and denote by
\mathsf{INSERT}[\mathbb{Y};F,G]
the category whose objects are
1
-morphisms
H\colon\mathbb{Y}\to\mathbb{X}
of
{\mathbf{K}}
together with a
2
-morphism
\tau\colon F\circ H\Rightarrow G\circ H
and whose morphisms
\varphi\colon(H,\tau)\to(H^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})
are
2
-morphisms
\varphi\colon H\Rightarrow H^{\prime}
of
{\mathbf{K}}
which commute with
\tau
and
\tau^{\prime}
, namely, making the following diagram commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\circ H}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\circ H^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G\circ H}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G\circ H^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\varphi}

An inserter
\mathbb{I}\colon F\Rightarrow G
of a pair of parallel
1
-morphisms
F,G\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
of a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
consists of an object
\mathbb{I}
together with a
1
-morphism
V\colon\mathbb{I}\to\mathbb{X}
and a
2
-morphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\theta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}

such that for each object
\mathbb{Y}
of
{\mathbf{K}}
, the induced functor

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathbb{I}}

\displaystyle\colon{\mathbf{K}}[\mathbb{Y},\mathbb{I}]\to\mathsf{INSERT}[\mathbb{Y};F,G]

which sends each
1
-morphism
H\colon\mathbb{Y}\to\mathbb{I}
to
(V\circ H\colon\mathbb{Y}\to\mathbb{X},\hat{\theta}_{H})
and each
2
-morphism
\psi\colon H\Rightarrow H^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{Y}\to\mathbb{I}
to
\psi_{V}\colon(V\circ H,\hat{\theta}_{H})\to(V\circ H^{\prime},\hat{\theta}_{H^{\prime}})
is an isomorphism of categories.

In general, there is no reason to believe that the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
would admit all inserters even when
{\mathbf{K}}
does so. To understand why, consider two lax tangent morphisms
(F,\alpha),(G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
between two tangent categories. To make the inserter
\mathbb{I}
of the two functors
F,G\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
into a tangent category, we need to whisker the tangent bundle functor of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
with the distributive laws as follows:

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(M,u)=(\mathrm{T}M,F\mathrm{T}M\xrightarrow{\alpha}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}FM\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}u}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}GM\xleftarrow{\beta}G\mathrm{T}M)

However, the distributive lax
\beta
is pointing in the wrong direction. The solution is to assume
(G,\beta)
to be strong. Furthermore, we also need to assume that the functor
G
preserves the
n
-fold pullback of the projection along itself and the universal property of the vertical lift.

Definition 4.2
.

A
1
-morphism
G\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}^{\prime}

preserves the fundamental tangent limits
of a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
provided that each functor
{\mathbf{K}}[\mathbb{X}\>|\>G,\beta]\colon\mathsf{END}_{\mathbf{K}}(\mathbb{X})\to{\mathbf{K}}[\mathbb{X}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime}]
preserves the pointwise
n
-fold pullback of the projection
p\colon\mathrm{T}\Rightarrow\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}
along itself and the pointwise pullback of the universality of the vertical lift
l\colon\mathrm{T}\Rightarrow\mathrm{T}^{2}
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. We also say that a tangent morphism
(G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
preserves the fundamental tangent limits provided that its underlying
1
-morphism
G
preserves the fundamental tangent limits of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

We start with a lemma.

Lemma 4.3
.

Consider two parallel lax tangent morphisms
(F,\alpha),(G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
of a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
and let
\mathbb{Y}
be an object of
{\mathbf{K}}
. If
(G,\beta)
is a strong tangent morphism that preserves the fundamental tangent limits, the category
\mathsf{INSERT}[\mathbb{Y};F,G]
is a tangent category where the tangent bundle functor sends an object
(H\colon\mathbb{Y}\to\mathbb{X},\tau)
to
(\mathrm{T}\circ H,\beta^{-1}\circ\tau_{\mathrm{T}}\circ\alpha)
.

Proof.

Let
\mathbb{Y}
be an object of
{\mathbf{K}}
. The category
\mathsf{INSERT}[\mathbb{Y};F,G]
is a subcategory of the Hom-category
{\mathbf{K}}[\mathbb{Y},\mathbb{X}]
, which is a tangent category with the pointwise tangent structure induced by
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. Consider an object
(H,\tau)
of
\mathsf{INSERT}[\mathbb{Y};F,G]
. It is clear that
(\mathrm{T}\circ H,\beta^{-1}\circ\tau_{\mathrm{T}}\circ\alpha)
is also an object of
\mathsf{INSERT}[\mathbb{Y};F,G]
. Moreover, by the compatibilities between the distributive laws
\alpha
and
\beta
with the tangent structures, it is easy to see that the structural natural transformations of the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{Y}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
restrict to
\mathsf{INSERT}[\mathbb{Y};F,G]
. Moreover, since the fundamental tangent limits of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, namely, the
n
-fold pullback of the projection along itself and the universality of the vertical lift, are pointwise limits, they are preserved by pre-composition with any
H\colon\mathbb{Y}\to\mathbb{X}
, thus,
(\mathrm{T}_{n}\circ H,\beta^{-1}_{n}\circ\tau_{\mathrm{T}_{n}}\circ\alpha_{n})
is the
n
-fold pullback of the projection in
\mathsf{INSERT}[\mathbb{Y};F,G]
, where:

\displaystyle\alpha_{n}\>\colon\!=\langle\alpha\circ F\pi_{1},\dots,\alpha\circ F\pi_{n}\rangle

\displaystyle\colon F\circ\mathrm{T}_{n}\Rightarrow\mathrm{T}^{\prime}_{n}\circ F

\displaystyle\beta_{n}^{-1}\>\colon\!=\langle\beta^{-1}\circ{\pi_{1}}_{G},\beta^{-1}\circ{\pi_{n}}_{G}\rangle

\displaystyle\colon\mathrm{T}^{\prime}_{n}\circ G\Rightarrow G\circ\mathrm{T}_{n}

Similarly, the vertical lift of
\mathsf{INSERT}[\mathbb{Y};F,G]
is universal. Thus,
\mathsf{INSERT}[\mathbb{Y};F,G]
is a tangent subcategory of
[\mathbb{Y}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
.
∎

Thanks to Lemma
4.3
, we can prove that inserters from lax tangent morphisms to strong tangent morphisms which preserve the fundamental tangent limits exist in
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
provided they exist in
{\mathbf{K}}
.

Proposition 4.4
.

Consider two parallel lax tangent morphisms
(F,\alpha),(G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
of a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
. Assume also that
(G,\beta)
is a strong tangent morphism which preserves the fundamental tangent limits. If there exists an inserter
\mathbb{I}\colon F\Rightarrow G
of
F
and
G
in
{\mathbf{K}}
, then
\mathbb{I}
comes equipped with a tangent structure
\mathbb{T}^{\mathbb{I}}
such that the universal
1
-morphism
V\colon\mathbb{I}\to\mathbb{X}
becomes a strict tangent morphism that preserves the fundamental tangent limits, the universal
2
-morphism
\hat{\theta}\colon F\circ V\Rightarrow G\circ G
becomes a tangent
2
-morphism and
(\mathbb{I},\mathbb{T}^{\mathbb{I}})\colon(F,\alpha)\Rightarrow(G,\beta)
becomes an inserter of
(F,\alpha)
and
(G,\beta)
in
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
. In particular, if
{\mathbf{K}}
admits inserters, so does
\mathsf{TNG}_{\cong}({\mathbf{K}})
.

Proof.

Thanks to Lemma
4.3
, for each object
\mathbb{Y}
of
{\mathbf{K}}
, the category
\mathsf{INSERT}[\mathbb{Y};F,G]
comes equipped with a tangent structure. This construction extends to a
2
-functor

\displaystyle\mathsf{INSERT}[-;F,G]\colon{\mathbf{K}}^{\mathsf{op}}\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}

However, by the universal property of the inserter,
\mathsf{INSERT}[-;F,G]\cong{\mathbf{K}}[-,\mathbb{I}]
is corepresentable. Therefore, by Corollary
3.2
, it is equivalent to a tangent structure
\mathbb{T}^{\mathbb{I}}
on
\mathbb{I}
. Now, consider a lax tangent morphism
(H,\gamma)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
together with a tangent
2
-morphism
\tau\colon(F,\alpha)\circ(H,\gamma)\Rightarrow(G,\beta)\circ(H,\gamma)
. By the universal property of the inserter, there exists a
1
-morphism
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muH\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\colon\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime}\to\mathbb{I}
such that:

\displaystyle V\circ\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muH\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu=H

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}_{H}=\tau

The
2
-morphism

\displaystyle V\circ\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muH\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\circ\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}=H\circ\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{\gamma}\mathrm{T}\circ H=\mathrm{T}\circ V\circ\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muH\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu

becomes a morphism of
\mathsf{INSERT}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime}\>|\>F,G]
, thus, by the universal property of the inserter, it induces a
2
-morphism

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\gamma\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu

\displaystyle\colon\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muH\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\circ\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow\mathrm{T}\circ\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muH\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu

which makes
(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muH\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu,\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\gamma\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
into a lax tangent morphism. Similarly, one can use the universal property of the inserter to prove that a tangent
2
-morphism
\varphi\colon(H,\gamma)\Rightarrow(H^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})
which commutes with
\tau
and
\tau^{\prime}
induces a unique tangent
2
-morphism
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\varphi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\colon(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muH\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu,\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\gamma\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\Rightarrow(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muH\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\prime},\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\gamma\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\prime})
.
∎

4.3
Equifiers

In the previous section, we proved the existence of inserters in the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
from lax tangent morphisms to strong tangent morphisms which preserve the fundamental tangent limits, provided the existence of these inserters in
{\mathbf{K}}
. In this section, we discuss the existence of equifiers in
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
. First, recall that an equifier
\mathbb{E}\colon\varphi\to\psi
between two
2
-morphisms
\varphi,\psi\colon F\Rightarrow G\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
in a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
consists of an object
\mathbb{E}
together with a
1
-morphism
W\colon\mathbb{E}\to\mathbb{X}
such that
\varphi_{W}=\psi_{W}
and the induced functor

\displaystyle{\mathbf{K}}(\mathbb{Y},W)\colon{\mathbf{K}}(\mathbb{Y},\mathbb{E})\to\mathsf{EQUIF}(\mathbb{Y};\varphi,\psi)

is an isomorphism of categories, where
\mathsf{EQUIF}(\mathbb{Y};\varphi,\psi)
is the category whose objects are
1
-morphisms
H\colon\mathbb{Y}\to\mathbb{X}
such that
\varphi_{H}=\psi_{H}
and morphisms are
2
-morphisms
\xi\colon H\Rightarrow H^{\prime}
.

In the context of categories, the equifier between two natural transformations
\varphi,\psi\colon F\Rightarrow G\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
is the subcategory
\mathbb{E}
of
\mathbb{X}
of objects
M
for which
\varphi_{M}=\psi_{M}
and all morphisms of
\mathbb{X}
.

As for inserters, the tangent structures do not lift to the equifier of two tangent
2
-morphisms between lax tangent morphisms. However, this is the case when the target tangent morphism is strong and preserves the fundamental tangent limits.

Lemma 4.5
.

Consider two parallel lax tangent morphisms
(F,\alpha),(G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
of a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
, two tangent
2
-morphisms
\varphi,\psi\colon(F,\alpha)\Rightarrow(G,\beta)
, and let
\mathbb{Y}
be an object of
{\mathbf{K}}
. If
(G,\beta)
is a strong tangent morphism that preserves the fundamental tangent limits, the category
\mathsf{EQUIF}[\mathbb{Y};\varphi,\psi]
is a tangent category where the tangent bundle functor sends an object
H\colon\mathbb{Y}\to\mathbb{X}
to
\mathrm{T}\circ H
.

Proof.

As per
\mathsf{INSERT}[\mathbb{Y};F,G]
, the category
\mathsf{EQUIF}[\mathbb{Y};\varphi,\psi]
is a subcategory of the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{Y}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
. Consider an object of
\mathsf{EQUIF}[\mathbb{Y};\varphi,\psi]
, namely, a
1
-morphism
H\colon\mathbb{Y}\to\mathbb{X}
such that
\varphi_{H}=\psi_{H}
. We want to prove that
\varphi_{\mathrm{T}\circ H}=\psi_{\mathrm{T}\circ H}

\displaystyle\quad\varphi_{\mathrm{T}\circ H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\quad(\varphi_{\mathrm{T}})_{H}

\displaystyle(\varphi_{\mathrm{T}}=\beta^{-1}\circ\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\varphi\circ\alpha)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\quad(\beta^{-1}\circ\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\varphi\circ\alpha)_{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\quad\beta^{-1}_{H}\circ\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\varphi_{H}\circ\alpha_{H}

\displaystyle(\varphi_{H}=\psi_{H})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\quad\beta^{-1}_{H}\circ\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\psi_{H}\circ\alpha_{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\quad(\beta^{-1}\circ\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\psi\circ\alpha)_{H}

\displaystyle(\beta^{-1}\circ\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\psi\circ\alpha=\psi_{\mathrm{T}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\quad(\psi_{\mathrm{T}})_{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\quad\psi_{\mathrm{T}\circ H}
