where we used that
\varphi
and
\psi
are tangent
2
-morphisms and that
\beta
is invertible. Similarly, using that
G
preserves the fundamental tangent limits, it is not hard to see that
-\circ\mathrm{T}_{n}
defines the
n
-fold pullback of the projection along itself and that the vertical lift is universal. Thus,
\mathsf{EQUIF}[\mathbb{Y};\varphi,\psi]
is a tangent subcategory of the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{Y}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
.
∎

Proposition 4.6
.

Consider two parallel lax tangent morphisms
(F,\alpha),(G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
of a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
and two tangent
2
-morphisms
\varphi,\psi\colon(F,\alpha)\Rightarrow(G,\beta)
. Assume also that
(G,\beta)
is a strong tangent morphism which preserves the fundamental tangent limits. If there exists an equifier
\mathbb{E}\colon\varphi\to\psi
of
\varphi
and
\psi
in
{\mathbf{K}}
, then
\mathbb{E}
comes equipped with a tangent structure
\mathbb{T}^{\mathbb{E}}
such that the universal
1
-morphism
W\colon\mathbb{E}\to\mathbb{X}
becomes a strict tangent morphism that preserves the fundamental tangent limits and
(\mathbb{E},\mathbb{T}^{\mathbb{E}})\colon\varphi\to\psi
becomes an equifier of
\varphi
and
\psi
in
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
. In particular, if
{\mathbf{K}}
admits equifiers, so does
\mathsf{TNG}_{\cong}({\mathbf{K}})
.

Proof.

Thanks to Lemma
4.5
, for each object
\mathbb{Y}
of
{\mathbf{K}}
, the category
\mathsf{EQUIF}[\mathbb{Y};\varphi,\psi]
comes equipped with a tangent structure. This construction extends to a
2
-functor

\displaystyle\mathsf{EQUIF}[-;\varphi,\psi]\colon{\mathbf{K}}^{\mathsf{op}}\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}

However, by the universal property of the equifier,
\mathsf{EQUIF}[-;\varphi,\psi]\cong{\mathbf{K}}[-,\mathbb{E}]
is corepresentable. Therefore, by Corollary
3.2
, it is equivalent to a tangent structure
\mathbb{T}^{\mathbb{E}}
on
\mathbb{E}
. Now, consider a lax tangent morphism
(H,\gamma)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
such that
\varphi_{H}=\psi_{H}
. By the universal property of the equifier, there exists a unique
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muH\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\colon\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime}\to\mathbb{E}
such that
W\circ\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muH\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu=H
. Moreover,
\gamma\colon H\circ\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow\mathrm{T}\circ H
becomes a morphism of
\mathsf{EQUIF}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime};\varphi,\psi]
. Thus, by the universal property of the equifier, it corresponds to a
2
-morphism
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\gamma\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\colon\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muH\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\circ\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}\to\mathrm{T}^{\mathbb{E}}\circ\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muH\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
, which makes
(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathsf{H}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu,\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\gamma\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
into a lax tangent morphism. Finally, the universal property of the equifier implies that every tangent
2
-morphism
\xi\colon(H,\gamma)\Rightarrow(H^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})
corresponds to a tangent
2
-morphism
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\xi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\colon(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muH\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu,\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\gamma\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\Rightarrow(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muH\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\prime},\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\gamma\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\prime})
.
∎

4.4
Pullbacks

In various constructions, we require the existence of some
2
-pullbacks in the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
. In this section, we study which
2
-pullbacks in the base
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
lift to
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
.

Consider two strict tangent morphisms
F\colon(\mathbb{X}_{1},\mathbb{T}_{1})\to(\mathbb{X}_{0},\mathbb{T}_{0})
and
G\colon(\mathbb{X}_{2},\mathbb{T}_{2})\to(\mathbb{X}_{0},\mathbb{T}_{0})
and let us assume the
2
-pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

exists in
{\mathbf{K}}
. Consider also the following
2
-pullback in
\mathsf{CAT}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X}_{1})\times_{{\mathbf{K}}[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{X}_{0}]}\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X}_{2})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X}_{2})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{K}}[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{X}_{2}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X}_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{K}}[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{X}_{1}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathbf{K}}[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{X}_{0}]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{K}}[\Pi_{2},\mathbb{X}_{2}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{K}}[\mathbb{X},G]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{K}}[\Pi_{1},\mathbb{X}_{1}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{K}}[\mathbb{X},F]}

(4.1)

The category
\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X}_{1})\times_{{\mathbf{K}}[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{X}_{0}]}\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X}_{2})
has per objects pairs
(A,B)
formed by endomorphisms
A\colon\mathbb{X}_{1}\to\mathbb{X}_{1}
and
B\colon\mathbb{X}_{2}\to\mathbb{X}_{2}
such that the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

and morphisms are pairs
(\varphi,\psi)\colon(A,B)\to(A^{\prime},B^{\prime})
of
2
-morphisms
\varphi\colon A\Rightarrow A^{\prime}
and
\psi\colon B\Rightarrow B^{\prime}
such that:

\displaystyle F\varphi_{\Pi_{1}}=G\psi_{\Pi_{2}}\colon F\circ A\circ\Pi_{1}=G\circ B\circ\Pi_{2}\to G\circ B^{\prime}\circ\Pi_{2}=F\circ A^{\prime}\circ\Pi_{1}

Using the universal property of the
2
-pullback of Equation (
4.1
), we can define a functor

\displaystyle\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X}_{1})\times_{{\mathbf{K}}[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{X}_{0}]}\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X}_{2})\to\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X})

which sends each pair
(A,B)
to
A\times_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}B\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}
and each morphism
(\varphi,\psi)\colon(A,B)\to(A^{\prime},B^{\prime})
to
\varphi\times_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}\psi\colon A\times_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}B\to A^{\prime}\times_{\mathbb{X}_{0}}B^{\prime}
.

Proposition 4.7
.

If
F\colon(\mathbb{X}_{1},\mathbb{T}_{1})\to(\mathbb{X}_{0},\mathbb{T}_{0})
and
G\colon(\mathbb{X}_{2},\mathbb{T}_{2})\to(\mathbb{X}_{0},\mathbb{T}_{0})
are two strict tangent morphisms which preserve the fundamental tangent limits and the
2
-pullback of Equation (
4.1
) exists in
{\mathbf{K}}
, then the
2
-pullback
\mathbb{X}
of
F
along
G
comes equipped with a tangent structure
\mathbb{T}
which makes the projections
\Pi_{k}\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X}_{k},\mathbb{T}_{k})
(
k=1,2
) strict tangent morphisms which preserve the fundamental tangent limits and
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
becomes the
2
-pullback of
F
and
G
in
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
.

Proof.

Since
F
and
G
are strict tangent morphisms,
(\mathrm{T}_{1},\mathrm{T}_{2})
becomes an object of
\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X}_{1})\times_{{\mathbf{K}}[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{X}_{0}]}\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X}_{2})
since:

\displaystyle F\circ\mathrm{T}_{1}\circ\Pi_{1}=\mathrm{T}_{0}\circ F\circ\Pi_{1}=\mathrm{T}_{0}\circ G\circ\Pi_{2}=G\circ\mathrm{T}_{2}\circ\Pi_{2}

Moreover, since
F
and
G
preserves the fundamental tangent limits, so do each
((\mathrm{T}_{1})_{n},(\mathrm{T}_{2})_{n})
. Using a similar argument,
(p_{1},p_{2})
,
(z_{1},z_{2})
,
(s_{1},s_{2})
,
(l_{1},l_{2})
, and
(c_{1},c_{2})
becomes morphisms of
\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X}_{1})\times_{{\mathbf{K}}[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{X}_{0}]}\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X}_{2})
. Therefore, we can define a functor:

\displaystyle\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}\xrightarrow{\langle\mathfrak{L}[\mathbb{T}_{1}],\mathfrak{L}[\mathbb{T}_{2}]\rangle}\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X}_{1})\times_{{\mathbf{K}}[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{X}_{0}]}\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X}_{2})

Moreover, we can postcompose such a functor by the functor
\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X}_{1})\times_{{\mathbf{K}}[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{X}_{0}]}\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X}_{2})\to\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X})
induced by the universal property of
\mathbb{X}
and obtain a functor:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}[\mathbb{T}]

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{WEIL}_{1}\xrightarrow{\langle\mathfrak{L}[\mathbb{T}_{1}],\mathfrak{L}[\mathbb{T}_{2}]>}\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X}_{1})\times_{{\mathbf{K}}[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{X}_{0}]}\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X}_{2})\to\mathsf{END}(\mathbb{X})

It is not hard to see that this is a strong monoidal functor. To prove that this functor preserves the pointwise fundamental tangent limits, consider an endomorphism
F\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}
and the following commutative diagram of
2
-morphisms:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

where
p
and
\pi_{k}
are
\mathfrak{L}[\mathbb{T}](p)
and
\mathfrak{L}[\mathbb{T}](\pi_{k})
, respectively. By postcomposing the diagram by each
\Pi_{j}
, (
j=1,2
) we obtain the diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi_{j}\circ F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi_{j}\circ\mathrm{T}_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi_{j}\circ\mathrm{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathrm{T}_{j})_{n}\circ\Pi_{j}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{j}\circ\Pi_{j}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{j}\circ\Pi_{j}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi_{j}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi_{j}\circ\mathrm{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi_{j}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{j}\varphi_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{j}\varphi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{j}\pi_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{j}\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{j}p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{n}\Pi_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}\Pi_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{j}\Pi_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{j}\Pi_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Pi_{j}p}

However, the internal square is a pullback diagram, thus, there exists a unique
2
-morphism
\xi_{j}\colon\Pi_{j}\circ F\Rightarrow(\mathrm{T}_{j})_{n}\circ\Pi_{j}
, for each
j=1,2
. Thus, by the universal property of the
2
-pullback of Equation (
4.1
), there exists a unique morphism
\langle\varphi_{1},\varphi_{n}\rangle\colon F\Rightarrow\mathrm{T}_{n}
such that:

\displaystyle\Pi_{j}\langle\varphi_{1},\varphi_{n}\rangle=\xi_{j}

In particular, is the unique
2
-morphism
\langle\varphi_{1},\varphi_{n}\rangle
satisfying the equations

\displaystyle\pi_{k}\circ\langle\varphi_{1},\varphi_{n}\rangle=\varphi_{k}

for
k=1,\dots,n
. Thus,
\mathrm{T}_{n}
is the
n
-fold pullback of
p
along itself. To prove that such a pullback is pointwise, one needs to precompose the previous diagram by an arbitrary morphism
G\colon\mathbb{Y}\to\mathbb{X}
and use that the
n
-fold pullbacks of
p_{j}\colon\mathrm{T}_{j}\to\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}_{j}}
are pointwise. Finally, using a similar strategy, one also shows that the vertical lift is universal. Thus,
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is a tangent category. Finally, to prove that
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is a
2
-pullback in
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
, one uses the universal property of the
2
-pullback (
4.1
) and that the projections
\Pi_{j}
are strict tangent morphisms, which is a consequence of the construction of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.
∎

5
The formal theory of vector fields

A vector field in differential geometry is interpreted as a smooth assignment of a tangent vector for each point of a finite-dimensional smooth manifold. In
[
23
]
and in
[
5
]
, vector fields were introduced in tangent category theory and employed in
[
9
]
to introduce ordinary differential equations in tangent category theory.

In this section, we extend the notion of vector fields to the formal theory of tangentads. For starters, in Section
5.1
we recall the notion of vector fields in tangent category theory, the commutative monoid and the Lie algebra structures of vector fields on an object of a tangent category (with negatives), and the tangent category of vector fields as presented in
[
9
]
.

In Section
5.2
, we characterize the universal property of vector fields in the context of tangent category theory and harness this characterization to define vector fields in the formal context of tangentads.

In Section
5.3
, we prove the functoriality of the construction of vector fields, show that vector fields carry a monad structure, and recover the commutative monoid and the Lie algebra structures in the formal context.

5.1
Vector fields in tangent category theory

A vector field on an object
M
in a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is a section
v\colon M\to\mathrm{T}M
of the projection
p\colon\mathrm{T}M\to M
of the tangent bundle on
M
, namely, the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

Vector fields on an object
M
in a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
form a commutative monoid
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};M)
whose zero element is the zero vector field
z\colon M\to\mathrm{T}M
and the sum
u+v
of two vector fields
u,v\colon M\to\mathrm{T}M
is the vector field:

\displaystyle u+v\colon M\xrightarrow{\langle u,v\rangle}\mathrm{T}_{2}M\xrightarrow{s}\mathrm{T}M

Moreover, when the tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
has negatives,
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};M)
comes equipped with a Lie algebra structure whose Lie bracket
[u,v]
between two vector fields
u,v\colon M\to\mathrm{T}M
is the vector field

\displaystyle[u,v]\>\colon\!=\{\mathrm{T}v\circ u-c\circ\mathrm{T}u\circ v\}

where

\displaystyle f-g\colon X\xrightarrow{\langle f,g\rangle}\mathrm{T}_{2}\mathrm{T}M\xrightarrow{s_{\mathrm{T}M}}\mathrm{T}^{2}M

for two morphisms
f,g\colon X\to\mathrm{T}^{2}M
, and where
\{h\}\colon X\to\mathrm{T}M
is defined by the universal property of the vertical lift as the unique morphism making the following diagram commute

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{2}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{2}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{2}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}\mathrm{T}_{2}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{2}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left\langle\{h\},p_{\mathrm{T}}\circ h\right\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\mathrm{T}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l\times_{\mathrm{T}M}z_{\mathrm{T}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

provided that:

\displaystyle\mathrm{T}p\circ h=z\circ p\circ p_{\mathrm{T}}\circ h

[
9
, Proposition 2.10]
establishes that vector fields of a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
form a new tangent category
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. We briefly recall this construction since it plays a crucial role in the following discourse:

​
[
Objects
]
:

An object of
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is a pair
(M,v)
formed by an object
M
of
\mathbb{X}
together with a vector field
v\colon M\to\mathrm{T}M
;

​
[
Morphisms
]
:

A morphism
f\colon(M,v)\to(N,u)
of
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is a morphism
f\colon M\to N
of
\mathbb{X}
for which the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}

​
[
Tangent bundle functor
]
:

The tangent bundle functor
\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}}
sends a pair
(M,v)
to
(\mathrm{T}M,v_{\mathrm{T}})
, where

\displaystyle v_{\mathrm{T}}\colon\mathrm{T}M\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}v}\mathrm{T}^{2}M\xrightarrow{c}\mathrm{T}^{2}M

and a morphism
f\colon(M,v)\to(N,u)
of vector fields to the morphism
\mathrm{T}f\colon(\mathrm{T}M,v_{\mathrm{T}})\to(\mathrm{T}N,u_{\mathrm{T}})
;

​
[
structural natural transformations
]
:

The structural natural transformations of
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
are the same as the ones of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

\mathsf{VF}
extends to a functor, as established by the next lemma.

Lemma 5.1
.

The assignment
\mathsf{VF}
which sends a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
to the tangent category
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of vector fields of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
extends to a
2
-functor

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}\colon\mathsf{TNGCAT}\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}

which sends a lax tangent morphism
(F,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
to the lax tangent morphism

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}(F,\alpha)\colon\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})

which sends a vector field
(M,v\colon M\to\mathrm{T}M)
to a vector field
(FM,v_{FM})
where

\displaystyle v_{FM}\colon FM\xrightarrow{Fv}F\mathrm{T}M\xrightarrow{\alpha_{M}}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}FM

and whose distributive law coincides with
\alpha
. Moreover,
\mathsf{VF}(F,\alpha)
sends a morphism
f\colon(M,v)\to(N,u)
of vector fields to
Ff\colon(FM,v_{FM})\to(FN,u_{FN})
. Finally, given a
2
-morphism
\varphi\colon(F,\alpha)\Rightarrow(G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}(\varphi)\colon\mathsf{VF}(F,\alpha)\Rightarrow\mathsf{VF}(G,\beta)

is simply
\varphi
.

Proof.

For starters, notice that since
p^{\prime}_{F}\circ\alpha=Fp
:

\displaystyle p^{\prime}_{FM}\circ v_{FM}=p^{\prime}_{FM}\circ\alpha_{M}\circ Fv=Fp_{M}\circ Fv=\mathsf{id}_{FM}

Therefore,
v_{FM}
is a vector field of
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
. To prove that
Ff
is a morphism of vector fields, consider the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FM}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\mathrm{T}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}FM}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces FN}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\mathrm{T}N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}FN}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ff}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\mathrm{T}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime}Ff}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

The left square diagram commutes since
f
is a morphism of vector fields, and the right square diagram commutes by naturality of
\alpha
. Therefore,
Ff
is a morphism of vector fields. Let us now prove that
\alpha\colon(FM,v_{FM})\to(FN,u_{FN})
is a morphism of vector fields. This amounts to prove that the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}FM}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}F\mathrm{T}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}}^{2}FM}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}}^{2}FM}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\mathrm{T}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\mathrm{T}^{2}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\mathrm{T}^{2}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}F\mathrm{T}M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime}Fv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{\prime}_{FM}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\mathrm{T}v}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{\mathrm{T}M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Fc_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{\mathrm{T}M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\alpha_{M}}

However, the left square diagram commutes by naturality of
\alpha
and the right rectangular diagram commutes by the compatibility between
\alpha
and
c
. Finally, each tangent natural transformation
\varphi\colon(F,\beta)\Rightarrow(G,\beta)
is sent to a tangent natural transformation
\mathsf{VF}(\varphi)=\varphi
.
∎

5.2
The universal property of vector fields

This section aims to characterize the correct universal property of the tangent category
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of vector fields of a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. For starters, consider the forgetful functor
\mathsf{U}\colon\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
which sends a pair
(M,v)
to the underlying object
M
and a morphism
f\colon(M,v)\to(N,u)
of vector fields to the underlying morphism
f\colon M\to N
.
\mathsf{U}
strictly preserves the tangent structures; therefore, it can be regarded as an object of the Hom-tangent category
[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
. One can also define a natural transformation
\mathcal{V}\colon\mathsf{U}\to\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{U}

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}\colon\mathsf{U}(M,v)=M\xrightarrow{v}\mathrm{T}M=(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{U})(M,v)

which picks out the vector field from each pair
(M,v)\in\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, where
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{U}
is the tangent bundle functor
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
of
[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
applied to the object
\mathsf{U}
. The naturality of
\mathcal{V}
corresponds to the commutativity of the following diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}(M,v)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{U})(M,v)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}(N,u)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{U}(N,u)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{(M,v)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{U}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{(N,u)}}

for each morphism
f\colon(M,v)\to(N,u)
, which is precisely the condition a morphism
f\colon M\to N
must satisfy in order to be a morphism of vector fields. Furthermore,
\mathcal{V}\colon\mathsf{U}\Rightarrow\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{U}
is compatible with the distributive laws of the two (strong) tangent morphisms
\mathsf{U}
and
\mathrm{T}\mathsf{U}
. Indeed, since
c^{2}=\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{T}^{2}}
, the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}}(M,v)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{U}(M,v)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{2}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{2}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{2}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{U}\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}}(M,v)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{U}(M,v)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{U}(M,v)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}}(M,v)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathcal{V}_{(M,v)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}v}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}v}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muc\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\mathsf{U}}}

where
\alpha
denotes the strict distributive law of
\mathsf{U}
, namely the identity. Finally,
\mathcal{V}\colon\mathsf{U}\to\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{U}
defines a vector field in
[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
since:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}(M,v)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{U}(M,v)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{U}(M,v)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{v}_{(M,v)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mup\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\mathsf{U}(M,v)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

We recap our findings in the following proposition.

Proposition 5.2
.

The forgetful functor
\mathsf{U}\colon\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is a strict tangent morphism. Furthermore, the tangent natural transformation
\mathcal{V}\colon\mathsf{U}\to\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{U}
which picks out the vector field out of each pair
(M,v)\in\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
defines a vector field on
\mathsf{U}
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
.

Our goal is to characterize the universal property enjoyed by the vector field
\mathcal{V}
on
\mathsf{U}
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
. For starters, let us unwrap the definition of a vector field in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]
for two arbitrary tangent categories:

​
[
Base object
]
:

The base object of a vector field in
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]
consists of a lax tangent morphism
(G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
;

​
[
Vector field
]
:

The vector field consists of a collection

\displaystyle u_{A}\colon GA\to\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}GA

of vector fields in
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
for each
A\in\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
. Such a collection must be natural in
A
, and must be compatible with the distributive law of
(G,\beta)
, namely the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G\mathrm{T}^{\prime}A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}GA}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}G\mathrm{T}^{\prime}A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}}^{2}GA}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}}^{2}GA}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}u_{GA}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{\prime\prime}_{GA}}

(5.1)

A morphism of vector fields
\varphi\colon(G,\beta;u)\to(G^{\prime},\beta^{\prime};u^{\prime})
is a tangent natural transformation
\varphi\colon(G,\beta)\Rightarrow(G^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})
, compatible with the vector fields as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}GA}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}G^{\prime}A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GA}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G^{\prime}A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}\varphi_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{\prime}_{A}}

Finally,
\mathsf{VF}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]
also carries a tangent structure whose tangent bundle functor sends a vector field
(G,\beta;u)
to
(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(G,\beta),u_{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu})
, where:

\displaystyle(u_{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu})_{A}\colon\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}GA\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}u_{A}}{\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}}^{2}GA\xrightarrow{c^{\prime\prime}_{GA}}{\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}}^{2}GA

This characterization extends readily to abstract tangentads.

For each tangent category
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
, a vector field
u\colon(G,\beta)\to\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(G,\beta)
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
induces a functor

\displaystyle\Gamma_{u}\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]\to\mathsf{VF}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]

which sends a lax tangent morphism
(H,\gamma)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
to the vector field

\displaystyle\Gamma_{u}(H,\gamma)\colon(G,\beta)\circ(H,\gamma)\xrightarrow{u_{(H,\gamma)}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(G,\beta))\circ(H,\gamma)=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu((G,\beta)\circ(H,\gamma))

defined on the lax tangent morphism
(G,\beta)\circ(H,\gamma)
obtained by postcomposing
(H,\gamma)
by
(G,\beta)
. Concretely, the vector field
\Gamma_{u}(H,\gamma)
is the natural transformation:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(H,\gamma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G,\beta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(G,\beta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}

Furthermore, the functor
\Gamma_{u}
sends a morphism
\varphi\colon(H,\gamma)\to(H^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})
of lax tangent morphisms to a morphism of vector fields:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{u}(\varphi)\colon\Gamma_{u}(H,\gamma)=u_{(H,\gamma)}\xrightarrow{u(\varphi)}u_{(H^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})}

Concretely, this corresponds to the natural transformation:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(H,\gamma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(H^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G,\beta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(G,\beta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}

The functor
\Gamma_{u}
also comes with a distributive law

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\beta}(H,\gamma)\colon\Gamma_{u}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(H,\gamma))\to\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}}(\Gamma_{u}(H,\gamma))

so defined:

\displaystyle G\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}HA\xrightarrow{\beta_{HA}}\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}GHA

To prove that
\Gamma_{\beta}
is a morphism of vector fields is to show the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}G\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}HA}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}}^{2}GHA}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}}^{2}GHA}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}HA}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}GHA}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}\beta_{HA}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{\prime\prime\prime}_{GHA}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}HA}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{u}(\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime}(H,\gamma))_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{HA}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}}(\Gamma_{u}(H,\gamma))_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}u_{HA}}

However, by the commutativity of Diagram (
5.1
) and by
{c^{\prime\prime\prime}}^{2}=\mathsf{id}_{{\mathrm{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}}^{2}}
, this diagram commutes. The compatibility of
\beta
with the tangent structures of
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime})
makes
(\Gamma_{u},\Gamma_{\beta})
into a lax tangent morphism:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(G,\beta;u)}\>\colon\!=(\Gamma_{u},\Gamma_{\beta})\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]\to\mathsf{VF}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]

We recap our findings in the next lemma.

Lemma 5.3
.

A vector field
(G,\beta;u)
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
induces a lax tangent morphism

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(G,\beta;u)}\>\colon\!=(\Gamma_{u},\Gamma_{\beta})\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]\to\mathsf{VF}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]

for each tangentad
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
. Furthermore,
\Gamma_{(G,\beta;u)}
is natural in
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
. Concretely, given a lax tangent morphism
(F,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
, the following diagram commutes in the
2
-category of tangent categories:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{VF}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{VF}[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{(G,\beta;u)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[F,\alpha\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{VF}[F,\alpha\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{(G,\beta;u)}}

Furthermore, when the base tangent morphism
(G,\beta)
is strong (strict), the induced tangent morphism
\Gamma_{(G,\beta;u)}
is also strong (strict).

Proof.

To define the induced lax tangent morphism
\Gamma_{(G,\beta;u)}
, one can readily extend the argument used in the discussion above for tangent categories by removing the dependency of
u
on the object
A
and replacing natural transformations with
2
-morphisms of
{\mathbf{K}}
. We leave the details to the reader. From the definition of the distributive law of
\Gamma_{(G,\beta;u)}
, one immediately concludes that if
(G,\beta)
is strong, respectively strict, so is
\Gamma_{(G,\beta;u)}
. To prove that
(G,\beta;u)
is natural in
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
, consider two lax tangent morphisms
(F,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
and
(H,\gamma)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
. Thus:

\displaystyle(\Gamma_{(G,\beta;u)}\circ[F,\alpha\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}])(H,\gamma))=\Gamma_{(G,\beta;u)}((H,\gamma)\circ(F,\alpha))

\displaystyle\quad=\Gamma_{(G,\beta;u)}((H,\gamma))\circ(F,\alpha)=\mathsf{VF}[F,\alpha\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]\circ\Gamma_{(G,\beta;u)}(H,\gamma)

Finally, since each
[F,\alpha\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]
is a strict tangent morphism,
\Gamma_{(G,\beta;u)}
is a tangent natural transformation.
∎

In particular, by Lemma
5.3
, the vector field
\mathcal{V}
of Proposition
5.2
induces a strict tangent natural transformation

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(\mathsf{U},\mathcal{V})}\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]\to\mathsf{VF}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

natural in
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
. In the following, we denote
\Gamma_{(\mathsf{U},\mathcal{V})}
simply by
\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}
.

We want to prove that
\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}
is invertible. Consider another tangent category
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
together with a lax tangent morphism
(G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and a vector field
u\colon(G,\beta)\to\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(G,\beta)
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
. Since each
u_{A}\colon GA\to\mathrm{T}GA
is a vector field in
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, we can define a functor

\displaystyle\Lambda[u]\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

which sends an object
A
of
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
to the vector field
u_{A}\colon GA\to\mathrm{T}GA
and a morphism
f\colon A\to B
of
Gf
. Notice that by naturality of
u
,
Gf\colon(GA,u_{A})\to(GB,u_{B})
is a morphism of vector fields. Furthermore,
\Lambda[u]
comes with a distributive law:

\displaystyle\Lambda[\beta]\colon\Lambda[u](\mathrm{T}^{\prime}A)=u_{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}A}\xrightarrow{\beta}\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}}u_{A}=\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}}(\Lambda[u](A))

Thanks to the commutativity of Diagram (
5.1
) and to
c^{2}=\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{T}^{2}}
,
\beta\colon(G\mathrm{T}^{\prime}A,u_{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}A})\to(\mathrm{T}GA,c\circ\mathrm{T}u_{A})
is a morphism of vector fields. Furthermore, since
\beta
is compatible with the tangent structures of
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
and
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
,
(\Lambda[u],\Lambda[\beta])
becomes a lax tangent morphism.

Lemma 5.4
.

Given a lax tangent morphism
(G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and a vector field
u\colon(G,\beta)\to\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(G,\beta)
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
, the functor
\Lambda[u]
together with the distributive law
\Lambda[\beta]
defines a lax tangent morphism:

\displaystyle\Lambda[G,\beta;u]\>\colon\!=(\Lambda[u],\Lambda[\beta])\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

Proof.

In the previous discussion, we have defined
\Lambda[G,\beta;u]
as the lax tangent morphism which picks out the vector field
u_{A}\colon GA\to\mathrm{T}GA
for each
A\in\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
and whose distributive law is induced by
\beta
. In particular, the naturality of
u
makes the functor
\Lambda[u]
well-defined, the compatibility of
u
with
\beta
makes
\Lambda[\beta]
into a morphism of vector fields, and the compatibility of
\beta
with the tangent structures of
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
and
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, makes
\Lambda[G,\beta]
into a lax tangent morphism.
∎

We can now prove the universal property of vector fields, which is the main result of this section.

Theorem 5.5
.

The vector field
\mathcal{V}\colon\mathsf{U}\to\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{U}
of Proposition
5.2
is universal. Concretely, the induced strict tangent natural transformation

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]\to\mathsf{VF}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

makes the functor

\displaystyle\mathsf{TNGCAT}^{\mathsf{op}}\xrightarrow{[-\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]}\mathsf{TNGCAT}^{\mathsf{op}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{VF}^{\mathsf{op}}}\mathsf{TNGCAT}^{\mathsf{op}}

which sends a tangent category
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
to the tangent category
\mathsf{VF}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
, into a corepresentable functor. In particular,
\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}
is invertible.

Proof.

Consider a tangent category
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
and a lax tangent morphism
(F,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, which sends each
A\in\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
to the vector field
u_{A}\colon FA\to\mathrm{T}FA
. We want to compare
(F,\alpha)
with
\Lambda[\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(F,\alpha)]
. Concretely,
\Lambda[\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(F,\alpha)]
sends an object
A
of
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
to the vector fields
\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(F,\alpha)_{A}
. However, this corresponds to the vector field
u_{A}\colon FA\to\mathrm{T}FA
. Given a morphism
\varphi\colon(F,\alpha)\to(F^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})
of
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]

\displaystyle\varphi_{A}\colon(FA,u_{A})\to(F^{\prime}A,u^{\prime}_{A})

the morphism
\Lambda[\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(\varphi)]
coincides with
\varphi
, since:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(\varphi)_{A}=FA\xrightarrow{\varphi}F^{\prime}A

Conversely, consider a vector field
(G,\beta;u)
. Thus,
\Lambda[G,\beta;u]
sends each
A
to the vector field
u_{A}\colon GA\to\mathrm{T}GA
. Therefore,
\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(\Lambda[G,\beta;u])
coincides with
(G,\beta;u)
. Similarly, for a given morphism
\varphi\colon(G,\beta;u)\to(G^{\prime},\beta^{\prime};u^{\prime})
of vector fields,
\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(\Lambda[\varphi])
coincides with
\varphi
.

Finally, it is not hard to see that the functor

\displaystyle\Lambda\colon\mathsf{VF}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]\to[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]

extends to a strict tangent morphism, since
\Lambda[\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}}(G,\beta;u)]
sends
A\in\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
to the vector field

\displaystyle\mathrm{T}GA\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}u_{A}}\mathrm{T}^{2}GA\xrightarrow{c_{GA}}\mathrm{T}^{2}GA

while
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(\Lambda[G,\beta;u])
sends
A\in\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
to the vector field

\displaystyle\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}}(u_{A})\colon\mathrm{T}GA\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}u_{A}}\mathrm{T}^{2}GA\xrightarrow{c_{GA}}\mathrm{T}^{2}GA

Therefore,
\Lambda
inverts
\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}
.
∎

Theorem
5.5
establishes the correct universal property of the vector fields construction. Thanks to this result, we can now introduce the notion of vector fields in the formal context of tangentads.

Definition 5.6
.

A tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
in a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}

admits the construction of vector fields
if there exists a tangentad
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
{\mathbf{K}}
together with a strict tangent morphism
\mathsf{U}\colon\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and a vector field
\mathcal{V}\colon\mathsf{U}\to\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{U}
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
such that the induced tangent natural transformation
\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}
of Lemma
5.3
is invertible. The vector field
\mathcal{V}
is called the
universal vector field
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is called the
tangentad of vector fields
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Definition 5.7
.

A
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}

admits the construction of vector fields
provided that every tangentad of
{\mathbf{K}}
admits the construction of vector fields.

We can now rephrase Theorem
5.5
as follows.

Corollary 5.8
.

The
2
-category
\mathsf{CAT}
of categories admits the construction of vector fields and the tangentad of vector fields of a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
\mathsf{CAT}
is the tangent category
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of vector fields of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

To ensure that Definitions
5.6
and
5.7
are well-posed, one requires the tangentad of vector fields of a given tangentad to be unique. The next proposition establishes that such a construction is defined uniquely up to a unique isomorphism.

Proposition 5.9
.

If a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
admits the construction of vector fields, the tangentad of vector fields
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is unique up to a unique isomorphism which extends to an isomorphism of the corresponding universal vector fields of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{U}^{\prime}\colon\mathsf{VF}^{\prime}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
together with a vector field
\mathcal{V^{\prime}}\colon\mathsf{U}^{\prime}\to\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{U}^{\prime}
be a second universal vector field of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. Via the isomorphism

\displaystyle[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]\cong\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]\cong[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathsf{VF}^{\prime}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]

the identity on
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, induces a tangent morphism
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}^{\prime}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
which inverts the tangent morphism
\mathsf{VF}^{\prime}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
induced by the identity on
\mathsf{VF}^{\prime}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
via the isomorphism:

\displaystyle[\mathsf{VF}^{\prime}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathsf{VF}^{\prime}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]\cong\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}^{\prime}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]\cong[\mathsf{VF}^{\prime}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]\qed

5.3
The formal structures of vector fields

In Section
5.1
, we recalled that (a) vector fields of a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
define a new tangent category
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
; (b) the assigment which sends a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
to
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is
2
-functorial; (c) vector fields over an object
M
in a tangent category form a commutative monoid
(\mathsf{VF}(X,\mathbb{T};M),z_{M},+)
; (d) vector fields over an object
M
in a tangent category with negatives form an abelian group
(\mathsf{VF}(X,\mathbb{T};M),z_{M},+,-)
; (e) vector fields over an object
M
in a tangent category with negatives form a Lie algebra
(\mathsf{VF}(X,\mathbb{T};M),z_{M},+,[,])
.

In this section, we recover the same structures for vector fields in the context of tangentads. For starters, notice that, in the context of tangentads, (a) is a built-in property, since
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is, by definition, a tangentad.

In this section,
{\mathbf{K}}
denotes a
2
-category which admits the construction of vector fields.

The functoriality of the construction of vector fields

The first step is to show that (b)
\mathsf{VF}
is
2
-functorial. Consider a lax tangent morphism
(F,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
, which induces a functor:

\displaystyle[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>F,\alpha]\colon[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]\to[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]

Since, by Lemma
5.1
,
\mathsf{VF}
is functorial in the context of tangent categories, this also defines a lax tangent morphism:

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>F,\alpha]\colon\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]\to\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]

However, by the universal property of vector fields:

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]\cong[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})]

Therefore, we obtain a functor

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]\to\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]\cong[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})]

which sends the universal vector field
\mathcal{V}
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
to a lax tangent morphism:

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}(F,\alpha)\colon\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})

Consider now a tangent
2
-morphism
\varphi\colon(F,\alpha)\Rightarrow(G,\beta)
, which induces a tangent natural transformation:

\displaystyle[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\varphi]\colon[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>F,\alpha]\Rightarrow[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>G,\beta]

Since, by Lemma
5.1
,
\mathsf{VF}
is
2
-functorial in the context of tangent categories, this also defines a natural transformation:

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\varphi]\colon\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>F,\alpha]\Rightarrow\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>G,\beta]

Therefore, under the isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]\cong[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})]

the morphism
\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\varphi]_{\mathcal{V}}
associated to the universal vector field
\mathcal{V}
corresponds to a tangent
2
-morphism:

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}(\varphi)\colon\mathsf{VF}(F,\alpha)\Rightarrow\mathsf{VF}(G,\beta)

Proposition 5.10
.

The assignment
\mathsf{VF}
which sends a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
to the tangentad of vector fields
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
extends to a
2
-functor:

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}\colon\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})\to\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})

Proof.

In the previous discussion, we defined the application of
\mathsf{VF}
on a lax tangent morphism
(F,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
as the lax tangent morphism corresponding to the universal vector field
\mathcal{V}
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
along the functor

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]\xrightarrow{\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>F,\alpha]}\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]\cong[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})]

and the application of
\mathsf{VF}
on a tangent
2
-morphism
\varphi\colon(F,\alpha)\Rightarrow(G,\beta)
as the tangent
2
-morphism corresponding to the universal vector field
\mathcal{V}
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
along the natural transformation

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\varphi]\colon\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>F,\alpha]\Rightarrow\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>G,\beta]

under the isomorphism:

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]\cong[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})]

To prove the functoriality of
\mathsf{VF}
, for starters, consider the application of
\mathsf{VF}
on the identity on
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. The induced functor
\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathsf{id}_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}]
coincides with the identity. Consequently, the lax tangent morphism
\mathsf{VF}(\mathsf{id}_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})})
corresponding to the universal vector field must be identity on
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. Consider now two lax tangent morphisms
(F,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
and
(G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
. Thus,
\mathsf{VF}((G,\beta)\circ(F,\alpha))
is the lax tangent morphism corresponding to he universal vector field via the functor:

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]\xrightarrow{\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>(G,\beta)\circ(F,\alpha)]}\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]\cong[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})]

However, by the functoriality of
\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>-]
:

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>(G,\beta)\circ(F,\alpha)]=\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>G,\beta]\circ\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>F,\alpha]

Thus, we can split the functor as follows:

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]\xrightarrow{\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>G,\beta]}\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]\xrightarrow{\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>F,\alpha]}\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]

\displaystyle\quad\cong[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})]

In particular, this functor sends
\mathcal{V}
to
\mathsf{VF}(G,\beta)\circ\mathsf{VF}(F,\alpha)
, thus:

\displaystyle\mathsf{VF}((G,\beta)\circ(F,\alpha))=\mathsf{VF}(G,\beta)\circ\mathsf{VF}(F,\alpha)

Using a similar argument, the identity over a lax tangent morphism
(F,\alpha)
must correspond to the identity over
\mathsf{VF}(F,\alpha)
and the composition
\psi\circ\varphi
of two tangent
2
-morphisms correspond to
\mathsf{VF}(\psi)\circ\mathsf{VF}(\varphi)
.
∎

The commutative monoid of vector fields

This section is concerned with the problem of reconstructing (c) the commutative monoid structure on the set of vector fields over an object in the formal context of tangentads. For this purpose, we assume the existence of the
n
-fold
2
-pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{VF}_{n}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}}

in
{\mathbf{K}}
, of
\mathsf{U}\colon\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
along itself for each tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
{\mathbf{K}}
and for each positive integer
n
. Notice that, by Proposition
4.7

\mathsf{VF}_{n}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is a
2
-pullback in
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
.

In the context of tangent category theory,
\mathsf{VF}_{n}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is the tangent category of tuples
(M;v_{1},\dots,v_{n})
formed by an object
M
of
\mathbb{X}
together with
n
vector fields
v_{1},\dots,v_{n}\colon M\to\mathrm{T}M
of
M
. A morphism
f\colon(M;v_{1},\dots,v_{n})\to(N;u_{1},\dots,u_{n})
in
\mathsf{VF}_{n}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is a morphism
f\colon M\to N
which is a morphism of vector fields
f\colon(M,v_{k})\to(N,u_{k})
for each
k=1,\dots,n
. The tangent structure of
\mathsf{VF}_{n}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is defined as in
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
with the tangent bundle functor acting on each vector field of the
n
-tuples. The
k
-th projection
\pi_{k}\colon\mathsf{VF}_{n}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is the strict tangent morphism which projects each tuple
(M;v_{1},\dots,v_{n})
into the
k
-th vector field
(M,v_{k})
.

For starters, let us define the zero element. Notice first, that the zero morphism
z\colon\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{X}}\Rightarrow\mathrm{T}
of a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
defines a vector field
z\colon\mathsf{id}_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\to(\mathrm{T},c)\circ\mathsf{id}_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}
on the identity of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
, namely:

\displaystyle z_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\in\mathsf{VF}[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

By the universal property of vector fields, this corresponds to a tangent morphism:

\displaystyle 0\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

In the context of tangent category theory,
0
sends an object
M
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
to the zero vector field
(M,z_{M}\colon M\to\mathrm{T}M)
on
M
and a morphism
f\colon M\to N
to the morphism
f\colon(M,z_{M})\to(N,z_{n})
, which is a morphism of vector fields since
z
is natural.

Lemma 5.11
.

The tangent morphism
0\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is strict.

Proof.

The strict tangent morphism
\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}\colon[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}\>|\>\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]\to\mathsf{VF}[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
induced by the universal vector field
\mathcal{V}
sends
0\circ(\mathrm{T},c)
to
z_{(\mathrm{T},c)}
which, by the compatibility between the zero morphism and the canonical flip is equal to the vector field
c\circ\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5muz
, which is also equal to the vector field
c\circ\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(0))
. Since
\mathsf{U}
is a strict tangent morphism and that
\mathcal{V}
, regarded a
2
-morphism, is a tangent morphism:

\displaystyle c\circ\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(0))=\mathcal{V}_{(\mathrm{T},c)}(0)=\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}((\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}},c^{\mathsf{VF}})\circ 0)

By the universal property of
\mathcal{V}
, we conclude that
0\circ(\mathrm{T},c)=(\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}},c^{\mathsf{VF}})\circ 0
. One can apply a similar argument to the structural natural transformations to prove that
0
is a strict tangent morphism.
∎

The next step is to define the sum of two vector fields on the same base. For starters, notice that the tangentad
\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
captures precisely the abstract version of the tangent category of pairs of vector fields on the same base object, therefore, we expect the sum to be a tangent morphism of type
+\colon\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Consider first the general case where
n
is any positive integer and let
\pi_{k}\colon\mathsf{VF}_{n}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
be the
k
-th projection. However,
\pi_{k}
is a (strict) tangent morphism, so in particular, an object of the Hom-tangent category
[\mathsf{VF}_{n}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]
. By the universal property of vector fields, this corresponds to a vector field

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{k}

\displaystyle\colon\pi_{k}\circ\mathsf{U}\to\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(\pi_{k}\circ\mathsf{U})

of
[\mathsf{VF}_{n}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
.

In the context of tangent category theory,
\mathcal{V}_{k}
is the natural transformation

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{k}(M;v_{1},\dots,v_{n})\colon\mathsf{U}\pi_{k}(M;v_{1},\dots,v_{n})=M\xrightarrow{v_{k}}\mathrm{T}M=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{U}\pi_{k}(M;v_{1},\dots,v_{n})

which picks out the
k
-th vector field in each tuple
(M;v_{1},\dots,v_{n})\in\mathsf{VF}_{n}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Consider now the case
n=2
.
\pi_{1},\pi_{2}\colon\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
induce two vector fields
\mathcal{V}_{1}
and
\mathcal{V}_{2}
over the same base object
\mathsf{U}_{2}\>\colon\!=\pi_{1}\circ\mathsf{U}=\pi_{2}\circ\mathsf{U}
. Thus, since these are vector fields in a tangent category, we can define a new vector field
\mathcal{V}_{1}+\mathcal{V}_{2}
, using that vector fields on the same base object of a tangent category can be summed together. By the universal property of vector fields,
\mathcal{V}_{1}+\mathcal{V}_{2}
must correspond to a tangent morphism:

\displaystyle+\colon\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

In the context of tangent category theory,
+
sends a tuple
(M;u,v)
formed by an object
M
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and by two vector fields
u,v\colon M\to\mathrm{T}M
of
M
to the vector field
(M,u+v)
.

Lemma 5.12
.

The tangent morphism
+\colon\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is strict.

Proof.

The strict tangent morphism
\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}\colon[\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]\to\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
induced by the universal vector field
\mathcal{V}
sends
+\circ(\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}_{2}},c^{\mathsf{VF}_{2}})
to:

\displaystyle(\mathcal{V}_{1}+\mathcal{V}_{2})_{(\mathrm{T},c)}=(\mathcal{V}_{1})_{(\mathrm{T},c)}+(\mathcal{V}_{2})_{(\mathrm{T},c)}

However,
\mathcal{V}_{k}=\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(\pi_{k})
. Using that
\mathsf{U}
is a strict tangent morphism and that
\mathcal{V}
is a tangent
2
-morphism:

\displaystyle\quad(\mathcal{V}_{1})_{(\mathrm{T},c)}+(\mathcal{V}_{2})_{(\mathrm{T},c)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\quad\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(\pi_{1})_{(\mathrm{T},c)}+\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(\pi_{2})_{(\mathrm{T},c)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\quad\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(\pi_{1}))+\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(\pi_{2}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\quad\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(\mathcal{V}_{1}+\mathcal{V}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\quad(\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}},c^{\mathsf{VF}})\circ+

Similarly, one can prove the compatibility with the structural
2
-morphisms.
∎

Theorem 5.13
.

Consider a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
which admits the construction of vector fields. Let us also assume the existence of
n
-fold
2
-pullbacks of
\mathsf{U}\colon\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
along itself in
{\mathbf{K}}
. Therefore,
\mathsf{U}
equipped with the strict tangent morphisms
0\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
+\colon\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
becomes an additive bundle in
\mathsf{TNG}_{=}({\mathbf{K}})
.

Proof.

By the universal property of vector fields, the conditions required for
(\mathsf{U},0,+)
to be an additive bundle are equivalent to the following equations of vector fields in the correct Hom-tangent categories:

\displaystyle z+\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{V}

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{1}+\mathcal{V}_{2}=\mathcal{V}_{2}+\mathcal{V}_{1}

\displaystyle(\mathcal{V}_{1}+\mathcal{V}_{2})+\mathcal{V}_{3}=\mathcal{V}_{1}+(\mathcal{V}_{2}+\mathcal{V}_{3})

In particular, the first equation establishes unitality, the second equation ensures commutativity, and the third equation guarantees associativity.
∎

The abelian group of vector fields

In this section, we show that (d) when a base tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
admits negatives, also the tangentad
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of vector field admits negatives. Moreover, we use negatives to define a negation morphism
-\colon\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
which makes the additive bundle
(\mathsf{U},0,+)
of Theorem
5.13
into an abelian group bundle.

Proposition 5.14
.

If a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
admits negatives and the construction of vector fields, the tangentad
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of vector fields of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
admits negatives as well.

Proof.

Consider a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
with negatives. By Proposition
3.1
, also each Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
admits negatives. In particular, the tangent category
[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
admits negatives, with negation denoted by
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mun\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
. It is not hard to see that this negation defines a morphism of vector fields
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mun\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\colon\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}},c^{\mathsf{VF}})\to\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}(\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}},c^{\mathsf{VF}})
. By the universal property of vector fields, this induces a tangent
2
-morphism
n\colon(\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}},c^{\mathsf{VF}})\Rightarrow(\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}},c^{\mathsf{VF}})
. The underlying
2
-morphism
n\colon\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}}\Rightarrow\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}}
defines a negation for the tangentad
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.
∎

The additive structure on
\mathsf{U}\colon\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of Theorem
5.13
is defined by the commutative monoid structure on vector fields in the Hom-tangent categories and by the universal property of vector fields. When the base tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
admits negatives, by Proposition
3.1
, also the tangent categories
\mathsf{VF}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
admit negatives. In particular,
\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
admits negatives. Therefore, one can take the negation of the universal vector field and defining a new vector field
-\mathcal{V}
. By the universal property of vector fields,
-\mathcal{V}
corresponds to a tangent morphism:

\displaystyle-\colon\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

In the context of tangent category theory,
-
sends a vector field
(M,v)
to the vector field
(M,-v)
.

Lemma 5.15
.

The tangent morphism
-\colon\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is strict.

Proof.

The strict tangent morphism
\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}\colon[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]\to\mathsf{VF}[\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
induced by the universal vector field
\mathcal{V}
sends
-\circ(\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}},c^{\mathsf{VF}})
to:

\displaystyle(-\mathcal{V})_{(\mathrm{T},c)}=\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}}(-\mathcal{V})=\Gamma_{\mathcal{V}}((\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}},c^{\mathsf{VF}})\circ-)

By the universal property of vector fields,
-\circ(\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}},c^{\mathsf{VF}})=(\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}},c^{\mathsf{VF}})\circ-
. Similarly, one can prove the compatibility with the structural
2
-morphisms.
∎

Theorem 5.16
.

Consider a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
which admits the construction of vector fields. Let us also assume the existence of
n
-fold
2
-pullbacks of
\mathsf{U}\colon\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
along itself in
{\mathbf{K}}
. For each tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
which admits negatives, the additive bundle
(\mathsf{U}\colon\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}),0,+)
of Theorem
5.13
comes with a negation
-\colon\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
which makes
\mathsf{U}
into an abelian group bundle in
\mathsf{TNG}_{=}{\mathbf{K}}
.

Proof.

By the universal property of vector fields, the condition required for
(\mathsf{U},0,+,-)
to be an abelian group bundle is equivalent to the following equation of vector fields:

\displaystyle-\mathcal{V}+\mathcal{V}=z

In particular, this establishes that the negation defines inverses for the sum.
∎

The Lie algebra of vector fields

In this section, we show (e) how to equip the abelian group bundle of Theorem
5.16
with a suitable Lie bracket, provided the existence of negatives. In Section
5.3
, we introduced the vector fields
\mathcal{V}_{k}
induced by the projections
\pi_{k}\colon\mathsf{VF}_{n}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. When
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
admits negatives, so does the Hom-tangent category
[\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}),(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]
, therefore, we can take the Lie bracket of the two vector fields
\mathcal{V}_{1}
and
\mathcal{V}_{2}
over
\mathsf{U}_{2}=\mathsf{U}\circ\pi_{1}=\mathsf{U}\circ\pi_{2}
and define a new vector field
[\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathcal{V}_{2}]
. By the universal property of vector fields, this corresponds to a tangent morphism:

\displaystyle[,]\colon\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

In the context of tangent category theory
[,]
sends a tuple
(M;u,v)
formed by an object
M
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and by two vector fields
u,v\colon M\to\mathrm{T}M
of
M
to the vector field
(M,[u,v])
.

Remark 5.17
.

In the context of tangent category theory
[,]
is, by construction, a functor. So, in particular, a morphism
f\colon(M;v_{1},v_{2})\to(N;u_{1},u_{2})
of
\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is sent to a morphism:

\displaystyle[,](f)

\displaystyle\colon(M,[v_{1},v_{2}])\to(N,[u_{1},u_{2}])

However, a morphism of
\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is a morphism
f\colon M\to N
which is a morphism of vector fields
f\colon(M,v_{1})\to(M,u_{1})
and
f\colon(M,v_{2})\to(N,u_{2})
.
[,]
sends a morphism
f
to itself. Therefore, the functoriality of
[,]
is equivalent to the following statement in differential geometry: if two pairs of vector fields
v_{1},u_{1}
and
v_{2},u_{2}
are
f
-related, namely,
f
is a morphism of vector fields
f\colon(M,v_{1})\to(N,u_{1})
and
f\colon(M,v_{2})\to(N,u_{2})
, the vector fields
[v_{1},v_{2}]
and
[u_{1},u_{2}]
are also
f
-related. This was proven in tangent category theory in
[
9
, Proposition 2.9]
. The novelty provided by the formal approach is in the simplicity of the proof. While in the original proof this was shown explicitly, from the formal point of view, this is a direct consequence of
[,]
being a functor. This shows that the formal approach can also be adopted to prove results in tangent category theory.

Lemma 5.18
.

The tangent morphism
[,]\colon\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is strict.

Proof.

We leave to the reader to complete the details of this proof, since it is substantially the same proof as the ones of Lemmas
5.11
,
5.12
, and
5.12
.
∎

In order to state the main result of this section, first we need to introduce the notion of a Lie algebra bundle in a category
\mathbb{X}
.

Definition 5.19
.

A
Lie algebra bundle
in a category
\mathbb{X}
consists of an additive bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to B
together with a morphism
[,]_{q}\colon E_{2}\to E
which satisfies the following conditions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{q}\times_{B}\mathsf{id}_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[,]_{q}\times_{B}\mathsf{id}_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[,]_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{q}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle z_{q}\circ q,\mathsf{id}_{E}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[,]_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{q}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle[,]_{q},[,]_{q}\circ\tau\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{q}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle[[,]_{q},]_{q},[[,]_{q},]_{q}\cdot\sigma,[[,]_{q},]_{q}\sigma^{2}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{q}\times_{B}\mathsf{id}_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{q}}

where
[[,]_{q},]_{q}
denotes

\displaystyle[[,]_{q},]_{q}\colon E_{3}\xrightarrow{[,]_{q}\times_{B}\mathsf{id}_{E}}E_{2}\xrightarrow{[,]_{q}}E

and
\sigma
denotes the ciclic permutation
(1\quad 2\quad 3)
whose action is induced by the canonical symmetry
\tau\colon E\times_{B}E^{\prime}\to E^{\prime}\times_{B}E
.

The first two diagrams in Definition
5.19
represent left additivity of the Lie bracket:

\displaystyle[x+_{q}y,z]_{q}=[x,z]_{q}+_{q}[y,z]_{q}

\displaystyle[0_{q},x]_{q}=0_{q}

The third diagram represents antisymmetry:

\displaystyle[x,y]_{q}+[y,x]_{q}=0_{q}

Finally, the last diagram represents the Jacobi identity:

\displaystyle[[x,y]_{q},z]_{q}+[[z,x]_{q}y]_{q}+[[y,z]_{q},x]_{q}=0_{q}

Theorem 5.20
.

Consider a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
which admits the construction of vector fields. Let us also assume the existence of
n
-fold
2
-pullbacks of
\mathsf{U}\colon\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
along itself in
{\mathbf{K}}
. For each tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
which admits negatives, the abelian group bundle
(\mathsf{U}\colon\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}),0,+,-)
of Theorem
5.16
comes with a Lie bracket
[,]\colon\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
which makes
\mathsf{U}
into a Lie algebra bundle in
\mathsf{TNG}_{=}{\mathbf{K}}
.

Proof.

By the universal property of vector fields, the conditions required for
(\mathsf{U},0,+,-,[,])
to be a Lie algebra bundle are equivalent to the following equations of vector fields

\displaystyle[\mathcal{V}_{1}+\mathcal{V}_{2},\mathcal{V}_{3}]=[\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathcal{V}_{3}]+[\mathcal{V}_{2},\mathcal{V}_{3}]

\displaystyle[z,\mathcal{V}]=z

\displaystyle[\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathcal{V}_{2}]+[\mathcal{V}_{2},\mathcal{V}_{1}]=z

\displaystyle[[\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathcal{V}_{2}],\mathcal{V}_{3}]+[[\mathcal{V}_{3},\mathcal{V}_{1}],\mathcal{V}_{2}]+[[\mathcal{V}_{2},\mathcal{V}_{3}],\mathcal{V}_{1}]=z

which hold by
[
5
, Theorem 3.17]
and by
[
6
, Theorem 4.2]
.
∎

The monad of vector fields

In this section we harness the additive bundle structure of Theorem
5.13
to define a
2
-monad structure on the
2
-endofunctor
\mathsf{VF}\colon\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})\to\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
. For starters, let us assume the existence of the
n
-fold pullbacks
\mathsf{VF}_{n}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
in
{\mathbf{K}}
. It is not hard to see that each
\mathsf{VF}_{n}
extends to a
2
-functor. Let us define:

\displaystyle\eta\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\xrightarrow{0}\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle\mu\colon\mathsf{VF}^{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\xrightarrow{\langle\mathsf{U}_{\mathsf{VF}},\mathsf{VF}\mathsf{U}\rangle}\mathsf{VF}_{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\xrightarrow{+}\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

Theorem 5.21
.

The
2
-endofunctor
\mathsf{VF}\colon\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})\to\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
together with the
2
-natural transformations

\displaystyle\eta_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle\mu_{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{VF}^{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

defines a
2
-monad on
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
.

Proof.

Unitality and associativity of
(\mathsf{VF},\eta,\mu)
are a direct consequence of
(\mathsf{U},0,+)
being an additive bundle. We leave the reader to complete the details.
∎

In the context of tangent category theory, the
2
-monad structure of
\mathsf{VF}
is the defined as follows. The unit is equal to the zero
0\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
which sends each object
M
of a tangent category to its zero vector field
(M,z_{M})
. To understand the multiplication, first recall that a vector field
v\colon(M,u)\to\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VF}}(M,u)
in the tangent category
\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
consists of a pair of vector fields
u,v\colon M\to\mathrm{T}M
of an object
M
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, which commute to each other, namely:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{2}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{2}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}v}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}u}

Therefore, the multiplication
\mu\colon\mathsf{VF}^{2}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VF}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
sends a pair of commutative vector fields
(u,v)
on an object
M
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
to the vector field
(M,u+v)
.

Remark 5.22
.

In future work, we would like to classify the (lax/colax/pseudo-)algebras of the
2
-monad
\mathsf{VF}
.

5.4
The construction of vector fields via (P)IE limits

In Section
4
, we discussed under which conditions PIE limits of a
2
-category lift to the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
of tangentads of
{\mathbf{K}}
. In this section, we employ inserters and equifiers to construct vector fields for tangentads. For starters, consider a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. Consider also the following two parallel tangent morphisms:
