\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}s_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{q}}

(b)

Additivity 2. The morphism
(z_{q},l)\colon(q,z_{q},s_{q})\to(p,z,s)
is morphism of additive bundles. Concretely, this corresponds to the commutativity of the following diagrams:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{q}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{2}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\pi_{1}l_{q},\pi_{2}l_{q}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{q}}

(c)

Linearity. The following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{2}E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}l_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}

(d)

Universality. The following is a pullback diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\pi_{1}l_{q},\pi_{2}z\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z}

Remark 7.3
.

In the literature, there are a few variations on the definition of a differential bundle. In particular, the original definition due to Cockett and Cruttwell
[
8
]
did not assume the projection to be display (tangent display maps were introduced by Cruttwell and by the author only more recently in
[
13
]
). Instead, the existence of only some pullbacks was required. As shown in
[
13
]
, requiring the projection to be display is, however, a very natural and desirable property, especially when dealing with connections. In the following, we refer to differential bundles as display differential bundles.

Differential bundles generalize vector bundles in the context of tangent categories
[
21
]
. In the following, we denote by
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
a differential bundle with projection, zero morphism, sum morphism, and vertical lift the morphisms
q\colon E\to M
,
z_{q}\colon M\to E
,
s_{q}\colon E_{2}\to E
, and
l_{q}\colon E\to\mathrm{T}E
, respectively.

Differential bundles of a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
form a new tangent category
\mathsf{DB}^{\ast}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, so defined:

​
[
Objects
]
:

An object of
\mathsf{DB}^{\ast}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is a differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
;

​
[
Morphisms
]
:

A morphism of
\mathsf{DB}^{\ast}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is a pair
(f,g)\colon\mathbf{q}\to\mathbf{q}^{\prime}
of morphisms of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
which commute with the projections

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

​
[
Tangent bundle functor
]
:

The tangent bundle functor sends a differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
to the differential bundle
\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{DB}}\mathbf{q}\>\colon\!=(\mathrm{T}q,\mathrm{T}z_{q},\mathrm{T}s_{q},l_{\mathrm{T}q})
, where we identified
\mathrm{T}E_{2}
with
\mathrm{T}_{2}E
, and whose vertical lift is defined as follows:

\displaystyle l_{\mathrm{T}q}\colon\mathrm{T}E\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}l_{q}}\mathrm{T}^{2}E\xrightarrow{c}\mathrm{T}^{2}E

​
[
Structural natural transformations
]
:

The structural natural transformations of
\mathsf{DB}^{\ast}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
are the same as the ones of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

In
\mathsf{DB}^{\ast}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, a
linear morphism
is a morphism
(f,g)\colon\mathbf{q}\to\mathbf{q}^{\prime}
between differential bundles, satisfying the commutativity of the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{q^{\prime}}}

Differential bundles and linear morphisms form a sub-tangent category of
\mathsf{DB}^{\ast}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, denoted by
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. The operation
\mathsf{DB}^{\ast}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
extends to a
2
-endofunctor, as discussed by the next lemma.

Lemma 7.4
.

There is a
2
-endofunctor

\displaystyle\mathsf{DB}^{\ast}\colon\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\mathscr{D}}\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\cong}

on the
2
-category of tangent categories and display tangent morphisms, which sends a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
to the tangent category
\mathsf{DB}^{\ast}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of display differential bundles of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and that sends a display tangent morphism
(F,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
to the display tangent morphism which sends a display differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
to the display differential bundle
(Fq,Fz_{q},Fs_{q},l_{Fq})
, whose vertical lift is defined as follows:

\displaystyle l_{Fq}\colon FE\xrightarrow{Fl_{q}}F\mathrm{T}E\xrightarrow{\alpha}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}FE

Furthermore,
\mathsf{DB}^{\ast}
restricts to linear morphisms, yielding another
2
-functor:

\displaystyle\mathsf{DB}\colon\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\mathscr{D}}\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\cong}

Proof.

[
8
, Proposition 4.22]
establishes that a Cartesian tangent morphism
[
8
, Definition 4.16]

(F,\alpha)
lifts to a functor
\mathsf{DB}^{\ast}(F)\colon\mathsf{DB}^{\ast}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{DB}^{\ast}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
, which also restricts to linear morphims, yielding a second functor
\mathsf{DB}(F)\colon\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
. However, display tangent morphisms are Cartesian; thus, every display tangent morphism
(F,\alpha)
lifts to a functor
\mathsf{DB}^{\ast}(F,\alpha)
, which preserves linear morphisms. It is not hard to see that the strong distributive law
\alpha
lifts to a strong distributive law
\mathsf{DB}^{\ast}(\alpha)
and
\mathsf{DB}(\alpha)
for the functors
\mathsf{DB}^{\ast}(F)
and
\mathsf{DB}(F)
. Thus

\displaystyle\mathsf{DB}^{\ast}(F,\alpha)\>\colon\!=(\mathsf{DB}^{\ast}(F),\mathsf{DB}^{\ast}(\alpha))

\displaystyle\mathsf{DB}(F,\alpha)\>\colon\!=(\mathsf{DB}(F),\mathsf{DB}(\alpha))

are strong tangent morphisms. Finally,
\mathsf{DB}^{\ast}
and
\mathsf{DB}
send tangent natural transformations to tangent natural transformations in an obvious way.
∎

7.2
The universal property of differential bundles

This section aims to characterize the correct universal property of the tangent category
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of differential bundles and linear morphisms of a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. For starters, consider the functors

\displaystyle\mathsf{Tot}\colon\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle\mathsf{Base}\colon\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

which send a differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
to its total object
E
and its base object
M
, respectively.

The next step is to show that the functors
\mathsf{Base}
and
\mathsf{Tot}
come equipped with the structure of a display differential bundle in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
. Define:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M}\colon\mathsf{Tot}(\mathbf{q})=E\xrightarrow{q}M=\mathsf{Base}(\mathbf{q})

Lemma 7.5
.

The tangent natural transformation
\mathcal{Q}\colon\mathsf{Tot}\Rightarrow\mathsf{Base}
is a tangent display map.

Proof.

By using that for every
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
in
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
,
\mathrm{T}^{n}q
admits all tangent pullbacks, it is easy to compute the pointwise pullback of
{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}^{n}\mathcal{Q}
along
\varphi\colon F\to{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}^{n}\mathsf{Base}
as the unique functor
G\colon\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathbb{X}
such that, for every
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
, the following is a pullback diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G\mathbf{q}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}^{n}\mathsf{Tot}(\mathbf{q})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\mathbf{q}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}^{n}\mathsf{Base}(\mathbf{q})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{\mathbf{q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{\mathbf{q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}^{n}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathbf{q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathbf{q}}}

Furthermore, using that the distributive law
\alpha
of
(F,\alpha)
is a morphism in
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, we obtain a unique morphism
\beta\colon G\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{DB}}\mathbf{q}\to\mathrm{T}G\mathbf{q}
as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{DB}}\mathbf{q}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}^{n+1}\mathsf{Tot}(\mathbf{q})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}G\mathbf{q}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}^{n+1}\mathsf{Tot}(\mathbf{q})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}F\mathbf{q}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}^{n+1}\mathsf{Base}(\mathbf{q})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{DB}}\mathbf{q}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}^{n+1}\mathsf{Base}(\mathbf{q})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{DB}}\mathbf{q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{\mathbf{q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi_{\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{DB}}\mathbf{q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}^{n+1}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathbf{q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}\psi_{\mathbf{q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}\xi_{\mathbf{q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}^{n+1}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathbf{q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}\varphi_{\mathbf{q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{\mathbf{q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{DB}}\mathbf{q}}}

It is not hard to prove that
(G,\beta)\colon\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
becomes a lax tangent morphism which is the pointwise pullback of
{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}^{n}q
along
\varphi
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
.
∎

Notice that each pullback of
\mathcal{Q}
along any tangent natural transformation
\varphi
not only exists but is also pointwise, namely, it is preserved by all functors
{\mathbf{K}}[F\>|\>\mathbb{X}]
for any
1
-morphism
F\colon\mathbb{X}^{\prime}\to\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
{\mathbf{K}}
. Since this plays an important role in our discussion, let us formally introduce this concept.

Definition 7.6
.

Given two tangentads
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
of a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
, a
pointwise tangent display map
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]
consists of a tangent display map
q
in this tangent category for which each pullback of
q
along any morphism of
[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]
is pointwise, namely, preserved by all functors of type
{\mathbf{K}}[F\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime}]
for each
F\colon\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime}\to\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
. Similarly, we call a
pointwise display differential bundle
a differential bundle in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]
whose underlying projection is a pointwise tangent display map.

We want to show that the tangent display map
\mathcal{Q}\colon\mathsf{Tot}\to\mathsf{Base}
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
comes equipped with the structure of a display differential bundle. For starters, let us consider a display differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and define the following morphisms:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z_{Q}}_{\mathbf{q}}\colon\mathsf{Base}(\mathbf{q})=M\xrightarrow{z_{q}}E=\mathsf{Tot}(\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle\mathcal{S_{Q}}_{\mathbf{q}}\colon\mathsf{Tot}_{2}(\mathbf{q})=E_{2}\xrightarrow{s_{q}}E=\mathsf{Tot}(\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle\mathcal{L_{Q}}_{\mathbf{q}}\colon\mathsf{Tot}(\mathbf{q})=E\xrightarrow{l_{q}}\mathrm{T}E=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{Tot}(\mathbf{q})

In particular,
\mathcal{Z_{Q}}
,
\mathcal{S_{Q}}
, and
\mathcal{L_{Q}}
are the tangent natural transformations which pick out the zero morphism
z_{q}
, the sum morphism
s_{q}
, and the vertical lift
l_{q}
of each of the display differential bundles
\mathbf{q}
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, respectively.

Proposition 7.7
.

The strict tangent morphisms
\mathsf{Base}\colon\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
\mathsf{Tot}\colon\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
equipped with
\mathcal{Q}
,
\mathcal{Z_{Q}}
,
\mathcal{S_{Q}}
, and
\mathcal{L_{Q}}
form a pointwise display differential bundle in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
.

Proof.

In Lemma
7.5
we already proved that
\mathbf{q}\colon\mathsf{Tot}\to\mathsf{Base}
is a tangent display map of the Hom-tangent category
[\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
. The axioms required for
\mathcal{Q}\>\colon\!=(\mathcal{Q},\mathcal{Z_{Q}},\mathcal{S_{Q}},\mathcal{L_{Q}})
to be a pointwise display differential bundle are a consequence of
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathbf{q}}=(q,z_{q},s_{q},l_{q})
being a display differential bundle for each
\mathbf{q}\in\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.
∎

To characterize the correct universal property enjoyed by the differential bundle of Proposition
7.7
, let us first unwrap the definition of a pointwise display differential bundle in each Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]
, where
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
are tangent categories:

​
[
Base object
]
:

The base object is a lax tangent morphism
\mathsf{Base}\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
;

​
[
Total object
]
:

The total object is a lax tangent morphism
\mathsf{Tot}\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
;

​
[
Projection
]
:

The projection consists of a pointwise tangent display map:

\displaystyle q\colon\mathsf{Tot}\to\mathsf{Base}

​
[
Zero morphism
]
:

The zero morphism consists of a tangent natural transformation:

\displaystyle z_{q}\colon\mathsf{Base}\to\mathsf{Tot}

​
[
Sum morphism
]
:

The sum morphism consists of a tangent natural transformation:

\displaystyle s_{q}\colon\mathsf{Tot}_{2}\to\mathsf{Tot}

​
[
Vertical lift
]
:

The vertical lift consists of a tangent natural transformation:

\displaystyle l_{q}\colon\mathsf{Tot}\to\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{Tot}

satisfying the axioms of a pointwise display differential bundle. A morphism of pointwise tangent differential bundles in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]
from a differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon(K,\theta)\to(G,\beta)
to a differential bundle
\mathbf{q}^{\prime}\colon(K^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})\to(G^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})
consists of a pair
(\varphi,\psi)
of tangent natural transformations

\displaystyle\varphi\colon(G,\beta)\Rightarrow(G^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})

\displaystyle\psi\colon(K,\theta)\Rightarrow(K^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})

which commutes with the projections
q
and
q^{\prime}
and such that
\psi
commutes with the vertical lifts
l_{q}
and
l_{q}^{\prime}
.

For each tangent category
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
, a pointwise display differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon(K,\theta)\to(G,\beta)
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
induces a functor

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathbf{q}}\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]\to\mathsf{DB}_{\mathsf{pt}}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]

which sends a lax tangent morphism
(H,\gamma)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
to the pointwise display differential bundle

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathbf{q}}(H,\gamma)\>\colon\!=((G,\beta)\circ(H,\gamma),(K,\theta)\circ(H,\gamma);q_{(H,\gamma)},{z_{q}}_{(H,\gamma)},{s_{q}}_{(H,\gamma)},{l_{q}}_{(H,\gamma)})

in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
. Concretely, the base and the total objects of
\Gamma_{\mathbf{q}}(H,\gamma)
are the lax tangent morphisms

\displaystyle(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\xrightarrow{(H,\gamma)}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})\xrightarrow{(G,\beta)}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime})

\displaystyle(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\xrightarrow{(H,\gamma)}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})\xrightarrow{(K,\theta)}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime})

respectively, while the zero, the sum, and the vertical lift of
\Gamma_{\mathbf{q}}(H,\gamma)
are the natural transformations:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(H,\gamma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(K,\theta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G,\beta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(H,\gamma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G,\beta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(K,\theta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{q}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(H,\gamma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(K,\theta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(K,\theta)_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{q}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(H,\gamma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(K,\theta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(K,\theta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l_{q}}

As observed for vector fields and differential bundles, this construction is functorial and extends to a tangent morphism.

Lemma 7.8
.

A differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon(K,\theta)\to(G,\beta)
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
induces a lax tangent morphism

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathbf{q}}\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]\to\mathsf{DB}_{\mathsf{pt}}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]

for each tangentad
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
. Furthermore,
\Gamma_{\mathbf{q}}
is natural in
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
and strong (strict) when both
(G,\beta)
and
(K,\theta)
are strong (strict).

Proof.

The proof of this lemma is fairly similar to that of Lemma
5.3
.
∎

In particular, by Lemma
7.8
, the differential bundle
\mathcal{Q}\colon\mathsf{Tot}\to\mathsf{Base}
of Proposition
7.7
induces a strict tangent natural transformation

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{Q}}\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]\to\mathsf{DB}_{\mathsf{pt}}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

natural in
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
.

We want to prove that
\Gamma_{\mathcal{Q}}
is invertible. Consider another tangent category
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
together with a pointwise display differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon(K,\theta)\to(G,\beta)
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
. For every
A\in\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
, the tuple

\displaystyle(q_{A}\colon KA\to GA,{z_{q}}_{A}\colon GA\to KA,{s_{q}}_{A}\colon K_{2}A\to KA,{l_{q}}_{A}\colon KA\to\mathrm{T}KA)

is a display differential bundle in
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. Therefore, we can define a functor

\displaystyle\Lambda[G,K;q,z_{q},s_{q},l_{q}]\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

which sends an object
A
of
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
to the differential bundle
\mathbf{q}_{A}\colon KA\to GA
, and a morphism
f\colon A\to B
to
(Gf,Kf)\colon\mathbf{q}_{A}\to\mathbf{q}_{B}
. By the naturality of
q
,
z_{q}
,
s_{q}
, and
l_{q}
,

\displaystyle(Gf,Kf)\colon(q_{A},{z_{q}}_{A},{s_{q}}_{A},{l_{q}}_{A})\to(q_{B},{z_{q}}_{B},{s_{q}}_{B},{l_{q}}_{B})

becomes a linear morphism of differential bundles of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. Furthermore,
\Lambda[\mathbf{q}]
comes with a distributive law

\displaystyle\Lambda[\beta,\theta]\colon\Lambda[G,K;q,z_{q},s_{q},l_{q}](\mathrm{T}^{\prime}A)=(q_{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}A},{z_{q}}_{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}A},{s_{q}}_{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}A},{l_{q}}_{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}A})\xrightarrow{(\beta_{A},\theta_{A})}(\mathrm{T}q_{A},\mathrm{T}{z_{q}}_{A},\mathrm{T}{s_{q}}_{A},\mathrm{T}{l_{q}}_{A})=

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{DB}}(\Lambda[G,K;q,z_{q},s_{q},l_{q}](A))

which is a morphism of differential bundles since each of the structural morphisms
q
,
z_{q}
,
s_{q}
, and
l_{q}
are tangent natural transformations and thus compatible with the distributive laws
\beta
and
\theta
.

Lemma 7.9
.

Given two lax tangent morphisms
(G,\beta),(K,\theta)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and a pointwise display differential bundle structure
\mathbf{q}\colon(K,\theta)\to(G,\beta)
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
, the functor
\Lambda[G,K;q,z_{q},s_{q},l_{q}]
together with the distributive law
\Lambda[\beta,\kappa]
defines a lax tangent morphism:

\displaystyle\Lambda[\mathbf{q}]\colon(\Lambda[G,K;q,z_{q},s_{q},l_{q}],\Lambda[\beta,\theta])\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

Proof.

In the previous discussion, we defined
\Lambda[\mathbf{q}]
as the lax tangent morphism which picks out the differential bundle
(q_{A}\colon KA\to GA,{z_{q}}_{A},{s_{q}}_{A},{l_{q}}_{A})
for each
A\in\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
and whose distributive law is induced by
\beta
and
\theta
. In particular, the naturality of the structure morphisms
q
,
z_{q}

s_{q}
, and
l_{q}
makes the functor
\Lambda[G,K;q,z_{q},s_{q},l_{q}]
well-defined, the compatibility of the structure morphisms,
\beta
and
\theta
makes
\Lambda[\beta,\theta]
into a morphism of differential bundles, and the compatibility of
\beta
and
\theta
with the tangent structures makes
\Lambda[\mathbf{q}]
into a lax tangent morphism.
∎

We can now prove the universal property of differential bundles, which is the main result of this section.

Theorem 7.10
.

The pointwise display differential bundle
\mathcal{Q}\colon\mathsf{Tot}\to\mathsf{Base}
of Proposition
7.7
is universal. Concretely, the induced strict tangent natural transformation

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{Q}}\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]\to\mathsf{DB}_{\mathsf{pt}}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

makes the functor

\displaystyle\mathsf{TNGCAT}^{\mathsf{op}}\xrightarrow{[-\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]}\mathsf{TNGCAT}^{\mathsf{op}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{DB}_{\mathsf{pt}}^{\mathsf{op}}}\mathsf{TNGCAT}^{\mathsf{op}}

which sends a tangent category
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
to the tangent category
\mathsf{DB}_{\mathsf{pt}}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
, into a corepresentable functor. In particular,
(\Gamma_{\mathcal{Q}}
is invertible.

Proof.

The proof of this theorem is fairly similar to that of Theorems
5.5
and
6.7
.
∎

Theorem
7.10
establishes the correct universal property of the differential bundles construction. Thanks to this result, we can finally introduce the notion of differential bundles in the formal context of tangentads.

Definition 7.11
.

A tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
in a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}

admits the construction of display differential bundles
if there exists a tangentad
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
{\mathbf{K}}
together with two strict tangent morphisms
\mathsf{Base},\mathsf{Tot}\colon\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and a pointwise display differential bundle
\mathcal{Q}\colon\mathsf{Tot}\to\mathsf{Base}
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
such that the induced tangent natural transformation
\Gamma_{\mathcal{Q}}
of Lemma
7.8
is invertible. The pointwise display differential bundle
\mathcal{Q}\colon\mathsf{Tot}\to\mathsf{Base}
is called the
universal
(
pointwise
)
display differential bundle
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is called the
tangentad of display differential bundles
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Definition 7.12
.

A
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}

admits the construction of display differential bundles
provided that every tangentad of
{\mathbf{K}}
admits the construction of display differential bundles.

We can now rephrase Theorem
7.10
as follows.

Corollary 7.13
.

The
2
-category
\mathsf{CAT}
of categories admits the construction of display differential bundles and the tangentad of display differential bundles of a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
\mathsf{CAT}
is the tangent category
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of display differential bundles of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

To ensure that Definitions
7.11
and
7.12
are well-posed, one requires the tangentad of display differential bundles of a given tangentad to be unique. The next proposition establishes that such a construction is defined uniquely up to a unique isomorphism.

Proposition 7.14
.

If a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
admits the construction of display differential bundles, the tangentad of display differential bundles
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is unique up to a unique isomorphism which extends to an isomorphism of the corresponding universal display differential bundles of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Proof.

The proof of this proposition is fairly similar to that of Proposition
5.9
.
∎

7.3
The formal structures of differential bundles

As proved in Lemma
7.4
, there is a
2
-functor

\displaystyle\mathsf{DB}\colon\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\mathscr{D}}\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\cong}

which sends a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
to the tangent category
\mathsf{DB}(\otimes,\mathbb{T})
of display differential bundles of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

In this section, we show that the construction of display differential bundles is
2
-functorial in the
2
-category of tangentads of
{\mathbf{K}}
.

The functoriality of the construction of differential bundles

Consider a strong tangent morphism
(F,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
between two tangentads of
{\mathbf{K}}
. It is not hard to see that the induced functor

\displaystyle[\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]\xrightarrow{[\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>F,\alpha]}[\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]

is a strong tangent morphism. In order to apply the functor
\mathsf{DB}_{\mathsf{pt}}
to
[\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>F,\alpha]
,
[\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>F,\alpha]
must preserve pointwise tangent display maps. Let us introduce the classes of tangent morphisms between tangentads which satisfy this condition.

Definition 7.15
.

A
display tangent morphism
from a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
to another tangentad
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
consists of a strong tangent morphism
(F,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
such that, for every tangentad
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
, the induced strong tangent morphism

\displaystyle[\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]\xrightarrow{[\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}\>|\>F,\alpha]}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]

is a display tangent morphism.

Tangentads, display tangent morphisms, and tangent
2
-morphisms of a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
form a
2
-category denoted by
\mathsf{TNG}_{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbf{K}})
.

Proposition 7.16
.

There is a
2
-functor

\displaystyle\mathsf{DB}\colon\mathsf{TNG}_{\mathscr{D}}({\mathbf{K}})\to\mathsf{TNG}_{\cong}({\mathbf{K}})

which sends a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
{\mathbf{K}}
to the tangentad of display differential bundles
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. In particular, for each display tangent morphism
(F,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
, the tangent morphism
\mathsf{DB}(F,\alpha)\colon\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
is strong.

Proof.

The proof is fairly similar to the proofs of Propositions
5.10
and
6.12
. Thus, we leave it to the reader to spell out the details.
∎

7.4
Differential objects vs differential bundles

[
8
, Proposition 3.4]
establishes an equivalence between differential objects in a Cartesian tangent category and differential bundles over the terminal object. In this section, we extend this result to the formal theory of tangentads.

For starters, consider a Cartesian tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}
with finite
2
-products and suppose
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
admits the construction of display differential bundles. Let
\mathcal{Q}\colon\mathsf{Tot}\to\mathsf{Base}
denote the universal pointwise display differential bundle of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
the tangentad of display differential bundles of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. To
restrict

\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
to those differential bundles whose base object is the terminal object, we need to consider the
2
-pullback of
\mathsf{Base}\colon\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
along
\ast\colon\mathds{1}\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
in
{\mathbf{K}}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathds{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Base}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ast}

(7.1)

Notice that, by Proposition
4.7
, the diagram of Equation (
7.1
), becomes a
2
-pullback in
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
.
Let
\mathsf{Inc}\colon\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
denote the inclusion of the
2
-pullback
\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
\mathsf{Base}
along
\ast
to
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. We want to prove that
\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
carries the structure of the universal differential object of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. Firstly, consider the tangent morphism

\displaystyle[\mathsf{Inc}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]\colon[\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]\to[\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

which precomposes a lax tangent morphism from
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
to
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
with
\mathsf{Inc}
. By Proposition
3.1
, this is a strict tangent morphism thus, by Lemma
7.4
, we can apply the
2
-functor
\mathsf{DB}_{\mathsf{pt}}
to it:

\displaystyle\mathsf{DB}_{\mathsf{pt}}[\mathsf{Inc}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]\colon\mathsf{DB}_{\mathsf{pt}}[\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]\to\mathsf{DB}_{\mathsf{pt}}[\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

In particular, this functor sends the universal pointwise differential bundle
\mathcal{Q}
to a pointwise display differential bundle
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathsf{Inc}}
. However, the base object of
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathsf{Inc}}
corresponds to the tangent morphism
\mathsf{Base}\circ\mathsf{Inc}=\ast\>\circ\>!=\top
, which is the terminal object of
[\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
. Thus,
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathsf{Inc}}
is a differential bundle on the terminal object. By
[
8
, Proposition 3.4]
,
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathsf{Inc}}
is then a differential object in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
. The goal is to prove that
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathsf{Inc}}
is the universal differential object of
[\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
.

Recall that every differential object
(G,\beta;\zeta,\sigma,\hat{p})
of
[\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
induces a functor

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(G,\beta;\zeta,\sigma,\hat{p})}\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]\to\mathsf{DO}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

which simply postcomposes by
(G,\beta;\zeta,\sigma,\hat{p})
. So, in particular, the differential object
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathsf{Inc}}
of
[\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
induces a functor

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{Q}_{\mathsf{Inc}}}\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]\to\mathsf{DO}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]\cong\mathsf{DB}_{\mathsf{pt}}|_{\mathds{1}}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

where
\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
denotes the tangent category of pointwise display differential bundles of
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
over the terminal object
\top
. Consider the following commutative diagram of tangent categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{DB}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{\mathcal{Q}_{\mathsf{Inc}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{Inc}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{\mathcal{Q}}}

(7.2)

The next lemma shows that this diagram is a pullback in the category
\mathsf{CAT}
of categories.

Lemma 7.17
.

The diagram of Equation (
7.2
) is a pullback in
\mathsf{CAT}
.

Proof.

Consider a category
\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime}
and two functors
A\colon\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime}\to[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]
and
B\colon\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
such that the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{DB}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{\mathcal{Q}_{\mathsf{Inc}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{Inc}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{\mathcal{Q}}}

Consider an object
X
of
\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime}
. The functor
A
sends
X
to a lax tangent morphism

\displaystyle A(X)\>\colon\!=(H_{X},\gamma_{X})\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

such that, when postcomposed by the structural
2
-morphisms of the universal pointwise display differential bundle
\mathcal{Q}
, namely, by applying
\Gamma_{\mathcal{Q}}
to
(H_{X},\gamma_{X})
, coincides with the display differential bundle
B(X)
, whose base object is the terminal object
\top
of
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
. In particular,
\mathsf{Base}\circ(H_{X},\gamma_{X})=\top=\ast\>\circ\>!
. However, from the universal property of the
2
-pullback of Equation (
7.1
), we obtain a unique tangent morphism
C(X)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
which makes the following diagram to commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathds{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(H_{X},\gamma_{X})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces!}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Base}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ast}

Consider now a morphism
f\colon X\to Y
of
\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime}
and the corresponding tangent
2
-morphism

\displaystyle A(f)\>\colon\!=\varphi_{f}\colon(H_{X},\gamma_{X})\Rightarrow(H_{Y},\gamma_{Y})

By the universal property of the
2
-pullback of Equation (
7.1
), we obtain a unique
2
-morphism
C(f)\colon C(X)\Rightarrow C(Y)
, satisfying the following equation:

\displaystyle\mathsf{Inc}(C(f))=\varphi_{f}\colon\mathsf{Inc}(C(X))=(H_{X},\gamma_{X})\to(H_{Y},\gamma_{Y})=\mathsf{Inc}(C(Y))

Let us define a functor

\displaystyle C\colon\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime}\to[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]

which sends each
X
to
C(X)
and each
f\colon X\to Y
to
C(f)
. By using the universal property of the
2
-pullback of Equation (
7.1
) one can show that
C
is a functor. Moreover, by postcomposing each
C(X)
by
\mathsf{Inc}
, we obtain back
(H_{X},\gamma_{X})
, namely,
A(X)
, and since
\Gamma_{\mathcal{Q}_{\mathsf{Inc}}}(H_{X},\gamma_{X})
must coincide with
\mathsf{Inc}(B(X))
, it is easy to prove that
\Gamma_{\mathcal{Q}_{\mathsf{Inc}}}(C(X))
is equal to
B(X)
. Thus,
C
is the unique functor which makes the following diagram commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{DB}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{\mathcal{Q}_{\mathsf{Inc}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{Inc}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{\mathcal{Q}}}

This proves the statement of the lemma.
∎

We can now prove the main result of this section.

Theorem 7.18
.

Let
{\mathbf{K}}
be a Cartesian
2
-category and
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
be a Cartesian tangentad of
{\mathbf{K}}
. If
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
admits the construction of display differential bundles and the
2
-pullback of Equation (
7.1
) exists in
\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})
,
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
admits the construction of differential objects and the tangentad of differential objects of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is
\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Proof.

Since isomorphisms are stable under pullbacks and
\Gamma_{\mathcal{Q}}
is an isomorphism of tangent categories, by Lemma
7.17
, also
\Gamma_{\mathcal{Q}_{\mathsf{Inc}}}
is an isomorphism of categories. However, since
\Gamma_{\mathcal{Q}_{\mathsf{Inc}}}
is a strict tangent morphism, its inverse is also a strict tangent morphism. Thus,
\Gamma_{\mathcal{Q}_{\mathsf{Inc}}}
becomes an isomorphism of tangent categories. By
[
8
, Proposition 3.4]
, every differential bundle on the terminal object is a differential object, thus,
\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
is isomorphic to
\mathsf{DO}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
. Thus, we obtain an isomorphism of tangent categories:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{Q}_{\mathsf{Inc}}}\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]\cong\mathsf{DO}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

Therefore,
\mathsf{DB}|_{\mathds{1}}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is the tangentad of differential objects of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.
∎

7.5
Applications

In Section
2.5
, we listed some examples of tangentads, and in Sections
5.5
and
6.4
, we computed the constructions of vector fields and of differential objects for tangent monads, tangent fibrations, tangent indexed categories, tangent split restriction categories, and showed how to extend these constructions to tangent restriction categories. In this section, we compute the construction of differential bundles for the same examples of tangentads and show how to extend it to tangent restriction categories. Since the proofs are fairly similar to those for differential objects, we provide the final constructions without giving the details of the proofs.

Tangent monads

Let us start by considering tangent monads. We previously discussed that strong tangent morphisms lift to the tangent categories of differential bundles. It is not hard to convince ourselves that every strong tangent monad
(S,\alpha)
on a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, namely, a tangent monad whose underlying tangent morphism is strong, lifts to
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
as a tangent monad
\mathsf{DB}(S,\alpha)
.

Theorem 7.19
.

In the
2
-category
\mathsf{TNGMND}
of tangent monads, each strong tangent monad
(S,\alpha)
on a tangent category admits the construction of differential bundles. Moreover, the tangentad of differential bundles of
(S,\alpha)
is the tangent monad
\mathsf{DB}(S,\alpha)
on
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Tangent fibrations

Since the underlying functor
\Pi\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of a tangent fibration is a strict tangent morphism, hence strong, it lifts to a strict tangent morphism
\mathsf{DB}(\Pi)\colon\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. However, since
\Pi
is a tangent fibration, one can show that
\mathsf{DB}(\Pi)
is also a tangent fibration.

Lemma 7.20
.

Consider a (cloven) tangent fibration
\Pi\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
between two tangent categories. The strict tangent morphism

\displaystyle\mathsf{DB}(\Pi)\colon\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

which sends a differential bundle
\mathbf{q}^{\prime}\colon E^{\prime}\to M^{\prime}
to the differential bundle
(\Pi(E),\Pi(\zeta_{E}),\Pi(\sigma_{E}),\Pi(\hat{p}_{E}))
is a (cloven) tangent fibration.

Theorem 7.21
.

The
2
-category
\mathsf{FIB}
of (cloven) fibrations admits the construction of differential bundles. In particular, the tangentad of differential bundles of a (cloven) tangent fibration
\Pi
is the tangent fibration
\mathsf{DB}(\Pi)\colon\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Tangent indexed categories

In this section, we employ the Grothendieck
2
-equivalence
\mathsf{TNGFIB}\simeq\mathsf{TNGINDX}
to compute the tangentad of differential bundles of a tangent indexed category. Let us start by proving that
2
-equivalences preserve the construction of differential bundles.

Proposition 7.22
.

Let
{\mathbf{K}}
and
{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}
be two
2
-categories and suppose there is a
2
-equivalence
\Xi\colon\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})\simeq\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}}^{\prime})
of the
2
-categories of tangentads of
{\mathbf{K}}
and
{\mathbf{K}}^{\prime}
. If a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
{\mathbf{K}}
admits the construction of differential bundles and
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
denotes the tangentad of differential bundles of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, so does
\Xi(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and the tangentad of differential bundles of
\Xi(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is
\Xi(\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}))
.

Proposition
7.22
together with the Grothendieck
2
-equivalence
\mathsf{TNGFIB}\cong\mathsf{TNGINDX}
allows us to compute the differential bundles of tangent indexed categories. Consider a tangent indexed category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};\mathscr{I},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
whose underlying indexed category
\mathscr{I}\colon\mathbb{X}^{\mathsf{op}}\to\mathsf{CAT}
sends an object
M
of
\mathbb{X}
to the category
\mathbb{X}^{M}
and a morphism
f\colon M\to N
to the functor
f^{\ast}\colon\mathbb{X}^{N}\to\mathbb{X}^{M}
. Define
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};\mathscr{I},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
as follows:

are pairs

and

Theorem 7.23
.

The
2
-category
\mathsf{INDX}
of indexed categories admits the construction of differential bundles. In particular, given a tangent indexed category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};\mathscr{I},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
, the tangent indexed category of differential bundles of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};\mathscr{I},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
is the tangent indexed category
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T};\mathscr{I},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})=(\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}),\mathsf{DB}(\mathscr{I}),\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{T}^{\prime}))
.

Tangent split restriction categories

In this section, we consider the construction of differential bundles for tangent split restriction categories.

Lemma 7.24
.

Consider a tangent split restriction category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and let
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
be the category whose objects are differential bundles
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
in the tangent subcategory
\mathsf{Tot}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and morphisms are morphisms
(f,g)
of differential bundles such that also their restriction idempotents
(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muf\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu,\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mug\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
are also morphisms of differential bundles. The tangent split restriction structure on
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
lifts to
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. In particular, the tangent bundle functor sends each differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
to
\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{DB}}\mathbf{q}
, where

\displaystyle l_{\mathrm{T}q}\colon\mathrm{T}E\xrightarrow{l_{q}}\mathrm{T}^{2}E\xrightarrow{c}\mathrm{T}^{2}E

Definition 7.25
.

A
restriction differential bundle
in a Cartesian tangent split restriction category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is an object of
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Concretely, a restriction differential bundle consists of two objects
M
and
E
and four total maps

\displaystyle q

\displaystyle\colon E\to M

\displaystyle z_{q}

\displaystyle\colon M\to E

\displaystyle s_{q}

\displaystyle\colon E_{2}\to E

\displaystyle l_{q}

\displaystyle\colon E\to\mathrm{T}E

where
E_{n}
is the restriction
n
-fold pullback of
q
along itself:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}

The total maps
q
,
z_{q}
,
s_{q}
,
l_{q}
satisfy the same equational axioms of a differential bundle in ordinary tangent category theory. However, the universality of the vertical lift is replaced with the following axiom: the following is a restriction pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z}

whose universal property is preserved by the tangent bundle functor, and where
\xi_{q}
is the morphism:

\displaystyle\xi_{q}

\displaystyle\colon E_{2}\xrightarrow{\langle\pi_{1}l_{q},\pi_{2}z\rangle}\mathrm{T}E_{2}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}s_{q}}\mathrm{T}E

Theorem 7.26
.

The
2
-category
\mathsf{SRSTR}
of split restriction categories admits the construction of differential bundles. In particular, the tangentad of differential bundles of a Cartesian tangent split restriction category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is the Cartesian tangent split restriction category
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Tangent restriction categories: a general approach

To extend the construction of differential bundles to tangent restriction categories, we adopt a similar technique to the one used to extend the constructions of vector fields and differential objects, namely, via a suitable
2
-pullback. The main difference with Sections
5.5
and
6.4
is the ambient
2
-category where we need to consider the
2
-pullback.

The issue is that the construction of differential bundles involves not one but two forgetful
1
-morphisms
\mathsf{Base},\mathsf{Tot}\colon\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
which project a differential bundle in
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
to its base and its total spaces, respectively. The purpose of the
2
-pullbacks of Equations (
5.2
) and (
6.1
) was to restrict to those restriction vector fields and restriction differential objects in
\mathsf{Split}_{R}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
whose underlying object was coming directly from
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
via the unit
\eta
. For differential bundles, we need to restrict to those restriction differential bundles
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
of
\mathsf{Split}_{R}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
whose base and total objects
M
and
E
come directly from objects of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
via
\eta
.

To solve this problem, we consider the
2
-category of parallel
1
-morphisms.

Definition 7.27
.

The
2
-category of
n
parallel morphisms
of a
2
-category
{\mathbf{J}}
is the
2
-category
\mathsf{Prll}_{n}({\mathbf{J}})
whose objects are the objects of
{\mathbf{J}}
, a
1
-morphism from an object
\mathbb{X}
to another object
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
consists of a
n
-tuple
(f_{1},\dots,f_{n})
of parallel
1
-morphisms
f_{1},\dots,f_{n}\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
of
{\mathbf{J}}
, and a
2
-morphism
(\varphi_{1},\dots,\varphi_{n})
from a
1
-morphism
(f_{1},\dots,f_{n})
to a morphism
(g_{1},\dots,g_{n})
consists of a
n
-tuple
\varphi_{1}\colon f_{1}\Rightarrow g_{1},\dots,\varphi_{n}\colon f_{n}\Rightarrow g_{n}
of
2
-morphisms of
{\mathbf{J}}
.

Consider a pullback-extension context, namely, two
2
-categories
{\mathbf{K}}
and
{\mathbf{J}}
together with two
2
-functors

\displaystyle\Xi\colon{\mathbf{J}}\leftrightarrows\mathsf{TNG}({\mathbf{K}})\colon\mathsf{Inc}

together with a natural
2
-transformation

\displaystyle\eta_{\mathbb{X}}

\displaystyle\colon\mathbb{X}\to\mathsf{Inc}(\Xi(\mathbb{X}))

natural in
\mathbb{X}\in{\mathbf{J}}
. Let us also consider an object
\mathbb{X}
of
{\mathbf{J}}
such that the tangentad
\Xi(\mathbb{X})
of
{\mathbf{K}}
admits the construction of differential bundles. Finally, let us assume the existence of the following
2
-pullback in
\mathsf{Prll}_{2}({\mathbf{J}})
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{Inc}(\mathsf{DB}(\Xi(\mathbb{X}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{Inc}(\Xi(\mathbb{X}))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{DB}(\eta,\eta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{Base}_{\mathbb{X}},\mathsf{Tot}_{\mathbb{X}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{Inc}(\mathsf{Base}_{\Xi(\mathbb{X})}),\mathsf{Inc}(\mathsf{Tot}_{\Xi(\mathbb{X})}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\eta,\eta)}

(7.3)

Definition 7.28
.

Let
({\mathbf{K}},{\mathbf{J}};\mathsf{Inc},\Xi;\eta)
be a pullback-extension context. An object
\mathbb{X}
of
{\mathbf{J}}
admits the
extended construction of differential bundles
(w.r.t. to the pullback-extension context) if
\Xi(\mathbb{X})
admits the construction of differential bundles in
{\mathbf{K}}
, and the
2
-pullback diagram of Equation (
7.3
) exists in
\mathsf{Prll}_{2}({\mathbf{J}})
. In this scenario, the
object of differential bundles
(w.r.t. to the pullback-extension context) of
\mathbb{X}
is the object
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X})
of
{\mathbf{J}}
, namely, the
2
-pullback of
(\mathsf{Inc}(\mathsf{Base}_{\Xi(\mathbb{X})}),\mathsf{Inc}(\mathsf{Tot}_{\Xi(\mathbb{X})}))
along
(\eta,\eta)
.

Consider a generic tangent restriction category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and let us unwrap the definition of
\mathsf{DB}(\mathsf{Split}_{R}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}))
. The objects of
\mathsf{DB}(\mathsf{Split}_{R}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}))
are tuples
(E,M,q,z_{q},s_{q},l_{q})
formed by two objects
M
and
E
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
together with four morphisms

\displaystyle q

\displaystyle\colon E\to M

\displaystyle z_{q}

\displaystyle\colon M\to E

\displaystyle s_{q}

\displaystyle\colon E_{2}\to E

\displaystyle l_{q}

\displaystyle\colon E\to\mathrm{T}E

such that:

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muz\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{q}\circ q=q

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muq\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\circ z_{q}=z_{q}

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mus_{q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muq\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\times_{M}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muq\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muq\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\circ s_{q}=s_{q}

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mul_{q}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muq\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu

\displaystyle\mathrm{T}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muq\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\circ l_{q}=l_{q}

Moreover,
\zeta_{A}
,
\sigma_{A}
,
\hat{p}_{A}
satisfy the axioms of a restriction differential bundle.

A morphism
(f,g)\colon(\mathbf{q}_{1}\colon E_{1}\to M_{1})\to(\mathbf{q}_{2}\colon E_{2}\to M_{2})
of
\mathsf{DB}(\mathsf{Split}_{R}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}))
consists of a pair of morphisms
f\colon M_{1}\to M_{2}
and
g\colon E_{1}\to E_{2}
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
which commutes with the structural morphism of the differential bundles and with their restriction idempotents. The tangent bundle functor sends each
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
to
\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{DB}}\mathbf{q}\colon\mathrm{T}E\to\mathrm{T}M
.

Now, consider a tangent (non-necessarily split) restriction category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and define
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
to be the full subcategory of
\mathsf{DB}(\mathsf{Split}_{R}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}))
spanned by the objects
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
where
q
,
z_{q}
,
s_{q}
, and
l_{q}
are total maps in
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, which is precisely when
q
and
z_{q}
are total.

Lemma 7.29
.

Let
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
be a tangent restriction category. The subcategory
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
\mathsf{DB}(\mathsf{Split}_{R}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}))
spanned by the objects
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
where
q
and
z_{q}
are total in
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is a tangent restriction category.

We can finally prove the main theorem of this section.

Theorem 7.30
.

Consider the pullback-extension context
(\mathsf{RSTR},\mathsf{TNGRSTR};\mathsf{Inc},\mathsf{Split}_{R};\eta)
of tangent restriction categories. The
2
-category
\mathsf{TNGRSTR}
admits the extended construction of differential bundles with respect to this pullback-extension context. Moreover, the object of differential bundles of a tangent restriction category is the tangent restriction category
\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

8
The formal theory of connections

The notion of connections on linear connections, introduced in
[
7
]
, extends the well-studied theory of connections on vector bundles, which is a key geometric concept of differential geometry and (pseudo-)Riemannian geometry. In particular, notions like covariant derivative, curvature, torsion, and parallel transport of a connection have been successfully extended to tangent category theory. In this section, we extend this theory to the context of tangentads.

For starters, we recall the definition of a connection on a linear connection in a tangent category and recall the construction of the tangent categories of connections. In Sections
8.2
and
8.3
, we identify the correct universal property enjoyed by the tangent categories of connections. Having in mind the goal to extend the notion of a covariant derivative of a connection, in Section
8.4
, we identify the correct universal property which classifies sections of differential bundles with a connection. Finally, in Section
8.5
, we discuss the functoriality of the constructions and formally construct the covariant derivative and the curvature tensor of a connection, and the torsion tensor for affine connections.

8.1
Connections in tangent category theory

A connection on a linear connection is composed by two parts: a vertical and a horizontal connection. Let us first recall the notion of a vertical connection

Definition 8.1
.

Given a display linear connection
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
of a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, a
vertical linear connection
on a linear connection consists of a morphism
k\colon\mathrm{T}E\to E
, satisfying the following conditions:

(a)

k
is a retract of the vertical lift
l_{q}\colon E\to\mathrm{T}E
, that is:

\displaystyle l_{q}k=\mathsf{id}_{E}

(b)

Linearity (1):

\displaystyle(p,k)\colon\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{LC}}\mathbf{q}\to\mathbf{q}

is a linear morphism of linear connections;

(c)

Linearity (2):

\displaystyle(q,k)\colon\mathbf{p}_{E}\to\mathbf{q}

is a linear morphism of linear connections.

When the linear connection
\mathbf{q}
is the tangent bundle
\mathbf{p}_{M}
,
k
is called an
vertical affine connection
on
M
.

To introduce horizontal connections, recall that the horizontal bundle of a tangent display map
q\colon E\to M
is the pullback of
q
along the tangent bundle projection
p\colon\mathrm{T}M\to M
, namely:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{H}q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

Since
\pi_{2}\colon\mathsf{H}q\to E
is the pullback of a linear connection (the tangent bundle),
\pi_{2}
becomes a linear connection, which is called the
horizontal bundle
of
q
.

Definition 8.2
.

Given a display linear connection
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
of a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, a
horizontal linear connection
on a linear connection consists of a morphism
h\colon\mathrm{T}E\to\mathsf{H}q
, satisfying the following conditions:

(a)

h
is a section of
\langle\mathrm{T}q,p\rangle\colon\mathrm{T}E\to\mathrm{T}M\times_{M}E=\mathsf{H}q
:

\displaystyle h\langle\mathrm{T}q,p\rangle=\mathsf{id}_{\mathsf{H}q}

(b)

Linearity (1):

\displaystyle(\mathsf{id}_{E},h)\colon\pi_{2}\to\mathbf{p}_{E}

is a linear morphism of linear connections, where
\pi_{2}\colon\mathsf{H}q\to E
denotes the horizontal bundle of
q
;

(c)

Linearity (2):

\displaystyle(\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{T}M},h)\colon\pi_{2}\to\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{LC}}\mathbf{q}

is a linear morphism of linear connections, where
\pi_{2}\colon\mathsf{H}q\to E
denotes the horizontal bundle of
q
.

When the linear connection
\mathbf{q}
is the tangent bundle
\mathbf{p}_{M}
,
h
is called an
horizontal affine connection
on
M
.

Definition 8.3
.

A
linear connection
on a display linear connection
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
consists of a vertical linear connection
k\colon E\to\mathrm{T}E
together with a horizontal linear connection
h\colon\mathrm{T}E\to\mathsf{H}q
satisfying the following two conditions:

•

The vertical and the horizontal components are orthogonal:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{H}q}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z_{q}}

•

The vertical and the horizontal components are in direct sum to each other:

\displaystyle\langle k,p\rangle(z\times l)\mathrm{T}s_{q}+\langle\mathrm{T}q,p\rangle h=\mathsf{id}_{\mathrm{T}E}

When the linear connection
\mathbf{q}
is the tangent bundle
\mathbf{p}_{M}
, a linear connection
(k,h)
is called an
affine connection
.

As shown by Cockett and Cruttwell, a (vertical) linear connection on a linear connection comes with a notion of a covariant derivative.

Definition 8.4
.

The
covariant derivative
of a vertical linear connection
k
of a linear connection
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
consists of a combinator
\nabla^{k}
which sends a vector field
v\colon M\to\mathrm{T}M
of
M
and a section
s\colon M\to E
of
q
to a section
\nabla^{k}_{v}s
of
q
so defined:

\displaystyle\nabla^{k}_{v}s\colon M\xrightarrow{v}\mathrm{T}M\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}s}\mathrm{T}E\xrightarrow{k}E

Furthermore, when the tangent category has negatives, a (vertical) linear connection on a linear connection comes with a notion of curvature.

Definition 8.5
.

The
curvature

\rho^{k}
of a vertical linear connection on a linear connection
k
of a linear connection
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
is the morphism so defined:

\displaystyle\rho^{k}\>\colon\!=k\circ\mathrm{T}k-k\circ\mathrm{T}k\circ c\colon\mathrm{T}^{2}E\to E

Furthermore, the
curvature tensor
of
k
is the combinator
\mathsf{R}^{k}
which sends two vector fields
u,v\colon M\to\mathrm{T}M
of
M
and a section
s\colon M\to E
of
q
to the section
\mathsf{R}^{k}_{u,v}s
of
q
so defined:

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}^{k}_{u,v}s\colon M\xrightarrow{v}\mathrm{T}M\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}u}\mathrm{T}^{2}M\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}^{2}s}\mathrm{T}^{2}E\xrightarrow{\rho^{k}}E

When the ambient tangent category has negatives, (vertical) affine connections carry a notion of torsion.

Definition 8.6
.

The
torsion
of a vertical affine connection
k
on an object
M
is the morphism so defined:

\displaystyle\tau^{k}\>\colon\!=ck-k\colon\mathrm{T}^{2}M\to\mathrm{T}M

Furthermore, the
torsion tensor
of
k
is the combinator
\mathsf{W}^{k}
which sends two vector fields
u,v\colon M\to\mathrm{T}M
of
M
to the vector field
\mathsf{W}^{k}_{u}v
of
M
so defined:

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{k}_{u}v\colon M\xrightarrow{v}\mathrm{T}M\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}u}\mathrm{T}^{2}M\xrightarrow{\tau^{k}}\mathrm{T}M

Vertical linear connections in a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
form a tangent category denoted by
\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. Concretely,
\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is the tangent category so defined:

​
[
objects
]
:

An object of
\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is a pair
(\mathbf{q},k)
formed by a linear connection
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
together with a vertical linear connection
k\colon\mathrm{T}E\to E
on
\mathbf{q}
;

​
[
morphisms
]
:

A morphism of
\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
from
(\mathbf{q},k)
to
(\mathbf{q}^{\prime},k^{\prime})
consists of a linear morphism
(f,g)\colon\mathbf{q}\to\mathbf{q}^{\prime}
of linear connections, satisfying the following coherence with the vertical connections:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

​
[
tangent bundle functor
]
:

The tangent bundle functor
\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VLC}}\colon\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
sends a pair
(\mathbf{q},k)
to
\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{LC}}\mathbf{q},k_{\mathrm{T}})
, where

\displaystyle k_{\mathrm{T}}\colon\mathrm{T}^{2}E\xrightarrow{c}\mathrm{T}^{2}E\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}k}\mathrm{T}E

and a morphism
(f,g)\colon(\mathbf{q},k)\to(\mathbf{q}^{\prime},k^{\prime})
to
(\mathrm{T}f,\mathrm{T}g)
;

​
[
structural natural transformations
]
:

The structural natural transformations of
\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
are the same as the ones of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Similarly, horizontal linear connections in a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
form a tangent category
\mathsf{HLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, defined as follows:

​
[
objects
]
:

An object of
\mathsf{HLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is a pair
(\mathbf{q},h)
formed by a display linear connection
\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M
together with a horizontal linear connection
h\colon\mathsf{H}\mathbf{q}\to\mathrm{T}E
on
\mathbf{q}
;

​
[
morphisms
]
:

A morphism of
\mathsf{HLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
from
(\mathbf{q},h)
to
(\mathbf{q}^{\prime},h^{\prime})
consists of a linear morphism
(f,g)\colon\mathbf{q}\to\mathbf{q}^{\prime}
of linear connections, satisfying the following coherence with the horizontal connections:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{H}q}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{H}q^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}E^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Hg}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{\prime}}

​
[
tangent bundle functor
]
:

The tangent bundle functor
\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{HLC}}\colon\mathsf{HLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{HLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
sends a pair
(\mathbf{q},h)
to
\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{LC}}\mathbf{q},h_{\mathrm{T}})
where:

\displaystyle h_{\mathrm{T}}\colon H\mathrm{T}E=\mathrm{T}^{2}M\times_{\mathrm{T}M}\mathrm{T}E\xrightarrow{\langle\pi_{1}c,\pi_{2}\rangle}\mathrm{T}(\mathrm{T}M\times_{M}E)=\mathrm{T}\mathsf{H}q\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}h}\mathrm{T}^{2}E

and a morphism
(f,g)\colon(\mathbf{q},k)\to(\mathbf{q}^{\prime},k^{\prime})
to
(\mathrm{T}f,\mathrm{T}g)
;

​
[
structural natural transformations
]
:

The structural natural transformations of
\mathsf{HLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
are the same as the ones of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Linear connections form a tangent category denoted by
\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, whose objects are triples
(\mathbf{q},k,h)
formed by a display linear connection
\mathbf{q}
together with a linear connection
(k,h)
on
\mathbf{q}
, and whose tangent structure acts on the vertical and the horizontal parts as in
\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
\mathsf{HLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, respectively.

Also vertical/horizontal affine connections form tangent categories, denoted by
\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
,
\mathsf{HAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, and
\mathsf{AC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, respectively
[
2
, Theorem 5.16]
. Concretely,
\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is the tangent category so defined:

​
[
objects
]
:

An object of
\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is a pair
(M,k)
formed by an object
M
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
together with a vertical affine connection
k\colon\mathrm{T}^{2}M\to\mathrm{T}M
on
M
;

​
[
morphisms
]
:

A morphism of
\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
from
(M,k)
to
(M^{\prime},k^{\prime})
consists of a morphism
f\colon M\to M^{\prime}
satisfying the following coherence with the vertical affine connections:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{2}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{2}M^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{2}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}f}

​
[
tangent bundle functor
]
:

The tangent bundle functor
\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{VAC}}\colon\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
sends a pair
(M,k)
to
\mathrm{T}M,k_{\mathrm{T}})
, where

\displaystyle k_{\mathrm{T}}\colon\mathrm{T}^{3}M\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}c}\mathrm{T}^{3}M\xrightarrow{c_{\mathrm{T}}}\mathrm{T}^{3}M\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}k}\mathrm{T}^{2}M\xrightarrow{c}\mathrm{T}^{2}M

and a morphism
f\colon(M,k)\to(M^{\prime},k^{\prime})
to
\mathrm{T}f
;

​
[
structural natural transformations
]
:

The structural natural transformations of
\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
are the same as the ones of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Similarly,
\mathsf{HAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
can be described as follows:

​
[
objects
]
:

An object of
\mathsf{HAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is a pair
(\mathbf{q},h)
formed by an object
M
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
together with a horizontal affine connection
h\colon\mathrm{T}_{2}M\to\mathrm{T}^{2}M
on
M
, where
\mathrm{T}_{2}M\to\mathrm{T}M
coincides with the horizontal bundle of
p\colon\mathrm{T}M\to M
;

​
[
morphisms
]
:

A morphism of
\mathsf{HAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
from
(M,h)
to
(M^{\prime},h^{\prime})
consists of a morphism
f\colon M\to M^{\prime}
, satisfying the following coherence with the horizontal connections:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{2}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}_{2}M^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{2}M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{T}^{2}M^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}_{2}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{T}^{2}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{\prime}}

​
[
tangent bundle functor
]
:

The tangent bundle functor
\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{HAC}}\colon\mathsf{HAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{HAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
sends a pair
(M,h)
to
\mathrm{T}M,h_{\mathrm{T}})
where:

\displaystyle h_{\mathrm{T}}\colon\mathrm{T}_{2}\mathrm{T}M\xrightarrow{\langle c\circ{\pi_{1}}_{\mathrm{T}},c\circ{\pi_{2}}_{\mathrm{T}}\rangle}\mathrm{T}\mathrm{T}_{2}M\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}h}\mathrm{T}^{3}M\xrightarrow{c_{\mathrm{T}}}\mathrm{T}^{3}M\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}c}\mathrm{T}^{3}M

and a morphism
f\colon(M,k)\to(M^{\prime},k^{\prime})
to
\mathrm{T}f
;

​
[
structural natural transformations
]
:

The structural natural transformations of
\mathsf{HAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
are the same as the ones of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Finally, affine connections form a tangent category denoted by
\mathsf{AC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, whose objects are triples
(M,k,h)
formed by an object
M
together with an affine connection
(k,h)
on
M
, and whose tangent structure acts on the vertical and the horizontal parts as in
\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
\mathsf{HAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, respectively.

The assignments which send a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
to the corresponding tangent categories of (vertical/horizontal) linear/affine connections are
2
-functorial, as shown by the next lemma.

Lemma 8.7
.

There are six
2
-functors

\displaystyle\mathsf{VLC}

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\mathscr{D}}\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\cong}

\displaystyle\mathsf{VAC}

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\cong}\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\cong}

\displaystyle\mathsf{HLC}

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\mathscr{D}}\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\cong}

\displaystyle\mathsf{HAC}

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\cong}\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\cong}

\displaystyle\mathsf{LC}

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\mathscr{D}}\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\cong}

\displaystyle\mathsf{AC}

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\cong}\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\cong}

on the
2
-category of tangent categories and display-preserving strong tangent morphisms, which send a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
to the tangent categories of (vertical/horizontal) linear/affine connections of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Proof.

In
[
2
, Sections 5 and 6]
, the
2
-functoriality of
\mathsf{AC}
is discussed; the proof of the
2
-functoriality of
\mathsf{VAC}
and
\mathsf{HAC}
is similar. To show the
2
-functoriality of
\mathsf{VLC}
,
\mathsf{HLC}
, and
\mathsf{LC}
, first, recall that, thanks to Lemma
8.7
,
\mathsf{LC}\colon\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\mathscr{D}}\to\mathsf{TNGCAT}_{\mathscr{D}}
is
2
-functorial. Consider a display-preserving strong tangent morphism
(F,\alpha)\colon(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
. Thus,
\mathsf{VLC}(F,\alpha)
sends a vertical linear connection
(\mathbf{q}\colon E\to M,k)
to the vertical connection on the display linear connection
\mathsf{LC}(F,\alpha)(\mathbf{q})
so defined:

\displaystyle k_{(F,\alpha)}\mathrm{T}^{\prime}FE\xrightarrow{\alpha^{-1}}F\mathrm{T}E\xrightarrow{Fk}FE

Moreover, given a tangent natural transformation
\varphi\colon(F,\alpha)\to(F^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})
,
\mathsf{VLC}(\varphi)
is the tangent natural transformation:

\displaystyle\mathsf{VLC}(\varphi)_{(\mathbf{q},k)}\colon(\mathsf{LC}(F,\alpha),k_{(F,\alpha)})\xrightarrow{(\varphi_{M}\colon FM\to F^{\prime}M,\varphi_{E}\colon FE\to F^{\prime}E)}(\mathsf{LC}(F^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}),k_{(F^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})})\qed

8.2
The universal property of linear connections

This section aims to characterize the correct universal property of the tangent categories
\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
,
\mathsf{HLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, and
\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of the various flavours of linear connections on linear connections of a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. For starters, notice that, for each of these three tangent categories, there is a strict tangent morphism which sends each object to its underlying display linear connection:

\displaystyle\mathsf{U}

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle\mathsf{U}

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{HLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle\mathsf{U}

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to\mathsf{DB}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

Thus, each of these strict tangent morphisms induces a functor from
\mathsf{DB}_{\mathsf{pt}}[\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
to the respective tangent categories of pointwise display differential bundles
\mathsf{DB}_{\mathsf{pt}}[\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
,
\mathsf{DB}_{\mathsf{pt}}[\mathsf{HLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
, and
\mathsf{DB}_{\mathsf{pt}}[\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
. In particular, the universal pointwise display differential bundle
\mathcal{Q}\colon\mathsf{Tot}\to\mathsf{Base}
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is sent to a display linear connection
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathsf{U}}
on each of the Hom-tangent categories
[\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
,
[\mathsf{HLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
, and
[\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
, denoted by

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}}\colon\mathsf{Tot}^{\mathsf{VLC}}\to\mathsf{Base}^{\mathsf{VLC}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{HLC}}\colon\mathsf{Tot}^{\mathsf{HLC}}\to\mathsf{Base}^{\mathsf{HLC}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{LC}}\colon\mathsf{Tot}^{\mathsf{LC}}\to\mathsf{Base}^{\mathsf{LC}}

respectively.

Now, define the following natural transformations

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{VLC}}}_{(\mathbf{q},k)}&\colon\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{Tot}^{\mathsf{VLC}}(\mathbf{q},k)=\mathrm{T}E\xrightarrow{k}E=\mathsf{Tot}^{\mathsf{VLC}}(\mathbf{q},k)\\
\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{HLC}}}_{(\mathbf{q},h)}&\colon(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathsf{H}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{HLC}})(\mathbf{q},h)=\mathsf{H}q\xrightarrow{h}\mathrm{T}E=\mathsf{Tot}^{\mathsf{HLC}}(\mathbf{q},h)\\
\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{LC}}}_{(\mathbf{q};k,h)}&\colon\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{Tot}^{\mathsf{LC}}(\mathbf{q};k,h)=\mathrm{T}E\xrightarrow{k}E=\mathsf{Tot}^{\mathsf{LC}}(\mathbf{q};k,h)\\
