\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{LC}}}_{(\mathbf{q};k,h)}&\colon(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathsf{H}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{LC}})(\mathbf{q};k,h)=\mathsf{H}q\xrightarrow{h}\mathrm{T}E=\mathsf{Tot}^{\mathsf{LC}}(\mathbf{q};k,h)\end{split}

(8.1)

where we denoted by
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathsf{H}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathcal{Q^{\mathsf{HLC}}}
and
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathsf{H}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathcal{Q^{\mathsf{LC}}}
the pointwise horizontal bundles of
\mathcal{Q^{\mathsf{HLC}}}
and
\mathcal{Q^{\mathsf{LC}}}
, respectively, in the corresponding Hom-tangent categories, namely, the pointwise pullbacks of
\mathcal{Q^{\mathsf{HLC}}}
and
\mathcal{Q^{\mathsf{LC}}}
along the projection.

Proposition 8.8
.

The natural transformations of Equation (
8.1
) define a vertical linear connection
\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{VLC}}}
on
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}}
, a horizontal linear connection
\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{HLC}}}
on
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{HLC}}
, and a linear connection
(\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{LC}}},\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{LC}}}
on
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{LC}}
, respectively.

Proof.

We already mentioned that
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}}
,
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{HLC}}
, and
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{LC}}
are pointwise display differential bundles since they are constructed from the universal pointwise display differential bundle. The naturality of
\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{VLC}}}
,
\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{HLC}}}
,
\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{LC}}}
, and
\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{LC}}}
is equivalent to the compatibilities between the morphisms of
\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
,
\mathsf{HLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, and
\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
with the connections. Furthermore, these natural transformations are compatible with the tangent structures by definition of the tangent bundle functors in each respective tangent category. Finally, the axioms required for these transformations to define connections are a direct consequence of each
(\mathbf{q},k)\in\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
,
(\mathbf{q},h)\in\mathsf{HLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, and
(\mathbf{q};k,h)\in\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
being vertical, horizontal, and linear connections in
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.
∎

To characterize the correct universal property enjoyed by the connections of Proposition
8.8
, let us first unwrap the definition of a (vertical/horizontal) linear connection in each Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]
, where
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
are tangent categories. Let us start with a vertical linear connection.

​
[
Differential bundle
]
:

A pointwise display differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon(K,\theta)\to(G,\beta)
;

​
[
Vertical connection
]
:

The vertical linear connection consists of a tangent natural transformation:

\displaystyle k\colon\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(K,\theta)\to(K,\theta)

satisfying the axioms of a vertical linear connection. A morphism of vertical linear connections in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]
from a vertical linear connection
(\mathbf{q}\colon(K,\theta)\to(G,\beta),k)
to a vertical linear connection
(\mathbf{q}^{\prime}\colon(K^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})\to(G^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}),k^{\prime})
consists of a linear morphism of linear connections
(\varphi,\psi)\colon\mathbf{q}\to\mathbf{q}^{\prime}
which commutes with the vertical linear connections.

Similarly, a horizontal linear connection consists of:

​
[
Differential bundle
]
:

A pointwise display differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon(K,\theta)\to(G,\beta)
;

​
[
Horizontal connection
]
:

The horizontal linear connection consists of a tangent natural transformation:

\displaystyle h\colon\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathsf{H}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathbf{q}\to\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(K,\theta)

satisfying the axioms of a horizontal linear connection. Notice that
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathsf{H}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathbf{q}\colon\mathsf{HLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is the strict tangent morphism which sends a horizontal linear connection
(\mathbf{q},h)
to the total space of the horizontal bundle
\mathsf{H}\mathbf{q}
of
\mathbf{q}
.

A morphism of horizontal linear connections in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]
from a horizontal linear connection
(\mathbf{q}\colon(K,\theta)\to(G,\beta),h)
to a horizontal linear connection
(\mathbf{q}^{\prime}\colon(K^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})\to(G^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}),h^{\prime})
consists of a linear morphism of linear connections
(\varphi,\psi)\colon\mathbf{q}\to\mathbf{q}^{\prime}
which commutes with the horizontal linear connections.

Finally, a linear connection consists of:

​
[
Differential bundle
]
:

A pointwise display differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon(K,\theta)\to(G,\beta)
;

​
[
Linear connection
]
:

The linear connection consists of a pair of tangent natural transformations:

\displaystyle k\colon\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(K,\theta)\to(K,\theta)

\displaystyle h\colon\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathsf{H}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathbf{q}\to\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(K,\theta)

such that
(\mathbf{q},k)
and
(\mathbf{q},h)
are a vertical and a horizontal linear connections, respectively;

satisfying the axioms of a linear connection.

A morphism of linear connections in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]
from a linear connection
(\mathbf{q}\colon(K,\theta)\to(G,\beta);k,h)
to a linear connection
(\mathbf{q}^{\prime}\colon(K^{\prime},\theta^{\prime})\to(G^{\prime},\beta^{\prime});k^{\prime},h^{\prime})
consists of a linear morphism of linear connections
(\varphi,\psi)\colon\mathbf{q}\to\mathbf{q}^{\prime}
which commutes with the vertical and the horizontal linear connections.

For each tangent category
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
, a vertical linear connection
(\mathbf{q},k)
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
induces a functor

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(\mathbf{q},k)}\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]\to\mathsf{VLC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]

which sends a lax tangent morphism
(H,\gamma)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
to the vertical linear connection

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(\mathbf{q},k)}(H,\gamma)\>\colon\!=(\Gamma_{\mathbf{q}}(H,\gamma),k_{(H,\gamma)})

in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
. Concretely, the base pointwise display differential bundle is induced by the functor
\Gamma_{\mathbf{q}}
of Lemma
7.8
and
k_{(H,\gamma)}
is the natural transformation:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(H,\gamma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(K,\theta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(K,\theta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}

Similarly, a horizontal linear connection
(\mathbf{q},h)
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
induces a functor

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(\mathbf{q},h)}\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]\to\mathsf{HLC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]

which sends a lax tangent morphism
(H,\gamma)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
to the horizontal linear connection

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(\mathbf{q},h)}(H,\gamma)\>\colon\!=(\Gamma_{\mathbf{q}}(H,\gamma),h_{(H,\gamma)})

in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
. Concretely, the base pointwise display differential bundle is induced by the functor
\Gamma_{\mathbf{q}}
of Lemma
7.8
and
h_{(H,\gamma)}
is the natural transformation:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(H,\gamma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathsf{H}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathbf{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(K,\theta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}

Furthermore, a linear connection
(\mathbf{q};k,h)
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
induces a functor

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(\mathbf{q};k,h)}\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]\to\mathsf{LC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]

which sends a lax tangent morphism
(H,\gamma)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
to the horizontal linear connection

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(\mathbf{q};k,h)}(H,\gamma)\>\colon\!=(\Gamma_{\mathbf{q}}(H,\gamma);k_{H,\gamma},h_{(H,\gamma)})

in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
. These constructions are functorial, as shown by the next lemma.

Lemma 8.9
.

Given a pointwise display differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon(K,\theta)\to(G,\beta)
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
, a vertical linear connection
k
on
\mathbf{q}
, a horizontal linear connection
h
on
\mathbf{q}
, and a linear connection
(k,h)
on
\mathbf{q}
induce lax tangent morphisms

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(\mathbf{q},k)}

\displaystyle\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]\to\mathsf{VLC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(\mathbf{q},h)}

\displaystyle\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]\to\mathsf{HLC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(\mathbf{q};k,h)}

\displaystyle\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]\to\mathsf{LC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]

for each tangentad
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
, which are natural in
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
and strong (strict) when both
(G,\beta)
and
(K,\theta)
are strong (strict).

Proof.

The proof of this lemma is fairly similar to that of Lemma
5.3
.
∎

By Lemma
8.9
, the vertical linear connection
\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{VLC}}}
on
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}}
, the horizontal linear connection
\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{HLC}}}
on
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{HLC}}
, and the linear connection
(\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{LC}}},\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{LC}}})
on
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{LC}}
, of Proposition
8.8
induce three strict tangent natural transformations

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}},K^{\mathsf{VLC}}}}

\displaystyle\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]\to\mathsf{VLC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{\mathbf{q}^{\mathsf{HLC}},H^{\mathsf{HLC}}}}

\displaystyle\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{HLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]\to\mathsf{HLC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{\mathbf{q}^{\mathsf{LC}},K^{\mathsf{LC}},H^{\mathsf{LC}}}}

\displaystyle\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]\to\mathsf{LC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

natural in
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
.

The goal is to prove that these three transformations are invertible. Consider another tangent category
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
together with a pointwise display differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon(K,\theta)\to(G,\beta)
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
equipped with a vertical connection
k
, with a horizontal connection
h
, and a linear connection
(k,h)
(each case to be taken separately). For every
A\in\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
, the tuples

\displaystyle(\mathbf{q}_{A}\colon KA\to GA,k_{A}\colon\mathrm{T}KA\to KA)

\displaystyle(\mathbf{q}_{A}\colon KA\to GA,h\colon\mathsf{H}{\mathbf{q}_{A}}\to\mathrm{T}KA)

\displaystyle(\mathbf{q}_{A}\colon KA\to GA;k_{A}\colon\mathrm{T}KA\to KA,h\colon\mathsf{H}{\mathbf{q}_{A}}\to\mathrm{T}KA)

are a vertical linear connection, a horizontal linear connection, and a linear connection in
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. Therefore, we can define the functors

\displaystyle\Lambda[\mathbf{q},k]

\displaystyle\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle\Lambda[\mathbf{q},h]

\displaystyle\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{HLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle\Lambda[\mathbf{q};k,h]

\displaystyle\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

which send an object
A
of
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
to the connections
(\mathbf{q}_{A},k_{A})
,
(\mathbf{q}_{A},h_{A})
, and
(\mathbf{q}_{A};k_{A},h_{A})
, respectively, and a morphism
f\colon A\to B
to
(Gf,Kf)
. By the naturality of
k
,
h
, and the structure morphisms of
\mathbf{q}
,
(Gf,Kf)
becomes a morphism of (vertical/horizontal) connections of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. Furthermore,
\Lambda[\mathbf{q},k]
,
\Lambda[\mathbf{q},h]
, and
\Lambda[\mathbf{q};k,h]
come with a distributive law induced by
\beta
and
\theta
.

Lemma 8.10
.

Given a pointwise display differential bundle structure
\mathbf{q}\colon(K,\theta)\to(G,\beta)
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
, equipped with a vertical linear connection
k
, a horizontal linear connection
h
, or a linear connection
(k,h)
, the functors
\Lambda[\mathbf{q},k]
,
\Lambda[\mathbf{q},h]
, and
\Lambda[\mathbf{q};k,h]
define lax tangent morphisms:

\displaystyle\Lambda[\mathbf{q},k]

\displaystyle\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle\Lambda[\mathbf{q},h]

\displaystyle\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{HLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle\Lambda[\mathbf{q};k,h]

\displaystyle\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

Proof.

In the previous discussion, we defined
\Lambda[\mathbf{q},k]
,
\Lambda[\mathbf{q},h]
, and
\Lambda[\mathbf{q};k,h]
as the lax tangent morphisms which pick out the connections
(\mathbf{q}_{A},k_{A})
,
(\mathbf{q}_{A},h_{A})
, and
(\mathbf{q}_{A};k_{A},h_{A})
for each
A\in\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
, respectively, and whose distributive law is induced by
\beta
and
\theta
. In particular, the naturality of the structure morphisms of
\mathbf{q}
and of the connections makes these functors well-defined, the compatibility of the structure morphisms,
\beta
and
\theta
, makes them into morphisms of connections, and the compatibility between
\beta
and
\theta
and the tangent structures make them into lax tangent morphisms.
∎

We can now prove the universal property of vertical, horizontal, and linear connections, which is the main result of this section.

Theorem 8.11
.

The vertical linear connection
\mathcal{\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}},K^{\mathsf{VLC}}}
, the horizontal linear connection
\mathcal{\mathbf{q}^{\mathsf{HLC}},H^{\mathsf{HLC}}}
, and the linear connection
\mathcal{\mathbf{q}^{\mathsf{LC}};K^{\mathsf{LC}},H^{\mathsf{LC}}}
of Proposition
8.8
are universal. Concretely, the induced strict tangent natural transformations

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}},K^{\mathsf{VLC}}}}

\displaystyle\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]\to\mathsf{VLC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{\mathbf{q}^{\mathsf{HLC}},H^{\mathsf{HLC}}}}

\displaystyle\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{HLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]\to\mathsf{HLC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{\mathbf{q}^{\mathsf{LC}},K^{\mathsf{LC}},H^{\mathsf{LC}}}}

\displaystyle\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]\to\mathsf{LC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

make the functors

\displaystyle\mathsf{TNGCAT}^{\mathsf{op}}\xrightarrow{[-\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]}\mathsf{TNGCAT}^{\mathsf{op}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{VLC}^{\mathsf{op}}}\mathsf{TNGCAT}^{\mathsf{op}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{TNGCAT}^{\mathsf{op}}\xrightarrow{[-\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]}\mathsf{TNGCAT}^{\mathsf{op}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{HLC}^{\mathsf{op}}}\mathsf{TNGCAT}^{\mathsf{op}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{TNGCAT}^{\mathsf{op}}\xrightarrow{[-\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]}\mathsf{TNGCAT}^{\mathsf{op}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{LC}^{\mathsf{op}}}\mathsf{TNGCAT}^{\mathsf{op}}

which send a tangent category
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
to the tangent categories
\mathsf{VLC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
,
\mathsf{HLC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
, and
\mathsf{LC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
, respectively, into corepresentable functors. In particular,
\Gamma_{\mathcal{\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}},K^{\mathsf{VLC}}}}
,
\Gamma_{\mathcal{\mathbf{q}^{\mathsf{HLC}},H^{\mathsf{HLC}}}}
, and
\Gamma_{\mathcal{\mathbf{q}^{\mathsf{LC}},K^{\mathsf{LC}},H^{\mathsf{LC}}}}
are invertible.

Proof.

The proof of this theorem is fairly similar to that of Theorems
5.5
and
6.7
.
∎

Theorem
8.11
establishes the correct universal property of vertical, horizontal, and linear connections. Thanks to this result, we can finally introduce the notion of vertical, horizontal, and linear connections in the formal context of tangentads. Let us start with vertical connections.

Definition 8.12
.

A tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
in a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}

admits the construction of vertical linear connections
if there exists a tangentad
\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
{\mathbf{K}}
together with a pointwise display tangent bundle
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}}\colon\mathsf{Tot}^{\mathsf{VLC}}\to\mathsf{Base}^{\mathsf{VLC}}
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
and a vertical linear connection
\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{VLC}}}
on
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}}
such that the induced tangent natural transformation
\Gamma_{\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}},\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{VLC}}}}
of Lemma
8.9
is invertible. The vertical linear connection
\mathcal{\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}},K^{\mathsf{VLC}}}\>\colon\!=(\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{VLC}},\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{VLC}}})
is called the
universal vertical linear connection
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is called the
tangentad of vertical linear connections
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Definition 8.13
.

A
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}

admits the construction of vertical linear connections
provided that every tangentad of
{\mathbf{K}}
admits the construction of vertical linear connections.

The next step is to introduce horizontal linear connections.

Definition 8.14
.

A tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
in a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}

admits the construction of horizontal linear connections
if there exists a tangentad
\mathsf{HLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
{\mathbf{K}}
together with a pointwise display tangent bundle
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{HLC}}\colon\mathsf{Tot}^{\mathsf{HLC}}\to\mathsf{Base}^{\mathsf{HLC}}
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathsf{HLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
and a horizontal linear connection
\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{HLC}}}
on
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{HLC}}
such that the induced tangent natural transformation
\Gamma_{\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{HLC}},\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{HLC}}}}
of Lemma
8.9
is invertible. The horizontal linear connection
\mathcal{\mathbf{q}^{\mathsf{HLC}},H^{\mathsf{HLC}}}\>\colon\!=(\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{HLC}},\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{HLC}}})
is called the
universal horizontal linear connection
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
\mathsf{HLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is called the
tangentad of horizontal linear connections
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Definition 8.15
.

A
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}

admits the construction of horizontal linear connections
provided that every tangentad of
{\mathbf{K}}
admits the construction of horizontal linear connections.

Finally, we can introduce linear connections.

Definition 8.16
.

A tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
in a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}

admits the construction of linear connections
if there exists a tangentad
\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
{\mathbf{K}}
together with a pointwise display tangent bundle
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{LC}}\colon\mathsf{Tot}^{\mathsf{LC}}\to\mathsf{Base}^{\mathsf{LC}}
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
and a linear connection
(\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{LC}}},\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{LC}}})
on
\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{LC}}
such that the induced tangent natural transformation
\Gamma_{\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{LC}};\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{LC}}},\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{LC}}}}
of Lemma
8.9
is invertible. The linear connection
\mathcal{\mathbf{q}^{\mathsf{LC}};K^{\mathsf{LC}},H^{\mathsf{LC}}}\>\colon\!=(\mathcal{Q}^{\mathsf{LC}};\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{LC}}},\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{LC}}})
is called the
universal linear connection
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is called the
tangentad of linear connections
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Definition 8.17
.

A
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}

admits the construction of linear connections
provided that every tangentad of
{\mathbf{K}}
admits the construction of linear connections.

We can now rephrase Theorem
8.11
as follows.

Corollary 8.18
.

The
2
-category
\mathsf{CAT}
of categories admits the constructions of vertical, horizontal, and linear connections and the tangentads of vertical, horizontal, and linear connections of a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
\mathsf{CAT}
are the tangent categories
\mathsf{VLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
,
\mathsf{HLC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, and
\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of vertical, horizontal, and linear connections of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, respectively.

To ensure that Definitions
8.12
,
8.14
, and
8.16
are well-posed, one requires the tangentads of vertical, horizontal, and linear connections of a given tangentad to be unique. The next proposition establishes that such constructions are defined uniquely up to a unique isomorphism.

Proposition 8.19
.

If a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
admits the construction of (vertical/horizontal) linear connections, the tangentad of (vertical/horizontal) linear connections of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is unique up to a unique isomorphism which extends to an isomorphism of the corresponding universal (vertical/horizontal) linear connections of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Proof.

The proof of this proposition is fairly similar to that of Proposition
5.9
.
∎

8.3
The universal property of affine connections

This section aims to characterize the correct universal property of the tangent categories
\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
,
\mathsf{HAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, and
\mathsf{AC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of the various flavours of affine connections on linear connections of a tangent category
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. For starters, notice that, for each of these three tangent categories, there is a strict tangent morphism which forgets the connection:

\displaystyle\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{VAC}}

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{HAC}}

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{HAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{AC}}

\displaystyle\colon\mathsf{AC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

Define the following natural transformations:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{VAC}}}_{(M,k)}&\colon\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{VAC}}(M,k)=\mathrm{T}^{2}M\xrightarrow{k}\mathrm{T}M=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{VAC}}(M,k)\\
\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{HAC}}}_{(M,h)}&\colon\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{2}\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{HAC}}(M,h)=\mathrm{T}_{2}M\xrightarrow{h}\mathrm{T}^{2}M=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{HAC}}(M,h)\\
\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{AC}}}_{(M;k,h)}&\colon\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{AC}}(M;k,h)=\mathrm{T}^{2}M\xrightarrow{k}\mathrm{T}M=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{AC}}(M;k,h)\\
\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{AC}}}_{(M;k,h)}&\colon\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{2}\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{AC}}(M;k,h)=\mathrm{T}_{2}M\xrightarrow{h}\mathrm{T}^{2}M=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{AC}}(M;k,h)\end{split}

(8.2)

Proposition 8.20
.

The natural transformations of Equation (
8.2
) define a vertical affine connection
\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{VAC}}}
on
\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{VAC}}
, a horizontal affine connection
\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{HAC}}}
on
\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{HAC}}
, and an affine connection
(\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{AC}}},\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{AC}}}
on
\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{AC}}
, respectively.

Proof.

We already mentioned that
\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{VAC}}
,
\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{HAC}}
, and
\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{AC}}
are pointwise display differential bundles since they are constructed from the universal pointwise display differential bundle. The naturality of
\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{VAC}}}
,
\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{HAC}}}
,
\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{AC}}}
, and
\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{AC}}}
is equivalent to the compatibilities between the morphisms of
\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
,
\mathsf{HAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, and
\mathsf{AC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
with the connections. Furthermore, these natural transformations are compatible with the tangent structures by definition of the tangent bundle functors in each respective tangent category. Finally, the axioms required for these transformations to define connections are a direct consequence of each
(M,k)\in\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
,
(M,h)\in\mathsf{HAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, and
(M;k,h)\in\mathsf{AC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
being vertical, horizontal, and affine connections in
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.
∎

To characterize the correct universal property enjoyed by the connections of Proposition
8.20
, let us first unwrap the definition of a (vertical/horizontal) affine connection in each Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]
, where
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
are tangent categories. Let us start with a vertical affine connection.

​
[
Base object
]
:

A lax tangent morphism
(G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
;

​
[
Vertical connection
]
:

The vertical affine connection consists of a tangent natural transformation:

\displaystyle k\colon\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}(G,\beta)\to\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(G,\beta)

satisfying the axioms of a vertical affine connection. A morphism of vertical affine connections in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]
from a vertical affine connection
(G,\beta;k)
to a vertical affine connection
(G^{\prime},\beta^{\prime};k^{\prime})
consists of a tangent natural transformation
\varphi\colon(G,\beta)\Rightarrow(G^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})
which commutes with the vertical affine connections.

Similarly, a horizontal affine connection consists of:

​
[
Base object
]
:

A lax tangent morphism
(G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
;

​
[
Horizontal connection
]
:

The horizontal affine connection consists of a tangent natural transformation:

\displaystyle h\colon\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{2}(G,\beta)\to\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(G,\beta)

satisfying the axioms of a horizontal affine connection.

A morphism of horizontal affine connections in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]
from a horizontal affine connection
(G,\beta;h)
to a horizontal affine connection
(G^{\prime},\beta^{\prime};h^{\prime})
consists of a tangent natural transformation
\varphi\colon(G,\beta)\Rightarrow(G^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})
which commutes with the horizontal affine connections.

Finally, an affine connection consists of:

​
[
Base object
]
:

A lax tangent morphism
(G,\beta)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
;

​
[
Affine connection
]
:

The affine connection consists of a pair of tangent natural transformations:

\displaystyle k\colon\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}(G,\beta)\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(G,\beta)

\displaystyle h\colon\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{2}(G,\beta)\to\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}(G,\beta)

such that
(G,\beta;k)
and
(G,\beta;h)
are a vertical and a horizontal affine connections, respectively;

satisfying the axioms of an affine connection.

A morphism of affine connections in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}]
from an affine connection
(G,\beta;k,h)
to an affine connection
(G^{\prime},\beta^{\prime};k^{\prime},h^{\prime})
consists of a tangent natural transformation
\varphi\colon(G,\beta)\Rightarrow(G^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})
which commutes with the vertical and the horizontal affine connections.

For each tangent category
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
, a vertical affine connection
(G,\beta;k)
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
induces a functor

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(G,\beta;k)}\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]\to\mathsf{VAC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]

which sends a lax tangent morphism
(H,\gamma)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
to the vertical affine connection

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(G,\beta;k)}(H,\gamma)\>\colon\!=((H,\gamma)\circ\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{VAC}},k_{(H,\gamma)})

in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
. Concretely,
k_{(H,\gamma)}
is the natural transformation:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(H,\gamma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G,\beta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(G,\beta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}

Similarly, a horizontal affine connection
(G,\beta;h)
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
induces a functor

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(G,\beta;h)}\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]\to\mathsf{HAC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]

which sends a lax tangent morphism
(H,\gamma)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
to the horizontal affine connection

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(G,\beta;h)}(H,\gamma)\>\colon\!=((H,\gamma)\circ\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{HAC}},h_{(H,\gamma)})

in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
. Concretely,
h_{(H,\gamma)}
is the natural transformation:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(H,\gamma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{2}(G,\beta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathrm{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(G,\beta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}

Furthermore,a affine connection
(\mathbf{q};k,h)
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
induces a functor

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(\mathbf{q};k,h)}\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]\to\mathsf{AC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]

which sends a lax tangent morphism
(H,\gamma)\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime})
to the horizontal affine connection

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(\mathbf{q};k,h)}(H,\gamma)\>\colon\!=((H,\gamma)\circ\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{AC}};k_{H,\gamma},h_{(H,\gamma)})

in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
. These constructions are functorial, as shown by the next lemma.

Lemma 8.21
.

Given an object
(G,\beta)
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
, a vertical affine connection
k
on
\mathbf{q}
, a horizontal affine connection
h
on
\mathbf{q}
, and an affine connection
(k,h)
on
\mathbf{q}
induce lax tangent morphisms

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(G,\beta;k)}

\displaystyle\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]\to\mathsf{VAC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(G,\beta;h)}

\displaystyle\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]\to\mathsf{HAC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(\mathbf{q};k,h)}

\displaystyle\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}]\to\mathsf{AC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X}^{\prime\prime\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime\prime}]

for each tangentad
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
, which are natural in
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
and strong (strict) when both
(G,\beta)
and
(G,\beta)
are strong (strict).

Proof.

The proof of this lemma is fairly similar to that of Lemma
5.3
.
∎

By Lemma
8.21
, the vertical affine connection
\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{VAC}}}
on
\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{VAC}}
, the horizontal affine connection
\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{HAC}}}
on
\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{HAC}}
, and the affine connection
(\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{AC}}},\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{AC}}})
on
\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{AC}}
, of Proposition
8.20
induce three strict tangent natural transformations

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{VAC}},K^{\mathsf{VAC}}}}

\displaystyle\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]\to\mathsf{VAC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{HAC}},H^{\mathsf{HAC}}}}

\displaystyle\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{HAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]\to\mathsf{HAC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{AC}},K^{\mathsf{AC}},H^{\mathsf{AC}}}}

\displaystyle\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{AC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]\to\mathsf{AC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

natural in
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
.

The goal is to prove that these three transformations are invertible. Consider another tangent category
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
together with an object
(G,\beta)
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
equipped with a vertical connection
k
, with a horizontal connection
h
, and an affine connection
(k,h)
(each case to be taken separately). For every
A\in\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
, the tuples

\displaystyle(GA,k_{A}\colon\mathrm{T}^{2}A\to\mathrm{T}GA)

\displaystyle(GA,h\colon\mathsf{H}{GA}\to\mathrm{T}^{2}A)

\displaystyle(GA;k_{A}\colon\mathrm{T}^{2}A\to\mathrm{T}GA,h\colon\mathsf{H}{GA}\to\mathrm{T}^{2}A)

are a vertical affine connection, a horizontal affine connection, and an affine connection in
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. Therefore, we can define the functors

\displaystyle\Lambda[G,\beta;k]

\displaystyle\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle\Lambda[G,\beta;h]

\displaystyle\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{HAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle\Lambda[\mathbf{q};k,h]

\displaystyle\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{AC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

which send an object
A
of
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
to the connections
(GA,k_{A})
,
(GA,h_{A})
, and
(GA;k_{A},h_{A})
, respectively, and a morphism
f\colon A\to B
to
Gf
. By the naturality of
k
,
h
, and the structure morphisms of
\mathbf{q}
,
Gf
becomes a morphism of (vertical/horizontal) connections of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
. Furthermore,
\Lambda[G,\beta;k]
,
\Lambda[G,\beta;h]
, and
\Lambda[\mathbf{q};k,h]
come with a distributive law induced by
\beta
and
\theta
.

Lemma 8.22
.

Given an object
(G,\beta)
in the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
, equipped with a vertical affine connection
k
, a horizontal affine connection
h
, or an affine connection
(k,h)
, the functors
\Lambda[G,\beta;k]
,
\Lambda[G,\beta;h]
, and
\Lambda[G,\beta;k,h]
define lax tangent morphisms:

\displaystyle\Lambda[G,\beta;k]

\displaystyle\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle\Lambda[G,\beta;h]

\displaystyle\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{HAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

\displaystyle\Lambda[G,\beta;k,h]

\displaystyle\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{AC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

Proof.

In the previous discussion, we defined
\Lambda[G,\beta;k]
,
\Lambda[G,\beta;h]
, and
\Lambda[G,\beta;k,h]
as the lax tangent morphisms which pick out the connections
(GA,k_{A})
,
(GA,h_{A})
, and
(GA;k_{A},h_{A})
for each
A\in\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
, respectively, and whose distributive law is induced by
\beta
. In particular, the naturality of the structure morphisms of
\mathbf{q}
and of the connections makes these functors well-defined, the compatibility of the structure morphisms,
\beta
, makes them into morphisms of connections, and the compatibility between
\beta
and the tangent structures makes them into lax tangent morphisms.
∎

We can now prove the universal property of vertical, horizontal, and affine connections, which is the main result of this section.

Theorem 8.23
.

The vertical affine connection
\mathcal{\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{VAC}},K^{\mathsf{VAC}}}
, the horizontal affine connection
\mathcal{\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{HAC}},H^{\mathsf{HAC}}}
, and the affine connection
\mathcal{\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{AC}};K^{\mathsf{AC}},H^{\mathsf{AC}}}
of Proposition
8.20
are universal. Concretely, the induced strict tangent natural transformations

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{VAC}},K^{\mathsf{VAC}}}}

\displaystyle\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]\to\mathsf{VAC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{HAC}},H^{\mathsf{HAC}}}}

\displaystyle\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{HAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]\to\mathsf{HAC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathcal{\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{AC}},K^{\mathsf{AC}},H^{\mathsf{AC}}}}

\displaystyle\colon[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathsf{AC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})]\to\mathsf{AC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]

make the functors

\displaystyle\mathsf{TNGCAT}^{\mathsf{op}}\xrightarrow{[-\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]}\mathsf{TNGCAT}^{\mathsf{op}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{VAC}^{\mathsf{op}}}\mathsf{TNGCAT}^{\mathsf{op}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{TNGCAT}^{\mathsf{op}}\xrightarrow{[-\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]}\mathsf{TNGCAT}^{\mathsf{op}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{HAC}^{\mathsf{op}}}\mathsf{TNGCAT}^{\mathsf{op}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{TNGCAT}^{\mathsf{op}}\xrightarrow{[-\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]}\mathsf{TNGCAT}^{\mathsf{op}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{AC}^{\mathsf{op}}}\mathsf{TNGCAT}^{\mathsf{op}}

which send a tangent category
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
to the tangent categories
\mathsf{VAC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
,
\mathsf{HAC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
, and
\mathsf{AC}[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
, respectively, into corepresentable functors. In particular,
\Gamma_{\mathcal{\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{VAC}},K^{\mathsf{VAC}}}}
,
\Gamma_{\mathcal{\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{HAC}},H^{\mathsf{HAC}}}}
, and
\Gamma_{\mathcal{\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{AC}},K^{\mathsf{AC}},H^{\mathsf{AC}}}}
are invertible.

Proof.

The proof of this theorem is fairly similar to that of Theorems
5.5
and
6.7
.
∎

Theorem
8.23
establishes the correct universal property of vertical, horizontal, and affine connections. Thanks to this result, we can finally introduce the notion of vertical, horizontal, and affine connections in the formal context of tangentads. Let us start with vertical connections.

Definition 8.24
.

A tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
in a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}

admits the construction of vertical affine connections
if there exists a tangentad
\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
{\mathbf{K}}
together with a tangent morphism
\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{VAC}}\colon\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and a vertical affine connection
\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{VAC}}}
on
\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{VAC}}
such that the induced tangent natural transformation
\Gamma_{\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{VAC}},\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{VAC}}}}
of Lemma
8.21
is invertible. The vertical affine connection
\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{VAC}}}\>\colon\!=(\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{VAC}},\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{VAC}}})
is called the
universal vertical affine connection
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is called the
tangentad of vertical affine connections
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Definition 8.25
.

A
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}

admits the construction of vertical affine connections
provided that every tangentad of
{\mathbf{K}}
admits the construction of vertical affine connections.

The next step is to introduce horizontal affine connections.

Definition 8.26
.

A tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
in a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}

admits the construction of horizontal affine connections
if there exists a tangentad
\mathsf{HAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
{\mathbf{K}}
together with a tangent morphism
\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{HAC}}\colon\mathsf{HAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and a horizontal affine connection
\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{HAC}}}
on
\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{HAC}}
such that the induced tangent natural transformation
\Gamma_{\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{HAC}},\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{HAC}}}}
of Lemma
8.21
is invertible. The horizontal affine connection
\mathcal{\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{HAC}},H^{\mathsf{HAC}}}\>\colon\!=(\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{HAC}},\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{HAC}}})
is called the
universal horizontal affine connection
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
\mathsf{HAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is called the
tangentad of horizontal affine connections
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Definition 8.27
.

A
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}

admits the construction of horizontal affine connections
provided that every tangentad of
{\mathbf{K}}
admits the construction of horizontal affine connections.

Finally, we can introduce affine connections.

Definition 8.28
.

A tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
in a
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}

admits the construction of affine connections
if there exists a tangentad
\mathsf{AC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
{\mathbf{K}}
together with a tangent morphism
\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{AC}}\colon\mathsf{AC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})\to(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and an affine connection
(\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{AC}}},\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{AC}}})
on
\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{AC}}
such that the induced tangent natural transformation
\Gamma_{\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{AC}};\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{AC}}},\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{AC}}}}
of Lemma
8.21
is invertible. The affine connection
\mathcal{\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{AC}};K^{\mathsf{AC}},H^{\mathsf{AC}}}\>\colon\!=(\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{AC}};\mathcal{K^{\mathsf{AC}}},\mathcal{H^{\mathsf{AC}}})
is called the
universal affine connection
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
\mathsf{AC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is called the
tangentad of affine connections
of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Definition 8.29
.

A
2
-category
{\mathbf{K}}

admits the construction of affine connections
provided that every tangentad of
{\mathbf{K}}
admits the construction of affine connections.

We can now rephrase Theorem
8.23
as follows.

Corollary 8.30
.

The
2
-category
\mathsf{CAT}
of categories admits the constructions of vertical, horizontal, and affine connections and the tangentads of vertical, horizontal, and affine connections of a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of
\mathsf{CAT}
are the tangent categories
\mathsf{VAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
,
\mathsf{HAC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, and
\mathsf{AC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
of vertical, horizontal, and affine connections of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
, respectively.

To ensure that Definitions
8.24
,
8.26
, and
8.28
are well-posed, one requires the tangentads of vertical, horizontal, and affine connections of a given tangentad to be unique. The next proposition establishes that such constructions are defined uniquely up to a unique isomorphism.

Proposition 8.31
.

If a tangentad
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
admits the construction of (vertical/horizontal) affine connections, the tangentad of (vertical/horizontal) affine connections of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
is unique up to a unique isomorphism which extends to an isomorphism of the corresponding universal (vertical/horizontal) affine connections of
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
.

Proof.

The proof of this proposition is fairly similar to that of Proposition
5.9
.
∎

8.4
The universal property of linear sections

One of the goals of the formal theory of connections is to extend to the formal context of tangentads notions like the covariant derivative, the curvature, and the torsion of a connection. These are operations which involve vector fields and sections of the linear connection on which the connection is defined. Thus, in order to introduce these concepts, we first need to capture the sections of differential bundles with a connection.

In Section
5
, we introduced the tangentad of vector fields of a given tangentad via a universal property. We adopt a similar strategy and introduce the tangentad of
linear sections
of differential bundles with a connection.

For starters, consider a pointwise display differential bundle
\mathbf{q}\colon(K,\theta)\to(G,\beta)
of the Hom-tangent category
[\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime}\>|\>\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T}]
; assume also that the underlying tangent morphisms
(G,\beta)
and
(K,\theta)
are strong. Via the universal property of linear connections, when
(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})
and
(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})
are tangent categories, the data of a pointwise display differential bundle
\mathbf{q}
is equivalent to a strong tangent morphism

\displaystyle\Lambda[\mathbf{q}]\colon(\mathbb{X}^{\prime},\mathbb{T}^{\prime})\to\mathsf{LC}(\mathbb{X},\mathbb{T})

which sends each
A\in\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
to the display linear connection
\mathbf{q}_{A}\colon KA\to GA
. Define the tangent category
\mathsf{LS}(\mathbf{q})
of linear sections of
\mathbf{q}
as follows:

​
[
Objects
]
:

An object of
\mathsf{LS}(\mathbf{q})
is a pair
(A,s)
formed by an object
A
of
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
together with a morphism
s\colon GA\to KA
, which is a section of
q_{A}\colon KA\to GA
, namely, the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces KA}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GA}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GA}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

​
[
Morphisms
]
:

A morphism of
\mathsf{LS}(\mathbf{q})
from a linear section
(A,s)
to another linear section
(B,t)
consists of a morphism
f\colon A\to B
of
\mathbb{X}^{\prime}
which commutes with the two linear sections, namely, such that the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces KA}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces KB}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GA}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GB}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Kf}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Gf}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}

[
Tangent bundle functor
]
:

The tangent bundle functor
\mathrm{T}^{\mathsf{LS}}\colon\mathsf{LS}(\mathbf{q})\to\mathsf{LS}(\mathbf{q})
sends a pair
(A,s)
to
(\mathrm{T}^{\prime}A,s_{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}})
, where:

\displaystyle s_{\mathrm{T}^{\prime}}\colon G\mathrm{T}^{\prime}A\xrightarrow{\beta}\mathrm{T}GA\xrightarrow{\mathrm{T}s}\mathrm{T}KA\xrightarrow{\theta^{-1}}K\mathrm{T}^{\prime}A

Moreover, it sends a morphism
f\colon(A,s)\to(B,t)
to
\mathrm{T}f
;

Structural natural transformations
